                   [2sum alpha_i delta_i+||x-sum alpha_i y_i||^2]. (6.1)

Define the extended-valued CONVEX ROOF

    Psi(y)=min{sum_i alpha_i delta_i:
                alpha_i>=0, sum alpha_i=1, sum alpha_i*y_i=y},
                                                               (6.2)

and set Psi=+infinity outside conv{y_i:i in I}. Each nonempty feasible
fiber is compact. The minimum is attained; taking convex combinations
of feasible probabilities proves convexity. Its epigraph is the closed
finite-dimensional polyhedron generated by the finite height points
and the upward vertical ray; alternatively compactness of probabilities
proves lower semicontinuity directly for bounded heights. It is proper.
Jensen's inequality for phi gives phi<=Psi. At every y_i, the supporting
piece i bounds Psi from below by delta_i, while the point mass bounds it
from above by delta_i. Thus Psi(y_i)=delta_i, and the same affine piece
shows g_i in partial Psi(y_i). Also Psi(0)=0=min Psi.

Equation (6.1) is exactly

    F(x)=min_y[Psi(y)+||x-y||^2/2].                   (6.3)

This identifies the upper model without importing a conjugacy theorem.
The finite affine maximum phi and the convex roof Psi interpolate the
same proximal data but are different convex functions. In particular

    f_phi(x)<=F(x)                                   (6.4)

has the LOWER-bound orientation for a proposed full terminal upper.
The fact that f_phi is legal only shows it is one possible extension.

For each t>=0 define three sets, including star as the point0:

    C_low(t)={y:phi(y)<=t},
    C_roof(t)={sum alpha_i*y_i:alpha a probability,
                             sum alpha_i*delta_i<=t},
    C_ray(t)=union_(0<=i<=R){theta*y_i:0<=theta<=u_i(t)},
    u_i(t)=min(1,t/delta_i) if delta_i>0, and1 otherwise.

Then C_roof(t) is the sublevel set of Psi, and

    C_ray(t) subset C_roof(t) subset C_low(t).                       (6.5)

The first inclusion uses only the probability on star and i; the second
uses phi<=Psi. All three sets are closed and nonempty; C_roof(t) and C_ray(t) are
compact. A Euclidean nearest point to each exists. For C_low(t) this follows
by restricting a distance-minimizing sequence to a bounded closed ball.

Define the actual one-anchor selector

    M_1=min_(i,0<=theta<=1)
                       [2theta*delta_i+||x_n-theta*y_i||^2].

The three threshold optimizations have exact values

    min_(t>=0)[2t+dist(x_n,C_low(t))^2]=2f_phi(x_n),
    min_(t>=0)[2t+dist(x_n,C_roof(t))^2]=2F(x_n),
    min_(t>=0)[2t+dist(x_n,C_ray(t))^2]=M_1.               (6.6)

For the first two identities, any level gives a trial point with height
at most t; conversely take the height of an attained envelope minimizer.
For C_ray(t), every member is theta*y_i with theta*delta_i<=t, so its
objective is at least M_1. Conversely choose a minimizing pair i,theta
and t=theta*delta_i. This proves equality and attainment.

The one-anchor threshold optimization is explicitly finite. Set

    d_i=||y_i||^2, b_i=<x_n,y_i>-delta_i,
    theta_i=clip_[0,1](b_i/d_i) if d_i>0, and0 otherwise,
    t_i=theta_i*delta_i.

Then the minimum in the third line of (6.6) is attained at one of these
n listed thresholds, and equals min_i[2t_i+||x_n-theta_i*y_i||^2].
If d_i=0, delta_i>=0 makes theta_i=0 minimizing. For d_i>0 this follows
by completing the scalar quadratic square. Thus no unevaluated threshold
or new feasibility oracle is offered as a selector.

Here is a VALID paid transfer from the lower level set. For any t>=0,
let z_t be its nearest point to x_n and put

    d_t=||x_n-z_t||, e_t=dist(z_t,C_ray(t)).

A nearest point in C_ray(t) and the triangle inequality give

    M_1 <= 2t+(d_t+e_t)^2.                           (6.7)

The missing alignment payment is 2d_t*e_t+e_t^2. Omitting it reverses
the role of the inclusions (6.5). Equivalently, on any original radial
segment, convexity gives the nonnegative chord-height error

    eta_i(theta)=theta*delta_i-phi(theta*y_i)>=0.

The segment cost is EXACTLY
2phi(theta*y_i)+||x_n-theta*y_i||^2+2eta_i(theta).
An affine-support or level-set replacement must pay that error as well
as any displacement to its chosen point. The next section evaluates
both effects explicitly rather than assuming them negligible.

No bound on e_t or eta_i at the required all-prefix scale has been
proved. Equation (6.7), or the corresponding finite selector threshold
bound, is still a theorem-level interface, not a solution. The convex
roof description does not authorize a two-anchor selector experiment.

## 7. Legal same-word discriminator and exact optimized payments

This is a legal witness ONLY against zero-error height-envelope transfer.
It is not a new attempt to prove the rate on a quadratic family.
For each original m>=1536 and its complete word H_m, put

    kappa=1/[2(1+S)]=1/(D+1),
    f_quad(x)=kappa*x^2/2, x_0=1.

This is one static scalar convex 1-smooth function with unique minimum0
and true initial radius1. Every physical factor satisfies
0<kappa*h_i<1/2. Thus the original trajectory is exactly the symbolic
identity

    x_i=product_(t<i)(1-kappa*h_t), g_i=kappa*x_i,

and 1=x_0>x_1>...>x_R>x_n>0. No recurrence was numerically replayed.
All original ordered pairs, including star and terminal, have

    Q_ij=-kappa*(1-kappa)*(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0.           (7.1)

Write X=x_R, b=1-A*kappa, Y=(1-kappa)*X. Then x_n=bX and

    y_i=(1-kappa)*x_i,
    delta_i=kappa*(1-kappa)*x_i^2/2,
    delta_R=kappa*X*Y/2.

Since n>=3 and each omitted step exceeds1, S>A+2. Consequently
kappa*(2A+1)<1, b>1/2, and all positive interval assertions below
are strict. This uses the same enclosing parameters, not a small-horizon
substitute or a modified last step.

### 7.1 The lower height extension and its true normalization

Its affine pieces are

    ell_i(y)=kappa*x_i*y-kappa*(1-kappa)*x_i^2/2.

For y<=Y and every i<R,

    ell_i(y)-ell_R(y)
       =kappa*(x_i-X)*[y-(1-kappa)*(x_i+X)/2]<=0.

It follows that, on y<=Y,

    phi(y)=max{0,kappa*X*(y-Y/2)}.                   (7.2)

All slopes are positive, so the COMPLETE zero set is (-infinity,Y/2].
The true closest initial radius of f_phi is therefore 1-Y/2, strictly
between0 and1. The selected minimizer0 is valid but not closest.

Put

    z_phi=x_n-kappa*X=[1-(A+1)*kappa]*X.

The inequality kappa*(2A+1)<1 gives Y/2<z_phi<Y. On that interval
phi has slope kappa*X. The subgradient optimality condition therefore
proves z_phi=prox_phi(x_n), globally, and

    2f_phi(x_n)=kappa*X^2*(1-2A*kappa).              (7.3)

In contrast, the actual original quadratic has

    2f_quad(x_n)=kappa*b^2*X^2,
    2f_quad(x_n)-2f_phi(x_n)=A^2*kappa^3*X^2>0.      (7.4)

Thus even the original static legal function has terminal value larger
than that of the supplied affine-height envelope, with the SAME valid
reference minimum0. The changed true closest radius is stated separately;
no normalized comparison between two different closest minima is implied.

### 7.2 The maximal extension and exact one-anchor optimum

For accepted1252's defining H take v_*=kappa*X/2. Each original piece is

    y_i*v_*-delta_i
       =[kappa*(1-kappa)/2]*x_i*(X-x_i)<=0,

strictly negative for i<R, zero for i=R; star is zero too. Define

    theta_*=(b-kappa/2)/(1-kappa).

It lies strictly between0 and1, and x_n=v_*+theta_*Y. The star/R
probability bounds 2F above by its segment value, while evaluation at
v_* bounds it below by the same value. Therefore

    M_1=2F(x_n)=kappa*X^2*(b-kappa/4).               (7.5)

The identity for M_1 follows because F bounds every segment below and
the actual R segment attains it. Strictness of the other affine pieces
shows the finite anchor is uniquely R at equality. This does not assert
that R is optimal for arbitrary other prefixes.

All delta_i are strictly positive. Hence, in a neighborhood of v=0,
H(v)=v^2/2 because every finite affine piece remains negative there.
For any x!=0, choosing a sufficiently small v of the same sign gives
x*v-H(v)>0. Thus F(x)>0 for x!=0 and F(0)=0. Its minimum is uniquely0,
so F also has true initial radius1. Accepted1252 guarantees that it is
one static convex 1-smooth extension of the entire prefix. It uses the
same original gradient sequence through the last physical update;
its terminal gradient is its own gradient at x_n, as required.

Subtracting (7.3) gives the exact strict terminal discrepancy

    2F(x_n)-2f_phi(x_n)=kappa^2*X^2*(A-1/4)>0.       (7.6)

Also 2F-2f_quad=kappa^2*X^2*(A*b-1/4)>0, since A>1 and b>1/2.
No actual function was replaced during its own trajectory: these are
three different fixed global functions sharing the queried prefix.

### 7.3 Level thresholds are optimized, not left implicit

For 0<=t<=delta_R, the height sets have the exact descriptions

    C_low(t)=(-infinity,Y/2+t/(kappa*X)],
    C_roof(t)=C_ray(t)=[0,2t/(kappa*X)].                        (7.7)

For C_low(t), solve all affine inequalities to get right endpoint
min_i[(1-kappa)*x_i/2+t/(kappa*x_i)]. The R term is smallest on this
range: subtracting it from the i term gives
(x_i-X)*[(1-kappa)/2-t/(kappa*x_i*X)]>=0.
For C_roof(t), every anchor has delta_i/y_i=kappa*x_i/2>=kappa*X/2;
therefore its convex combination obeys y<=2t/(kappa*X).
The entire interval is attained by star/R probabilities. The same
argument bounds every radial segment, and the R segment attains the
interval, proving the C_ray(t) identity.

The globally optimal lower-envelope threshold is

    t_phi=phi(z_phi)
         =kappa*X^2*[1/2-(A+1/2)*kappa],
    0<t_phi<delta_R.                                (7.8)

Optimality follows from the exact proximal minimizer, or by completing
the scalar square in 2t+dist(x_n,C_low(t))^2 on (7.7). This is a closed
formula, with no level-set feasibility problem left to solve.

The globally optimal original one-anchor threshold is

    t_*=theta_*delta_R
        =(kappa*X^2/2)*[1-(A+1/2)*kappa],
    0<t_*<delta_R.                                  (7.9)

It follows from (7.5), or the minimum of
2t+(x_n-2t/(kappa*X))^2 in the relevant interval. The fact that the
minima are global, including t>delta_R, already follows from the
proximal optimality in Section7.1 and exact equality in Section7.2.

At the lower-envelope optimal threshold, its closest point is z_phi,
but the right endpoint of the permitted one-anchor level set is

    2t_phi/(kappa*X)=[1-(2A+1)*kappa]*X.

Hence the alignment payment in (6.7) has explicit positive size

    e_(t_phi)=A*kappa*X, d_(t_phi)=kappa*X,
    2d_(t_phi)*e_(t_phi)+e_(t_phi)^2
                            =(A^2+2A)*kappa^2*X^2.  (7.10)

Ignoring this gap is not justified by the common proximal prefix.
Paying it at t_phi is a valid but suboptimal one-anchor bound; optimizing
the CORRECT level sets gives the different threshold t_*.

The chord-height calculation at the correct anchor point is also exact.
Put z_*=theta_*Y=(b-kappa/2)*X. Both z_* and z_phi lie in (Y/2,Y), and
z_*-z_phi=kappa*X/2. Equations (7.2) and (7.9) give

    eta_R(theta_*)=(kappa^2*X^2/2)*(A-1/2)>0.

The increase of 2phi(z)+(x_n-z)^2 from z_phi to z_* is
||z_*-z_phi||^2=kappa^2*X^2/4. Adding twice the chord error gives

    kappa^2*X^2/4+2eta_R(theta_*)
                       =kappa^2*X^2*(A-1/4),

exactly the discrepancy (7.6). Thus both geometry and height errors are
paid explicitly. This is a complete same-word discriminator against the
zero-error replacement, not an all-function upper or a failed selector.
Its strict gap is not claimed to exceed the17/(16D) budget.

## 8. What remains and what this invocation excludes

The exact one-anchor target is still

    M_1<=17||x_0||^2/(16D)

for every original legal H_m prefix in every finite dimension. Its
connection to the full target remains 2f_n<=2F(x_n)<=M_1. Nothing here
reverses that chain. Accepted931's benchmark separation still awaits a
FULL W upper, not a check on selected quadratic or height families.

Within Mechanism1, the literal last-leaf collector is conclusively
excluded as a full PSD certificate. Adding nonlocal rows is necessary
for leaving E80 but is not sufficient; equations (4.5)--(4.6) exhibit
real mixed-source and short-leaf payments that its coefficients miss.
The signed identity itself survives. No claim excludes all transports,
all terminal probability choices, or all geometry-dependent selectors.

Within Mechanism2, phi is a valid convex height function with the right
query proxes and a checked minimum. Its envelope gives a lower extension,
so its own level-set rate cannot simply be transferred upward to F or
M_1. A future positive-height proof must either work with the correct
upper height roof and still control the original single-anchor segments,
or uniformly pay errors such as those in (6.7). The exact finite threshold
formula does not supply such a payment bound. No third route was opened.

The prior zero-height and small-height results were recalled as awareness,
not reused as verified lemmas or re-screened. The root-sign, Huber and
2D Huber/quadratic families were not repeated as rate tests. Section7's
positive monotone quadratic serves the distinct role of testing the
proposed height-envelope replacement, and all needed data are rederived
within this single joint draft.

## 9. Retrieval, honesty, and resource record

Read TASK.md, the applicable latest master guidance, own local-memory
continuation records, the original PROBLEM.md in bounded text passages,
and the applicable parts of accepted1252 plus the full accepted931 proof.
Used query-memory, direct-proving, and construct-counterexamples
procedures. Shared recall covered the E80 obstruction and prior adaptive
anchor report. Broad initial combined retrievals were truncated; the
needed report definitions and accepted facts were then read separately
in bounded passages. No truncated evidence is a correctness premise.

One Matlas theorem query on proximal/sublevel geometry returned lemmas
about robust regression, online proximal optimization and penalized
stochastic methods. None is used. One web background query identified
Parikh--Boyd, Proximal Algorithms,
https://stanford.edu/~boyd/papers/pdf/prox_algs.pdf.
It is terminology/background discovery only. No theorem from that source
is imported; no claim of downloading or reading its complete proof is
made. Every needed convex-height argument above is printed in full and
is part of this JOINT UNVERIFIED draft, relying externally only on the
identified accepted project facts. There was no external fact submission.

Resources: mathematical programs0; numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/
recurrence replays0; solvers0; optimizers0; benchmarkDP0; formal calls/
fact_submit0; subagents0; installations0; consults0. Symbolic products
and inequalities are mathematical text, not executed computations.
Administrative text reads/writes/hashes and shared-memory operations only,
plus the stated public mathematical-text retrieval. No runtime counter,
TASK, service, other worker, or truth-store file was changed.

Return is at TASK's explicit cap1, not a declaration that the project is
solved or should stop. No automatic renewal, publication outward,
finalization, revocation, shutdown, or partial Goal completion is done.

# One integrated full-certificate path and its exact subthreshold boundary

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is the ONE analytical
round assigned after completed41. No fact_submit is authorized or performed.
The original exact-profile Goal remains OPEN. This path supplies no improving
seed and is not claimed as progress on the original target.

Accepted inputs: fact04219554e41d9750, proof Sections10--12 (the original
primitive identity, composition, and physical substitution machinery);
fact1252b8823bb5a290 (the original smooth-convex interpolation convention);
fact4c92d3fc4eb6153e (the five/six-step thresholds and correct physical rays).
Every other ingredient below is rederived in this joint draft. No earlier
unverified finding is used as a mathematical premise.

The outcome is a concrete feasible path on a closed parameter interval, with
all30 ordered multipliers specified and a genuinely nonzero full remainder
of rank5 in its interior. At its endpoint the remainder vanishes. An actual
one-dimensional quadratic excludes every continuation point beyond that
endpoint in the entire original certificate cone. The endpoint is an already
available basic primitive construction below the required threshold.

## 1. Original coordinates and the explicit physical path

Normalize smoothness to1. For a length-r word h, use all original formal
gradients g_0,...,g_r, values f_0,...,f_r, and positions

  x_i=x_0-sum_(j<i) h_j g_j.

Set s=sum h_i, A=1+s, and

  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
  V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

The accepted convention is Q_ij<=0 on every actual trajectory. A full
primitive certificate consists of an array Lambda with zero diagonal and
every ordered off-diagonal entry nonnegative. With e=ones(r+1), define

  T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
  L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
  omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
  D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r,-s(s+2)).

The requirements are L_flow^T e=omega and

  M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0.           (1)

Fact04219554e41d9750 gives the full original-space identity

  V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
               =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.             (2)

All local symbols introduced in this report have the implicit suffix d42.
The benchmark symbols C_n,B_n,Omega,G and U_n^ZJ retain their accepted
meanings; C(t) below is a child capacity, not the benchmark sequence.

Define exact algebraic constants

  a=sqrt(2), lambda=1+a, rho=a-1=1/lambda,
  b=(-a+sqrt(10+8a))/2,
  p=a+b.

Thus b is the unique positive root of

  b^2+a b=2(1+a)=2lambda.                               (3)

For the ONE chosen parameter interval 1/2<=t<=1 put

  X=t a, Y=t b, C=1+X+Y=1+t p, delta=1-t,
  d=rho C-delta,
  h(t)=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y).                                  (4)

The steps, every coefficient, and the remainder will vary together.
The actual capacity is

  A(t)=2C+d=lambda C-delta
      =a+t(lambda p+1).                                (5)

In particular A(t) is strictly increasing. The start is the explicit word
h(1/2), not a conjecturally feasible seed. Its full array and PSD proof are
given below, jointly with those for every other parameter value.

The elementary bounds used repeatedly are

  7/5<a<3/2, p>3, C>5/2, d>1/2, 0<d<C.                (6)

For p>3, the increasing polynomial F(u)=u^2+a u-2(1+a) satisfies
F(3-a)=7-5a<0, so its positive root b exceeds3-a. Therefore C>5/2.
Also rho>2/5, delta<=1/2, and hence d>1/2. Finally
C-d=(2-a)C+delta>C/2. These arguments establish every denominator sign
in the construction without numerical evaluation.

## 2. The two-step child: complete array and full remainder

For arbitrary positive X,Y, set S=X+Y and C=1+S. Define

  u=2S(1+X)/Y, v=2SC/Y.

The full three-gradient child array is

              0             1             2
  row0        0             2             0
  row1        2(1+X)        0             u
  row2        0             v             0.          (7)

Its row sum minus column sum is (-2X,-2Y,2S), because v-u=2S.
Substitution into(1) gives, on the entire three-gradient space,

  M_child=w0(g_0-g_1)^2+w1(g_1-g_2)^2,                 (8)
  w0=2-X^2,
  w1=(S/Y)[2-Y^2+(2-Y)X].

Here and below an expression in vectors denotes its quadratic form, with
squared norms and inner products replacing scalar products. To audit(8),
the matrix entries are M_00=w0, M_01=-w0, M_02=0,
M_12=-w1, M_22=w1, and M_11=w0+w1. For example,

  M_12=[v(Y-1)-u]/2,
  M_22=-S(S+2)+v.

These equal -w1 and w1 respectively by the definitions of u,v. Thus this
is the full original matrix, not a principal-submatrix check.

For the actual children X=t a,Y=t b, equation(3) gives

  u=2(p/b)(1+t a), v=2(p/b)C,
  w0=2(1-t)(1+t),
  w1=2(p/b)(1-t)(1+lambda t).                           (9)

Consequently both weights are strictly positive for 1/2<=t<1 and both
vanish at t=1. All four displayed child multipliers remain positive.
This proves the actual child certificates without importing a draft result.

## 3. The off-root bridge identity and its actual correction

This section derives the identity needed for(4). Take two physical words
alpha,beta, each of capacity C, concatenate (alpha,1+d,beta), and suppose
0<=d<C. Let P denote the bridge query, Z the terminal query, and j range
over the nonterminal queries in beta. Write beta_j for the corresponding
physical steps. Define

  c=(C+d)/(C-d), k=1+2c=(3C+d)/(C-d),
  Delta=d(2C+d)-C^2,
  E=sum_j beta_j Q_(j,P)+Q_(Z,P)+d Q_(P,Z)
                                      +sum_j beta_j Q_(j,Z).

Direct coefficient matching yields

  V_(alpha,1+d,beta)
    =V_alpha+k V_beta-2c E
                     +c Delta(||g_Z||^2-||g_P||^2).     (10)

Here is the complete coefficient audit. The value part of E is

  2sum_j beta_j f_j+(d-C)f_P+(2-C-d)f_Z.

Its gradient part, after the physical GD substitution, is

  sum_j beta_j(1-beta_j)||g_j||^2
   +[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_P||^2+(C+d)||g_Z||^2/2.           (11)

For (11), internal mixed products cancel between the two sums over j.
The bridge-to-tail products cancel against d Q_(P,Z); terminal-to-tail
products cancel against Q_(Z,P); and the bridge-terminal product also
cancels. Thus no mixed entry is omitted.

Subtracting V_alpha+k V_beta from the parent gives the value part

  2(C+d)(f_P-f_Z)-4c sum_j beta_j(f_j-f_Z),

which is the value part of -2c E because c(C-d)=C+d. The bridge-gradient
coefficient before subtracting -2c E is C^2-C-d-d^2; the difference is
C^2-k d^2=-c Delta. The tail internal coefficients are
-2c beta_j(1-beta_j). The terminal-gradient coefficient is
-(2C+d-1)(2C+d)+k(C-1)C; subtracting -c(C+d) leaves c Delta.
The identity c Delta=k d^2-C^2 verifies the two endpoint comparisons.
These checks prove(10) as a polynomial identity in all original gradients
and values.

Away from Delta=0, the final diagonal difference in(10) alone is not a
PSD correction. Define instead

  tau=-c Delta/(C+d)=-Delta/(C-d), eta=2tau.             (12)

Add eta to BOTH actual multipliers on (P,Z) and (Z,P). This is a
zero-divergence coefficient change, performed jointly with the changing
physical word. Put

  zeta=g_P-g_Z,
  J=sum_j beta_j(g_j-g_Z).

The exact resulting remainder is

  M_alpha+k M_beta
     +tau[(C-d)||zeta||^2-2<zeta,J>]                   (13)

or, equivalently,

  M_alpha+k M_beta-tau||J||^2/(C-d)
     +tau(C-d)||zeta-J/(C-d)||^2.                     (14)

To check the correction, x_P-x_Z=(1+d)g_P+sum_j beta_j g_j gives

  Q_(P,Z)+Q_(Z,P)
       =-d||zeta||^2-(C+d)<zeta,g_Z>-<zeta,J>.

Adding twice tau times this expression to
c Delta(||g_Z||^2-||g_P||^2) gives exactly(13).
Thus the full mixed term in(13) is retained and must be paid by real
child slack. No diagonal-only or terminal-only PSD claim is used.

## 4. All30 multipliers of the five-step path

For the parameters in(4), Delta and the correction have signs

  Delta=-delta(2a C-delta)<=0,
  tau=delta(2a C-delta)/(C-d)>=0,
  eta=2tau>=0.                                         (15)

The inequality is strict for t<1. The full six-by-six array is

          0          1       2         3          4          5
  row0    0          2       0         0          0          0
  row1    2(1+X)     0       u         0          0          0
  row2    0          v       0         0          0          2cd+eta
  row3    0          0       2cX       0          2k         2cX
  row4    0          0       2cY       2k(1+X)    0          ku+2cY
  row5    0          0       2c+eta    0          kv         0.       (16)

This specifies all36 entries, including the zero diagonal and all30
ordered off-diagonal entries. Every listed nonzero entry is positive
on the whole closed interval; every other entry is exactly zero.
The selected support is a property of this constructed path only. It
is not asserted to be a lossless restriction on the original cone.

For a direct flow check, use v-u=2(C-1), k=1+2c, and c(C-d)=C+d.
The first two divergences are -2X,-2Y. The bridge divergence is

  v-u+2c(d-C)=-2(1+d).

The divergences at nodes3 and4 are respectively

  4cX-2kX=-2X,
  4cY+2kX+k(u-v)=-2Y.

The final divergence is

  k(v-u)+2c(2-C-d)=2(2C+d-1)=2s.

Thus L_flow^T e is precisely the required original vector omega. No
equation is imposed between any of the six formal gradients.

Array(16) is also obtained by embedding(7) on nodes0,1,2, embedding
k times(7) on nodes3,4,5, adding2c E from(10), and adding the two
actual correction coefficients eta. In particular its full matrix
from(1), not merely its function flow, is exactly(13).

## 5. Full PSD throughout the interval, with a strict margin

Set

  U_L=g_0-g_1, V_L=g_1-g_2,
  U_R=g_3-g_4, V_R=g_4-g_5, zeta=g_2-g_5.

Then J=X U_R+(X+Y)V_R, and the full original remainder is

  M_prim=w0||U_L||^2+w1||V_L||^2
      +k(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)
      +tau[(C-d)||zeta||^2-2<zeta,J>].                 (17)

For t<1 define

  R=X^2/w0+(X+Y)^2/w1=Rbar/(1-t),
  Rbar=t^2[1/(1+t)+lambda/(1+lambda t)],
  q=tau/(C-d), sigma=k-q R.                            (18)

The expression for R uses a^2=2 and p b=2lambda. Both scalar
functions t^2/(1+t) and lambda t^2/(1+lambda t) are increasing for
t>=0: their derivatives are t(2+t)/(1+t)^2 and
lambda t(2+lambda t)/(1+lambda t)^2. Hence

  Rbar<=1/2+lambda/(1+lambda)=lambda/2.                 (19)

The last equality follows from lambda/(1+lambda)=a/2.
Combining delta=1-t with(15) gives

  sigma=[(3C+d)(C-d)-(2a C-delta)Rbar]/(C-d)^2.         (20)

In fact sigma has an explicit strictly positive margin. From(6),

  (3C+d)(C-d)>(3/2)C^2>(15/4)C,
  (2a C-delta)Rbar<=a lambda C=(2+a)C<(7/2)C.

Therefore the numerator in(20) exceeds C/4 and

  sigma>C/[4(C-d)^2]>1/(4C)>0.                         (21)

There is no unresolved Schur-complement inequality here. An explicit
sum of squares for(17) is

  w0||U_L||^2+w1||V_L||^2
  +sigma(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)
  +q||(X+Y)sqrt(w0/w1)U_R-X sqrt(w1/w0)V_R||^2
  +tau(C-d)||zeta-J/(C-d)||^2.                         (22)

Expansion of the fourth term uses the exact weighted identity

  R(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)-||J||^2
   =||(X+Y)sqrt(w0/w1)U_R-X sqrt(w1/w0)V_R||^2.

Every coefficient in(22) is nonnegative. For t<1, w0,w1,sigma and
tau are strictly positive. Vanishing of(22) forces U_L=V_L=U_R=V_R=0
and then zeta=0. Thus, as a matrix on the ENTIRE R^6, M_prim has
kernel exactly span{(1,1,1,1,1,1)} and rank5. No quotient has replaced
the full PSD requirement.

For completeness, the common-gradient kernel is forced for every original
PSD certificate, not an extra restriction selected here. Set all formal
gradients equal to the scalar1 and set f_i=f_0-sum_(j<i)h_j. Then every Q_ij
and V_h vanish: the latter uses s^2=sum h_i^2+2sum_(j<i)h_j h_i.
Thus(2) gives e^T M_prim e=0. A PSD matrix with this equality satisfies
M_prim e=0. Rank5 is consequently the largest possible rank on R^6.

At t=1, use(17), not the divisions in(18): w0=w1=tau=0, so M_prim=0
on all of R^6. All entries in(16) remain finite and nonnegative.
Consequently(4),(16),(17) give a complete integrated feasible path for
every t in[1/2,1], including a genuine full-rank-on-e-perp start.
By(2), it proves V_h>=0 on every actual smooth convex trajectory.

## 6. The exact boundary and the full-cone quadratic obstruction

The physical formula(4) makes sense for all t>=1/2. At t=1 write
C_*=1+a+b. Equation(3) implies

  (b-1)C_*=lambda, (a-1)(b-1)=1/C_*.

The middle excess is d_*=rho C_*, and A_*=lambda C_*.
Therefore the five-step unit-quadratic product satisfies

  A_* (a-1)^2(b-1)^2 d_*=1.                            (23)

Every step in h(t) is strictly increasing with t. The four outside
derivatives are a,b,a,b, and the middle derivative is rho p+1>0.
At t=1 all five steps are strictly greater than1; in particular b>3-a>1.
Thus for EVERY t>1,

  A(t) product_(i=0)^4(h_i(t)-1)>1.                    (24)

Use the one static, actual scalar function f(x)=x^2/2, its unique
minimizer0, and x_0=1. Put g_i=x_i, f_i=x_i^2/2, with the original
GD recursion x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i. Every ordered Q_ij is exactly zero.
Writing Pi=product_(i=0)^4(1-h_i), direct telescoping gives

  V_h=sum_(i=0)^4 h_i(2-h_i)g_i^2-s(s+2)g_5^2
      =1-A^2 Pi^2.                                    (25)

Equation(24) makes(25) strictly negative. Since every Q_ij=0, any
array satisfying the original flow and full PSD would give V_h>=0
by(2), a contradiction. This excludes ALL original nonnegative arrays
and arbitrary full PSD remainders for these words, not just(16).
The claim is only about the explicitly chosen curve h(t), t>1.

The particular continued array also has an exact negative full-space
mode. For t>1, C-d=(3-a)+t[(2-a)p-1]>0, whereas Delta>0.
Evaluating(17) on (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3,g_4,g_5)=(1,1,1,0,0,0) gives

  M_prim[g]=-Delta<0.                                  (26)

Its coefficient signs need not be decided to establish this PSD
failure. The stronger actual-function obstruction(25) already rules
out every alternative array on the same continued physical word.

## 7. Capacity comparison and why the accepted lift cannot help here

This path reaches its largest feasible parameter at t=1, with capacity
A_*=lambda(1+a+b). It remains strictly below the accepted sufficient
threshold Gamma5_c107=7977607/806000.

For a transparent exact comparison, a<71/50. At u=161/100,

  F(u)=5921/10000-(39/100)a>383/10000>0,

so b<161/100. Consequently

  C_*<403/100, lambda<121/50,
  A_*<48763/5000<49/5<Gamma5_c107,                     (27)
  Gamma5_c107-49/5=78807/806000>0.

The parameter that would reach the accepted threshold is explicitly

  t_Gamma=(Gamma5_c107-a)/(lambda p+1)>1.

It and every larger parameter are already excluded by(24)--(25).
No numerical threshold search or unproved feasibility oracle is used.

There is also a direct comparison to the ORIGINAL benchmark that does
not mistake the sufficient constant Gamma5 for the true ray ratio.
The endpoint word h(1) is basic in the accepted primitive construction:

* The single step a=sqrt(2) joins two empty primitive words.
* The two-step word (a,b) joins (a) to an empty word, since its bridge
  excess b-1 satisfies (b-1)(lambda+b)=lambda.
* The full endpoint joins two such two-step words, with bridge excess
  rho C_* satisfying (rho C_*)(2C_*+rho C_*)=C_*^2.

Thus h(1) uses exactly the ordinary primitive joins from empty leaves.
Let an arbitrary fully expanded maximizing original B_n tree be fixed.
Replace all n primitive empty leaves by h(t), keep the dominant leaf
empty, and recompute the physical bridges as in fact04219554e41d9750,
Sections11--12. Denote the actual output word by Hsub_n(t) and its mass
by Dsub_n(t). Its length is6n.

The maps Omega and G are increasing in each positive capacity, directly
from their defining square-root formulas. Since A(t)<=A_*, induction
over this fixed tree gives

  Dsub_n(t)<=Dsub_n(1).

At t=1 the expanded object is itself an admissible original primitive/
dominant construction with6n steps. The all-split recurrence defining
B_(6n) therefore gives

  Dsub_n(t)<=Dsub_n(1)<=B_(6n).                         (28)

The accepted general full-certificate substitution machinery, applicable
to the actual primitive certificates proved jointly above, gives the
all-function equality W_(6n)=1/Dsub_n(t), with its matching Huber witness.
Alternatively, the Huber lower witness alone suffices for the following
failure comparison:

  W_(6n)(Hsub_n(t))>=1/B_(6n)=U_(6n)^ZJ.              (29)

This holds for every n>=0 and every t in[1/2,1]. In particular it holds
at the correct selected physical horizons18*2^j-6, obtained by taking
n=3*2^j-1. No relabeling of6n as n occurs. The other authorized length6
threshold and its horizons21*2^j-7 are not used by this length5 pilot.

## 8. Scope and resource record

This is one completely specified deformation of actual original
certificates, with an integrated finite interval, a full sign proof,
all six flow equations, and a full-space PSD factorization. It changes
the physical word and matrix together, and its interior remainder has
the maximum possible rank5 given the unavoidable common-gradient kernel.

It is nevertheless a failed improving-seed mechanism. The endpoint is
an already known subthreshold basic construction. Neither its existence
nor deforming below it is original-target progress. Equations(25)--(29)
explain exactly why this chosen path and its accepted lifts cannot supply
the requested improvement.

The support in(16), the equal child capacities, and the chosen affine
parameter curve are construction choices, never lossless reductions.
There is no assertion of full-cone nonexistence outside this curve, no
arbitrary-word exact-profile result, no new verified fact, and no target
completion. A different physical curve would need its own simultaneous
full sign/flow/PSD proof and would have to avoid the exhibited quadratic
boundary; no such continuation is asserted here.

Recall included TASK.md, master_guidance7e217295720a817f, synthesis
0c2edccc0f91af09, the relevant accepted proofs, earlier scoped dead ends,
and the later b069a3c0765b4d44 notice preserving max2's one-round cap.

# Max40: unrestricted step-2 predecessor and a changing-gradient continuation

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No assertion newly proved
here is a fact-graph node. This is analytical round40, the first of two
rounds under master guidance78e668c9782fc528 and the current TASK.md:
completed baseline39, completed ceiling41, attempted ceiling42 including
the historical interruption. Runtime completion is recorded only after
this session returns. No numerical, solver, machine-algebra, replay,
formal-verifier, installation, consult, or subagent resource is used.

The original all-nonnegative-word, all-sufficiently-large-horizon target
W_n(h)>=(1-o(1))/B_n, with W=2R, remains OPEN. This manuscript does not
replace that quantifier by the particular diagnostic words below.

Accepted inputs, each read in the fact graph:

- 62a7d9329121fd75: the finite triangular jets on the word(2,2,1), and
  only its stated finite reference comparisons.
- 1252b8823bb5a290: smooth-convex interpolation inequalities, the explicit
  conjugate construction, and normalization.
- 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: the COMPLETE alternating-ray channel F_ray_n.
- dfde2fa326c908bf: the FULL schedule-dependent complex-polynomial channel
  D_h, Gap_h and its terminal upper bound.
- b98bb807d514e80d: K_n and the exact benchmark recurrences defining B_n.

All additional statements, including minimum rigidity, the new lower
identity, the construction, all its rows, and all new-word comparisons,
are proved jointly below. The unverified max39 draft was a retrieval
cursor and a source of questions; none of its new claims is a premise.

Short letters in this manuscript are local abbreviations. In shared
metadata the new objects have the suffix40; in particular local p,q,e
mean accepted p_ft,q_ft,e_ft, not the project's exponent p or ray q.
Inner products and norms are Euclidean. The notation conv means the set
of finite convex combinations. A subgradient y of a convex function psi
at g means psi(v)>=psi(g)+<y,v-g> for every v.

## 1. Retained data and the exact unrestricted question

Let E be the old two-dimensional plane and set

    p=(1,2sqrt(3)), q=(-2,-sqrt(3)), e=(1,0),
    nu=(1,-sqrt(3)).

These vectors are embedded isometrically in any larger Euclidean space.
The retained positions, gradients, and ABSOLUTE values are exactly

    position      gradient       value
    p             p              13/2
    -p            q               9/2
    3e            e               5/2
    2e            e               3/2.                         (1)

The physical updates are p-2p=-p, -p-2q=3e, and3e-e=2e. Accepted
62a7d9329121fd75 supplies these data and all their ordered rows.

For a preceding step2 the new point and gradient obey

    X=p+2z, grad F(X)=z.                                    (2)

Define D_2 to be the infimum of dist(X,argmin F)^2 over EVERY finite
dimension and EVERY everywhere finite convex differentiable function F
with globally1-Lipschitz gradient satisfying(1)-(2). No restriction is
imposed on z, its conjugate height, the dimension, the minimum location,
the number of minimizers, or the unsampled values of F. In particular
neither <e,z><=1 nor <nu,z><=1 is assumed.

Sections2-5 prove D_2=17, with an actual minimum. The exact best payoff
for this retained-jet predecessor problem is therefore3/17. This is a
family optimum, not a determination of unrestricted W_4(2,2,2,1).

## 2. Forced minimum level and the complete height inequalities

For any actual jet (x_i,g_i,f_i), put

    beta_i=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    y_i=x_i-g_i.

The old heights beta_i are all0. Their y_i, in the order(1), are
0,nu,2e,e. The exact signed interpolation expression from accepted
1252b8823bb5a290 expands to

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =beta_j-beta_i+<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                       (3)

Every actual ordered row has Q_ij<=0. Both orientations will be retained.

Every interpolant of(1) has minimum value0 at the origin. To prove this,
write g=grad F(0). The smooth-convex inequality from each old gradient
b in {p,q,e} gives

    F(0)>=||g||^2/2-<b,g>.

The positive relation p/6+q/3+e/2=0 yields F(0)>=||g||^2/2.
The smooth descent upper bound from the jet at p gives

    F(0)<=13/2-13+13/2=0.

Thus F(0)=0 and g=0. Convexity makes0 a global minimizer. If m is ANY
minimizer, the old lower interpolation rows give <p,m>,<q,m>,<e,m><=0.
Their positive weighted sum is0, so all three products vanish. Since
p,e span E,

    argmin F is a nonempty closed convex subset of E-perpendicular. (4)

In particular a closest minimizer to X exists. This proves the minimum
normalization without assuming the old projection formula globally.

For the new jet write beta=<X,z>-F(X)-||z||^2/2 and y=p+z. The entire
set of new-old directed inequalities is

    beta>=0,
    beta>=<nu,z>-1,
    beta>=2(<e,z>-1),
    beta>=<e,z>-1,
    beta<=<p+z,z-b> for b=p,q,e.                            (5)

The last e inequality is repeated for the two old points sharing gradient
e; both lower inequalities from their distinct residuals are displayed.
For any minimizer m the new/minimum row also gives

    beta>=<m,z>.                                         (6)

These are consequences of actual interpolation. Sufficiency will not be
asserted without constructing a single static function.

## 3. A sharp all-dimensional radius identity

Choose m to be the closest minimizer to X. Decompose z=v+t, where v is
in E and t is perpendicular to E. By(4), m is perpendicular to E too.
Introduce four NONNEGATIVE slacks, each from a specified original row:

    A_p=||z||^2-13-beta,
    A_q=||z||^2+<p-q,v>+8-beta,
    B_nu=beta-<nu,v>+1,
    C_min=beta-<m,t>.                                    (7)

Here A_p and A_q are the two upper inequalities in(5) for b=p,q;
B_nu is the lower inequality from the old q jet; C_min is(6).
The constants use ||p||^2=13 and <p,q>=-8.

The following is an exact identity:

    ||p+2z-m||^2
      =17+||v+p||^2+||m-t||^2
          +2A_p+A_q+B_nu+2C_min.                         (8)

For an explicit expansion check, the sum of the four slack terms is

    3||z||^2+2<p,v>-17-2<m,t>,

because p-q-nu=2p and the beta coefficients cancel. Adding the two
squared norms and17 gives

    13+4||v||^2+4<p,v>+4||t||^2+||m||^2-4<m,t>,

which is the left side. Thus(8) uses no rank reduction, assumed reversal,
closure, continuity argument, gradient wedge, or fixed minimizer.
Every summand beyond17 is nonnegative, proving

    D_2>=17.                                             (9)

Equality in(8) requires v=-p and m=t. The zero C_min and B_nu slacks
then give beta=||t||^2 and beta=<nu,-p>-1=4. Consequently every optimizer
has the following necessary new-jet geometry, up to an orthogonal change
of coordinates perpendicular to E:

    ||t||=2, m=t, z=-p+t, X=-p+2t, beta=4,
    F(X)=17/2.                                          (10)

The last value follows from the definition of beta. In particular any
optimizer has a nonzero transverse gradient and at least two distinct
minimizers0,m. Its dimension is at least3. This is a necessary equality
condition for actual optimizers, not an assumed sparsity principle.

## 4. A static conjugate construction and its exact minimum set

The following construction will attain(10) and carry complete histories.
For any integer k>=0, choose pairwise orthogonal vectors

    a_1,...,a_k in E-perpendicular, each of norm2,

and work in the actual space E+span{a_1,...,a_k}, of dimension k+2.
Set

    M_0=0, M_j=sum_(l=1)^j a_l for1<=j<=k,
    psi_k(w)=max{0,<nu,w>-1,2<e,w>-2,
                         <M_1,w>,...,<M_k,w>},
    H_k(w)=||w||^2/2+psi_k(w),
    F_k(x)=sup_w {<x,w>-H_k(w)}.                         (11)

When k=0 the list of M planes is empty. This F_0 need not equal the old
triangle projection function at unsampled points. The required invariant
is precisely ALL the old physical jets(1), not global equality to that
projection function. Each complete run below uses one static F_k.

The function H_k is finite and1-strongly convex, and H_k>=||w||^2/2.
Thus for every x its conjugate has a unique maximizing vector g(x).
The maximizing condition is x in the subdifferential of H_k at g(x).
Adding the two strong-subgradient inequalities at x,x' gives

    <x-x',g(x)-g(x')>>=||g(x)-g(x')||^2.

Hence g is1-Lipschitz. The two maximizing inequalities also give

    0<=F_k(x')-F_k(x)-<g(x),x'-x>
       <=<g(x')-g(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

Therefore F_k is finite, convex, differentiable and globally1-smooth,
with grad F_k=g. This repeats the elementary construction underlying
accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and checks its applicability to(11).

If an affine plane of psi_k with slope y is active at g, then y is a
subgradient of psi_k at g. At x=g+y the actual gradient is g and

    F_k(x)=<x,g>-||g||^2/2-psi_k(g).                     (12)

The same statement holds for any convex combination of active slopes.

The full minimum set and minimum value are exactly

    min F_k=0,
    argmin F_k=P_k:=conv{M_0,M_1,...,M_k}.                (13)

For x in P_k, <x,w><=max_j<M_j,w><=psi_k(w), so the supremum in(11)
is0, attained at w=0. Conversely take x outside P_k and let m_0 be its
Euclidean projection onto that compact convex set. Put d=x-m_0. The
projection inequality gives

    <d,M_j><=<d,m_0> for all j,
    <x,d>=<m_0,d>+||d||^2>max_j<M_j,d>.

For sufficiently small epsilon>0, the two affine planes with intercepts
-1 and-2 in(11) remain strictly below the maximum of the M planes at
w=epsilon d. Therefore

    <x,epsilon d>-H_k(epsilon d)
      =epsilon[<x,d>-max_j<M_j,d>]-epsilon^2||d||^2/2>0

when epsilon is small enough. Thus F_k(x)>0. This proves(13) without
silently replacing the true minimum set by a chosen point.

## 5. Full physical history and the attained unrestricted predecessor

Define backward-indexed new gradients, positions, heights and values by

    g_0=p,
    g_j=-g_(j-1)+a_j
       =(-1)^j p+sum_(l=1)^j (-1)^(j-l)a_l,
    X_j=M_j+g_j,
    beta_j=<M_j,g_j>
          =4 if j is odd, and0 if j is even,
    f_j=(13+4j)/2,                         0<=j<=k.       (14)

The index j counts prepends BACKWARD. The forward physical trajectory is

    X_k,X_(k-1),...,X_0=p,-p,3e,2e,                     (15)

and its word is k+2 copies of2 followed by1. In particular X_0 denotes
the old p point in this manuscript, not the start of the forward word
when k>0. The initial point of that word is X_k.

We now verify every jet on the SAME function F_k.

First, psi_k(p)=psi_k(q)=psi_k(e)=0. At p the zero plane is active;
at q the nu plane is active because <nu,q>=1. At e both the zero
plane and the2e plane are active. All M_j planes equal0 at old planar
gradients. Thus the residuals0,nu,2e,e are subgradients at p,q,e,e,
respectively. Formula(12) gives exactly ALL four old absolute values(1).

For each new g_i, the M-plane values are explicit:

    <M_j,g_i>=beta_i                              if j>=i,
    <M_j,g_i>=0                                   if j<i is even,
    <M_j,g_i>=4(-1)^(i-1)                         if j<i is odd. (16)

These formulas include M_0=0 with value0. Every entry is at most beta_i.
The two fixed affine planes at g_i have values

    <nu,g_i>-1 = -6 if i even, and4 if i odd,
    2<e,g_i>-2 = 0 if i even, and-4 if i odd.            (17)

Together(16)-(17) prove psi_k(g_i)=beta_i, with the M_i plane active
(the zero plane for i=0). Hence M_i is its subgradient at g_i, and
(12) gives grad F_k(X_i)=g_i and F_k(X_i)=||g_i||^2/2=f_i.
Orthogonality gives ||g_i||^2=13+4i.

The physical recursion is checked without reversing an unspecified map:

    X_j-2g_j=M_j-g_j=M_(j-1)+g_(j-1)=X_(j-1).          (18)

The gradients genuinely change: g_j+g_(j-1)=a_j has a new nonzero
coordinate. The original suffix updates were already checked in(1).

For explicit completeness, all ordered interpolation rows also follow
directly from(3). Between two backward-indexed jets i,j,

    Q_ij=<M_j,g_i>-beta_i<=0                            (19)

by(16). From any new jet i to the four old jets in the order(1), the
four Q values are

    -beta_i,
    -6 if i even and0 if i odd,
    0 if i even and-8 if i odd,
    0 if i even and-6 if i odd.                         (20)

For every reversed old/new row, Q_old,i=0, since M_i is perpendicular
to the old gradients and <M_i,g_i>=beta_i. The old-old rows are those
of accepted62a7d9329121fd75. Repeated occurrences of the p jet at i=0
have the same position, gradient and value and cause no exception.

For any actual minimizer m in P_k, the row from jet i to m is
<m,g_i>-beta_i<=0 by(16) and convexity. Its reverse row is0. For the
four old jets, the rows to m are0 because m has zero planar component;
their reverse rows are respectively0,-1,-2,-1.
Thus both orientations to EVERY actual minimizer, not only one selected
minimum sample, hold as well. Equations(19)-(20) account for all earlier,
new, old, terminal and optimum pairs.

The closest minimizer to X_k is M_k. For0<=j<=k,

    <g_k,M_j-M_k>
       =-4 sum_(l=j+1)^k (-1)^(k-l)
       =0 if k-j is even, and-4 if k-j is odd.           (21)

The same inequality holds for every convex combination in P_k. Expanding
||X_k-m||^2 about M_k shows it is at least||g_k||^2, with equality at
M_k. Therefore the TRUE initial squared radius and payoff are

    dist(X_k,argmin F_k)^2=13+4k,
    2(F_k(2e)-min F_k)/dist(X_k,argmin F_k)^2
       =3/(13+4k).                                     (22)

For k=1, take a_1 of norm2. The resulting point and gradient are

    M_1=a_1, g_1=-p+a_1, X_1=-p+2a_1,
    beta_1=4, F_1(X_1)=17/2,
    argmin F_1=[0,a_1], dist(X_1,argmin F_1)^2=17.       (23)

This attains(9), proving the unrestricted sharp answer D_2=17 and all
the claimed equality requirements. In particular <nu,g_1>=5>1: the
new gradient violates the old wedge. The original absolute jets still
all hold. Its radius is strictly below the previously displayed planar
wedge-free radius29-4sqrt(3), since12-4sqrt(3)>0.

The first GENUINELY CHANGING TWO-step prepend is k=2:

    g_1=-p+a_1, X_1=-p+2a_1, f_1=17/2,
    g_2=p-a_1+a_2, X_2=p+2a_2, f_2=21/2,
    M_2=a_1+a_2,
    argmin F_2=conv{0,a_1,a_1+a_2}.

It executes the actual word(2,2,2,2,1), including the two new steps2,
has true squared radius21, and has payoff1/7. Notice that||X_2||^2=29;
using the origin denominator would lose the correct normalization.

Equations(11),(14),(18),(21) are an executable symbolic continuation:
retain every affine plane and every jet; add a fresh orthogonal vector
of norm2, add its new cumulative minimum plane, reflect the previous
gradient and add that vector. The finite maximum in(11) is an explicit
function, not an unknown feasible-set oracle. Extending from k to k+1
can change unsampled function values but preserves all earlier queried
points, gradients, absolute values and rows in the natural embedding.
For EACH resulting finite physical word, all queries use its single F_k.

## 6. Complete same-word alternating-ray and single-cut comparisons

Write r=k+2, and let h^(r) be exactly r copies of2 followed by1.
Its actual length is n=r+1; there is no changed enclosing parameter.
The construction payoff is

    V_r=3/(4r+5), for every integer r>=2.                (24)

We apply the COMPLETE accepted reference1fca3e5dd555cb4a, retaining its
initial point1, specified minimizer0, remote offsets and accumulated
areas. For a checkpoint decomposition, that fact defines ordinary-block
sums s_i, selected steps b_i, positive nonterminal magnitudes m_i and
nonnegative offsets O_i through

    m_0=1/(1+s_0), O_0=0,
    m_(i+1)=((b_i-1)m_i-O_i)/(1+s_(i+1)),
    O_(i+1)=O_i+s_i m_i.

For at least two checkpoints, m_old=m_(ell-2)>0 if ell is their count,
and the terminal constraint is

    z_term=(b_(ell-1)-1)m_(ell-1)-O_(ell-1)
       >=(1+s_ell)m_old.                                (25)

If the final step1 is selected along with another checkpoint, z_term<=0
and(25) is impossible. A singleton step1 has payoff0. If at least two
checkpoints are all steps2, each recurrence gives m_(i+1)<=m_i because
O_i>=0 and s_(i+1)>=0. The terminal unselected block includes step1,
so s_ell>=1. Consequently

    z_term<=m_(ell-1)<=m_old<2m_old<=(1+s_ell)m_old,

contradicting(25). This eliminates EVERY multi-checkpoint set before
any area optimization, without omitting a feasible terminal interval.

The empty checkpoint set gives1/(4r+3). A singleton at the step2
with zero-based index j,0<=j<=r-1, gives exactly

    1/[(2j+1)^2(4r-4j-1)].                              (26)

Its denominator minus4r-1 is

    4j[(j+1)(4r-4j-1)-1]>=0,

since4r-4j-1>=3. Thus the singleton j=0 gives the largest value.
We have proved the full same-word identities

    F_ray_(r+1)(h^(r))=K_(r+1)(h^(r))=1/(4r-1).         (27)

The K identity follows either directly from its empty/singleton formula
or from the accepted inclusion K<=F_ray and the singleton lower bound.
For all r>=2,

    V_r/F_ray_(r+1)(h^(r))=3(4r-1)/(4r+5)>1,

and the ratio tends to3 as r tends to infinity. These comparisons use
the exact full word after every prepend, not the old suffix reference.

## 7. The full accepted complex-polynomial channel on the new words

Use exactly D_h and Gap_h from accepted dfde2fa326c908bf. For completeness,
for a complex parameter z=alpha+i beta, the prefix polynomial is
Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z); the points and gradients are
xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z Poly_i(z), with optimum xi_*=gamma_*=0.
For every directed index-simple cycle C on these sample indices, put

    Area_C=sum_(j->i in C) Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i),
    Energy_C=sum_(j->i in C)|xi_i-xi_j|^2,
    kappa=alpha-|z|^2.

The FULL legal set D_h requires kappa Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C| for
every cycle. The accepted Gap_h(z) is the maximal normalized terminal
value over legal values for these precise points and gradients, with
initial point1 and specified minimizer0. Its denominator remains1.
It is NOT renormalized about another closest minimizer. The accepted
bound we use is

    Gap_h(z)<=(2Re z-|z|^2)|Poly_(r+1)(z)|^2.            (28)

For h=h^(r) with r>=2, set

    t=1-2z=d+i b, sigma=|t|^2.

Here d,b are real, and these letters are local to this section. The
cycle on optimum and index0 gives kappa>=0, so0<=sigma<=1, and
kappa=(1-sigma)/4, beta=-b/2. The first three physical points are
1,t,t^2. The cycle(*,0,1), with positions0,1,t, has area b and
energy2(1+sigma-d). The cycle(0,1,2), with positions1,t,t^2, has area
b|1-t|^2 and energy2|1-t|^2(1+sigma+d).

If t=1, z=0 and Gap_h(z)=0. Otherwise division of the second cycle
inequality by|1-t|^2 is legitimate. The two inequalities imply

    (1-sigma)(1+sigma-d)>=2b^2,
    (1-sigma)(1+sigma+d)>=2b^2,
    d^2>= (sigma^2+2sigma-1)/2.                          (29)

These are only necessary conditions, used to bound the full legal set
from above. No substitution of the smaller universal lens occurs.
Repeated geometric positions do not invalidate index-simple cycles.

The actual terminal polynomial is t^r(1+t)/2. Substitution in(28) gives

    Gap_h(z)<=sigma^r[4-(2d+sigma-1)^2]/16.              (30)

We now prove a uniform elementary estimate for the bracket. Put
delta=1-sigma. If delta>=1/5, the bracket is at most4<=20delta.
If0<=delta<=1/5, (29) gives

    d^2>=1-2delta+delta^2/2>=(1-2delta)^2,

because their difference is2delta-7delta^2/2>=0. Thus either
d>=1-2delta or d<=-(1-2delta). In the first case
2d+sigma-1=2d-delta>=2-5delta. In the second its absolute value
is at least2-3delta>=2-5delta. The latter lower bound is nonnegative.
Consequently the bracket in either case is at most

    4-(2-5delta)^2=20delta-25delta^2<=20delta.

Combining both cases with(30), maximizing the single scalar expression
sigma^r(1-sigma) by its derivative, and then using the binomial theorem,
gives

    sup_(z in D_(h^(r))) Gap_(h^(r))(z)
      <= (5/4) r^r/(r+1)^(r+1)
      <= 5/[8(r+1)].                                   (31)

For the last step, (1+1/r)^r>=1+r/r=2 implies
(r/(r+1))^r<=1/2. Every integer r>=2 is included. The endpoints
sigma=0,1 have zero in the derived bound; t=1 was already handled.

Comparison with the ACTUAL constructed payoff is strict:

    V_r-5/[8(r+1)]
      =(4r-1)/[8(r+1)(4r+5)]>0.                         (32)

Thus the entire accepted schedule-dependent complex-polynomial channel
is below the new witness, on EVERY new word in this diagnostic family.
This includes the complete real proportional-gradient polynomial channel
with its allowed values. In particular it also controls every scalar
convex quadratic: for curvature theta in[0,1] the real parameter theta
is legal (its beta is0 and kappa=theta-theta^2>=0), and its actual
terminal payoff is no larger than Gap_h(theta). Orthogonal sums of
quadratics give squared-distance weighted averages of scalar payoffs;
flat directions lie in the minimum set and contribute no true distance.

This does not compare with EVERY nonlinear one-dimensional trajectory,
which need not have a fixed proportional-gradient polynomial parameter.
It does not upper-bound unrestricted W_(r+1)(h^(r)).

For reference,1/(4r-1)<=5/[8(r+1)] when r>=2. Thus the maximum of the
three applicable accepted ray, quadratic and FULL complex channels is
at most5/[8(r+1)], whereas V_r divided by this bound is

    24(r+1)/[5(4r+5)]>1.                                (33)

The full complex comparison is an inequality, not an asserted exact
formula for that channel. The ray and K formulas(27) are exact.

## 8. Normalization, benchmark, and the unresolved quantifier

Every F_k above has smoothness1 and minimum0. Multiplying it by any
L>0 gives smoothness L, and GD steps h_t/L give the identical trajectory
and normalized payoff(22). To make the true initial distance1, let
rho=sqrt(13+4k) and define

    f(u)=L F_k(M_k+rho u)/rho^2.

Its initial point is g_k/rho of norm1, the closest minimizer is0, its
gradient is L/rho times the old gradient, and its GD trajectory is the
translated and rescaled version of(15). Thus the original project
convention W=2R is unchanged. The normalized payoff is
2(F_k(2e)-0)/(13+4k)=3/(13+4k).

The exact benchmark can be compared on the stated words too. From the
accepted recurrence in b98bb807d514e80d, selecting k=0 at horizon n
gives S_bullet(n)>=S_bullet(n-1)+psi(0,S_bullet(n-1)). For y>=0,

    psi(0,y)=(3-2y+sqrt((2y+1)(2y+9)))/4>=1,

because the square root is at least2y+1. Induction from S_bullet(0)=0
gives B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1>=2n+1. Hence on n=r+1,

    V_r/U_(r+1)^ZJ=3B_(r+1)/(4r+5)
       >=3(2r+3)/(4r+5)>1.                              (34)

This is a complete quantitative comparison on the diagnostic family,
not an arbitrary-word assertion. The family has only steps2 and a final
step1. The original target quantifies over EVERY nonnegative word.
No unknown feasible-set oracle, finite gain, or assertion of suffix
redesign is substituted for that missing uniform statement.

## 9. Scoped theorem proved by this joint draft

Subject to formal verification, Sections1-8 establish the following
single self-contained scoped result.

For every finite-dimensional globally1-smooth convex interpolant of the
four fixed absolute jets(1), every actual step-2 predecessor X=p+2z has
true squared distance at least17. Equality is attained by the explicit
three-dimensional F_1 in(11), with minimum segment[0,a_1], closest
minimizer a_1, ||a_1||=2, new gradient -p+a_1, new point -p+2a_1, new
value17/2 and conjugate height4. Every equality case necessarily has
this new-jet geometry up to an isometry fixing the old plane. Thus the
exact unrestricted retained-jet optimum is D_2=17 and its payoff3/17.

For every integer k>=0, the explicit finite-dimensional F_k in(11)
retains those same old jets and realizes the full word consisting of
k+2 steps2 followed by1. Its additional gradients genuinely change
according to(14); all earlier rows, absolute values and the entire
minimum simplex(13) are retained. Its true squared initial radius is
13+4k, its terminal value3/2, and its normalized payoff3/(13+4k).
For r=k+2 this payoff strictly exceeds the COMPLETE accepted ray
reference1/(4r-1), all scalar/orthogonal convex quadratics, and the FULL
accepted complex-polynomial reference bounded in(31). Its ray ratio
tends to3. It also exceeds1/B_(r+1) on these specific physical words.

Optimality over ALL retained-data interpolants has been proved here
ONLY for the one-step c=2 problem. No claim of such optimality for k>=2
is made. The continuation theorem asserts an actual priced family and
its complete stated reference separation, not a minimal-dimension or
rank theorem. There is no claim that a fixed finite-dimensional function
simultaneously realizes all k, nor any shortcut that discards old rows.

The old stable-prefix regime with all steps in[0,1] was not reopened.
The new continuation uses actual steps2. Mixed or arbitrary step-size
prefixes, and a uniform original-profile bridge, remain open. Any later
extension must retain the complete planes and minimum simplex in(11),
not just the old triangle or the new gradient norm.

## 10. Retrieval and resource provenance

The search program was the active unrestricted predecessor/large-prefix
construction; its missing mechanism was a sharp radius certificate
with movable minima and an executable full-history continuation.
Matlas was called on finite smooth-convex interpolation with movable
minima; it returned the error "Remote end closed connection without
response". This was reported rather than interpreted as an empty result.
Primary-source web search located arXiv1502.05666, already available at
downloads/1502.05666.txt. Sections2.4-2.5, Theorem4's proof and
Corollary1 were read. Their definitions distinguish conjugate values,
strong convexity, primal gradients and subgradients. The max-affine
conjugate pattern is applicable to(11), and its needed smoothness and
interpolation are proved directly here from the accepted predecessor.
No external theorem is imported as a new proof premise.

An initial shell search returned "rg: command not found"; bounded
grep/sed/direct file reads were used. An early broad shared-memory
recall exceeded the output display, so exact relevant files and latest
guidance were reread in bounded sections. File I/O and metadata checks
are the only local programs used; no mathematical computation was run.

This manuscript is to be published as one COMPLETE joint shared draft.
There is no fact_submit authorization in this wave. A shared draft,
manual audit, or eventual independent research audit does not count as
verifier acceptance. One allocated analytical round remains after this
round returns, subject to any newer TASK/master guidance.

# A variable-first-step threshold region excluded by the nested witness

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Max4 round39, the first
of two analytical rounds under master_guidance78e668c9782fc528, completed
baseline38 and ceiling40. No fact_submit is authorized. This document is
not a fact, an accepted theorem, or a solution of the original Goal.

The only accepted input used in the certificate exclusion is
04219554e41d9750, Section10. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the
interpolation orientation; the actual functions and all their rows are
also constructed directly below. No other mathematical assertion is
imported as a premise. In particular the earlier nested construction is
rederived on its enlarged domain, rather than extrapolated.

Audit warning, explicitly acknowledged: original43a616da7da451c7 has the
false first-increment bound identified by f0730b188bcec643 and main
guidance09f6c32b4d027b38. Its original file and hash are preserved. None
of its scalar positivity bounds, its old-system survivor assertion, or
its whole-box theorem is used in this proof. A local repair of that
scalar argument is not treated as acceptance of its complete package.

All reasoning below is symbolic. No numerical evaluation, solver,
machine algebra, replay, installation, consult, subagent or verifier
call was used.

## 1. Original certificate and target region

Let h=(u,w,z,v) be four nonnegative physical steps, and put

 s=u+w+z+v, C=1+s.

The original full primitive certificate has twenty nonnegative numbers
Lambda_ij for distinct i,j in{0,1,2,3,4}, with Lambda_ii=0. Define the
column e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T, the matrix T by T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i and
T_it=0 for t>i, the flow matrix L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, and

 omega=(-2u,-2w,-2z,-2v,2s)^T,
 Dprim=diag(u(2-u),w(2-w),z(2-z),v(2-v),-s(s+2)),
 M=Dprim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.

The requirements are L_flow^T e=omega and M positive semidefinite on
all of R^5. Every original coordinate is retained, including at zero
steps. There is no sparsity, rank, reversal, or symmetry restriction.

For any actual gradient-descent trajectory of a convex 1-smooth
function, write X_i for the positions, G_i for the gradients, F_i for
the values, and define

 Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(F_i-F_4)
       +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||G_i||^2-sC||G_4||^2.      (1)

The accepted identity from04219554e41d9750 is

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(G_0,...,G_4)>.  (2)

Here the matrix inner product is trace of the transpose times the
second matrix. Since every Q_ij<=0, an actual trajectory with V_h<0
excludes every such original certificate.

The region considered in this document is

 h>=0, C>=C0:=193/25, w<=4/3, 6w+7v<=18.             (3)

There is no fixed first step, no third-step interval or upper bound,
and no assumed ordering of the four steps. In particular (3) contains
the entire threshold part of w<=4/3,v<=10/7, as well as the additional
region v>10/7,w<3-7v/6. The conclusion will be that (3) has no full
primitive certificate. It is not a reduction of all threshold words
to this region.

## 2. The cut conditions, with their actual functions

For an index j in{0,1,2,3} put Pj=sum_(i<j)h_i and Rj=sum_(i>j)h_i.
Every full primitive certificate necessarily satisfies

 C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+Pj)(1+Rj).                         (4)

To prove (4) when h_j>1, set A=1+Pj,B=1+Rj,r=(h_j-1)/B>0.
Use the single one-dimensional function

 F_cut(x)=max_(g in[-r,1]) (xg-g^2/2), X_0=A.

Its derivative is the projection of x onto[-r,1]. Up to query j the
positions are 1 plus the remaining prefix sum, so their derivative is1.
The selected update takes X_j=1 to-(h_j-1)=-Br. Every later position
is -r times one plus its remaining tail sum, so the derivative is-r
and X_4=-r. All sampled values equal X_i G_i-G_i^2/2.

For any saturated values F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2, expansion of the
updates gives

 V_h=||X_0||^2-||X_4+sG_4||^2.                     (5)

Indeed 2sum h_i<X_i,G_i>-sum h_i^2||G_i||^2 telescopes to
||X_0||^2-||X_4||^2; the remaining terms in(1) are
-2s<X_4,G_4>-s^2||G_4||^2. Thus the cut function has
V_h=A^2-C^2r^2, proving(4) by(2). When h_j<=1, (4) is automatic.
This construction includes zero steps elsewhere in the word. It has
an actual minimizer0 and is convex and1-smooth by the projection
argument given explicitly in the next section.

In particular the first and last cuts imply

 u<=2C/(C+1)<2, v<=2C/(C+1)<2.                      (6)

For a step at most1 these bounds are automatic, since C>=193/25>1.
For a step exceeding1 they follow by rearranging C(u-1)<=C-u, or
respectively C(v-1)<=C-v.

## 3. Complete phase domain of one nested planar family

For this section only, assume

 u>1, w>=0, z>1, v>=0,
 a=u-1, m=1+w, c=z-1, D=1+v,
 N=u+m, q=a/m>0, t=c/D>0,
 C=N+D+c=N+D(1+t).                                  (7)

No upper bound on u,w,z,v is needed here. Given an amplitude rho>=0,
define five base and auxiliary scalar positions and gradients by

 Xbase=(N,m,1,-c,-t), Gbase=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
 Xaux=(1,-a,-q,cq,tq), Gaux=(1,-q,-q,tq,tq),
 X_i=(Xbase_i,sqrt(rho)*Xaux_i),
 G_i=(Gbase_i,sqrt(rho)*Gaux_i),
 F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                          (8)

These are the original four physical updates. The base updates use
N-u=m,m-w=1,1-z=-c,-c+vt=-t. The auxiliary updates use
1-u=-a,-a+wq=-q,-q+zq=cq,cq-vtq=tq.

For the saturated values in(8), the residual is exactly

 Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>.                            (9)

The base target normals are(N-1,w,0,-vt,0); the auxiliary target
normals are(0,-wq,0,vtq,0). Here is the complete finite-row check,
organized by the target index j; omitted diagonal terms are zero.

 * Target0: sources1,2 give0; sources3,4 give-(1+t)(N-1).
 * Target1: source0 gives-rho*w*q*(1+q); source2 gives0;
   sources3,4 give-w*(1+t)*(1+rho*q^2).
 * Targets2 and4: every source gives0.
 * Target3: sources1,2 give-vt*(1+t)*(1+rho*q^2);
   source4 gives0; source0 gives

       Q_03=vt[-(1+t)+rho*q*(1-tq)].                 (10)

All twenty directed finite rows are therefore nonpositive exactly when

 0<=rho<=(1+t)/(q(1-tq))  if v>0 and tq<1;
 arbitrary rho>=0        if v=0 or tq>=1.             (11)

The equality cases v=0 and tq=1 are included. Necessity of this phase
condition is the single row(10), and sufficiency is the full list above.

Add the optimum jet X_star=G_star=F_star=0. All Q_(i,star) vanish.
For Q_(star,i)=-<G_i,X_i-G_i>, the respective inner products are

 N-1, w*(1+rho*q^2), 0, vt^2*(1+rho*q^2), 0,

which are all nonnegative. Thus every optimum row holds as well.

An explicit single static function is

 K_rho=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3},
 F_rho(X)=max_(G in K_rho)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).         (12)

The other sampled gradients satisfy G_2=G_1 and G_4=G_3=-tG_1, so
0 belongs to K_rho. Equation(9), for every source vertex, is precisely
the condition <G-G_i,X_i-G_i><=0 for all G in K_rho. Completing the
square identifies the maximizer in(12) with the unique Euclidean
projection of X onto K_rho. Consequently it is G_i at X_i, and(12)
has the values F_i in(8).

To verify smoothness directly, the two projection inequalities at
points X,X' give <X-X',P(X)-P(X')>>=||P(X)-P(X')||^2, where P is
projection onto K_rho. Hence P is1-Lipschitz. The two maximizing
inequalities give

 0<=F_rho(X')-F_rho(X)-<P(X),X'-X>
     <=<P(X')-P(X),X'-X><=||X'-X||^2.

Thus F_rho is differentiable with gradient P, and is convex as a
maximum of affine functions of X. It is finite since K_rho is compact.
Finally 0 in K_rho implies F_rho>=0=F_rho(0). Its true minimizer set
is nonempty; no assertion of uniqueness or normalized payoff is needed.

By(5) and X_4=G_4, the exact primitive energy is

 V_h(rho)=N^2-C^2t^2+rho*(1-C^2t^2q^2).             (13)

This proves the complete all-amplitude test within the explicit family.
If v>0 and tq<1, it is equivalent to testing rho=0 and the upper
endpoint in(11), since(13) is affine in rho. If v=0 or tq>=1, it is
equivalent to both N^2-C^2t^2>=0 and1-C^2t^2q^2>=0. In particular
tq>=1 is impossible for a full certificate on(7), because C>1.
These are statements only about the family(8),(12); no classification
of all legal planar functions or all scalar trajectories is asserted.

## 4. Exact reduced energy in the relevant phase

Suppose0<tq<1 and choose the legal amplitude

 rho=(1+t)/(q(1-tq)).

It remains legal when v=0, even though that case also allows larger
amplitudes. Define

 k=(q*N^2-1/q)/(1+q)
   =u*(N-m/(u-1))
   =u^2+m*u-m-m/(u-1),
 Gred(u,m,C,D)=N^2-k*(1+t)-C^2*t^2,
 N=u+m, t=(C-N-D)/D.                                (14)

To check the formula for k, factor its numerator using
(u-1)N+m=u(N-1) and m+u-1=N-1. Direct expansion of(13) then gives

 V_h(rho)=(1+q)/(1-tq)*Gred(u,m,C,D).                (15)

The factor is positive. No feasibility of a formal covariance matrix
or deletion of an interpolation pair has entered this reduction.

## 5. All old-cut survivors in(3) enter this phase

Assume all cuts(4) hold for a word in(3). Then(6) gives u,v<2, and

 z=C-1-u-w-v > C0-19/3=104/75>1.                    (16)

Set m=1+w,D=1+v,N=u+m. These have1<=m<=7/3 and1<=D<3. The third
cut gives Ct<=N, or equivalently

 N>=fcut(C,D):=C(C-D)/(C+D).                         (17)

Here C>D. The function fcut increases in C and decreases in D,
as follows from

 partial_C fcut=1-2D^2/(C+D)^2>0,
 partial_D fcut=-2C^2/(C+D)^2<0.

Put D0=17/7. If D<=D0, then

 N>=fcut(C0,D0)=89359/22200>4,
 u=N-m>5/3.                                         (18)

If D>=D0, the last condition in(3) becomes

 m<=mline(D):=(31-7D)/6.

On D0<=D<3 we have5/3<mline(D)<=7/3. Furthermore
fcut(C0,D)-mline(D) is strictly increasing in D, since

 C0/(C0+D)<=1351/1776<16/21,
 d/dD [fcut(C0,D)-mline(D)]
      >=7/6-512/441=5/882>0.

Its value at D0 is greater than5/3 by(18). Thus in both cases

 5/3<u<2, 0<q=(u-1)/m<1, 0<t<=N/C<1.               (19)

This proves the required physical phase, including all potential zero
and small values of w or v. It also proves u>1 rather than assuming
away a first step at most1.

## 6. Reduction of the whole region to one rational corner

For5/3<u<2 and1<=m<=7/3, the coefficient k in(14) is positive.
Indeed its derivatives in m and u are

 k_m=u-1-1/(u-1)<0,
 k_u=2u+m+m/(u-1)^2>0,

so its minimum on the closed bounding rectangle is
k(5/3,7/3)=5/6>0. Wherever t>0, differentiation of(14) gives

 partial_C Gred=-k/D-2C*t^2-2C^2*t/D<0,
 partial_D Gred=(k+2C^2*t)*(C-N)/D^2>0,
 partial_m Gred=2N-k_m*(1+t)+k/D+2C^2*t/D>0.         (20)

Let Dhat=max(D,D0), and mhat=mline(Dhat). Conditions(3) imply
m<=mhat, D<=Dhat. The first cut also gives

 C>=Cmin(u):=max(C0,u/(2-u)).

Increasing m and D to mhat and Dhat, and decreasing C to Cmin, in
(20) therefore gives

 Gred(u,m,C,D)<=Gred(u,mline(Dhat),Cmin(u),Dhat).       (21)

All intermediate arguments of(20) have t>0. For example at the
smallest C, largest u and the final m,D one has

 t=(C0-u-31/6)/Dhat+1/6>79/225>0,

using u<2 and Dhat<3; earlier comparison points have larger C-N-D.
The coefficient k stays positive by the rectangle bound above.
The virtual comparison parameters need not satisfy every old cut;
only the proven sign conditions for(20) are used.

For each fixed Dhat in[D0,3], write m=mline(Dhat). The function on
the right of(21) has its maximum in5/3<u<2 at

 U0=2C0/(C0+1)=193/109.                              (22)

Here is a full derivative proof, retaining both sides of the first-cut
boundary. If5/3<u<=U0, then Cmin=C0. Since U0<9/5, the formulas give

 N>10/3, 2/5<t<3/5, k_u<12, C0>7, Dhat<=3.

For the lower t bound, C0-u-31/6>113/150 and thus
t>113/450+1/6=94/225>2/5. The upper bound follows by setting
u=5/3,Dhat=D0 in the decreasing expression
(C0-u-31/6)/Dhat+1/6, yielding226/425<3/5.
For k_u, use
2u+m+m/(u-1)^2<18/5+7/3+21/4=671/60<12.

The derivative of Gred in this interval is

 2N-k_u*(1+t)+k/Dhat+2C0^2*t/Dhat
   >20/3-96/5+196/15=8/15>0.                        (23)

If U0<=u<2, then Cmin=C_u:=u/(2-u). Since U0>7/4,

 C_u'=2/(2-u)^2>32,
 t_u=(C_u'-1)/Dhat>1.

At U0 the value of t exceeds2/5, and it increases thereafter.
Now k,k_u are positive, C_u>7 and N<13/3. The derivative is

 2N-k_u*(1+t)-k*t_u-2C_u*C_u'*t^2-2C_u^2*t*t_u
   <26/3-98/3=-24<0.                               (24)

Equations(23),(24) establish(22), including equality at the boundary.

## 7. The remaining tail comparison, entirely analytically

Fix u=U0,C=C0 and let D range in[D0,3], with m=mline(D). Put

 Astar=C0-U0-31/6=12797/16350, t=Astar/D+1/6,
 Hred(D)=Gred(U0,mline(D),C0,D).

Exact rational comparisons give0<Astar<4/5, U0>7/4,
U0^2<16/5 and C0^2<60. Differentiating, with k_m evaluated at U0,
gives

 Hred'(D)=-7N/3+(7/6)*k_m*(1+t)
                    +Astar*(k+2C0^2*t)/D^2.         (25)

Since k_m<0 and k<U0^2<16/5, while
N>(83-14D)/12, (25) is strictly less than -Fbound(D), where

 Fbound(D)=(581-98D)/36-464/(25D^2)-384/(5D^3).       (26)

For the positive term in(25), the stated estimate follows from

 (4/5)/D^2 *[16/5+120*(4/(5D)+1/6)]
       =464/(25D^2)+384/(5D^3).

The derivative of(26) is

 Fbound'(D)=-49/18+928/(25D^3)+1152/(5D^4)>0,

because D<=3 gives1152/(5D^4)>=128/45>49/18.
Also12/5<D0<5/2, so

 Fbound(D0)>28/3-29/9-50/9=5/9.

Therefore Hred'(D)<-5/9 on the entire closed interval, and its
maximum occurs at D0. This includes the diagonal extension of the
original w<=4/3,v<=10/7 slab; changing v up to the endpoint2 has not
been justified by an unsupported extrapolation of a fixed trajectory.

At the one remaining rational corner, the exact quantities are

 u=193/109, m=7/3, D=17/7, C=193/25,
 N=1342/327<411/100,
 t=67952/138975, 1+t>37/25,
 k=815039/427716>19/10,
 Ct=13114736/3474375>377/100.

Consequently

 Gred < (411/100)^2-(19/10)*(37/25)-(377/100)^2
      =-83/625.                                     (27)

All quantities bounded below in the product k(1+t) are positive.
Combining(21)--(27) proves this same strict bound for every original
word in(3) satisfying all cuts(4).

## 8. Main regional conclusion and remaining scope

For every nonnegative four-step word h=(u,w,z,v) satisfying

 sum h>=168/25, w<=4/3, 6w+7v<=18,

there is no original twenty-nonnegative-multiplier, exact-flow,
full-R5 positive semidefinite primitive certificate.

Indeed, if any cut(4) fails, the explicit one-dimensional function of
Section2 already has V_h<0. Otherwise Section5 gives the precise
legal phase of Section3. Choose rho=(1+t)/(q(1-tq)) in its explicit
static planar function(12). Then(15),(27) yield

 V_h(rho)<-83/625<0,

since(1+q)/(1-tq)>1. Its every original finite and optimum row has
been checked. Equation(2) gives the contradiction to the full original
certificate, without any assumption about a reduced cone.

This is also an empty region for the explicit expanded conjunction
N_pub AND S_scalar AND the correctly scoped nested-cut condition:
that conjunction already contains the cuts(4) and the capped member
of Section3. No implication from a smaller finite list to S_scalar is
asserted or used. The exact published inventory and its conditional
domains are neither weakened nor silently extended.

The result applies at and above the actual sufficient threshold, with
variable first step and without an upper bound on the third step.
It is a complete analytical exclusion of this stated region, not a
new independently claimed test, a perturbative surrogate for a seed,
or a global four-step exclusion. The new phase statement in Section3
only describes the displayed static family.

In particular any surviving full threshold seed must obey

 w>4/3 OR 6w+7v>18.                                 (28)

This complement must still be intersected with the full previous
inventory, including its zero/small-step reductions, every curvature
and value branch, the separate complete proportional-gradient scalar
channel, and all original coefficient and covariance constraints.
There is no exact h,Lambda,M here. No reversal or swapping of the
physical word transfers(28) without an additional proof. No negative
normalized objective error or resolution of the original asymptotic
profile Goal follows.

One analytical round remains after round39 returns within the current
completed ceiling40. All numerical, solver, machine-algebra, replay,
formal-verifier, installation, consult and subagent allowances remain
zero. The original scalar draft's audit and any corrected version must
remain separately identified; this regional proof does not validate it.

# Independent analytical audit of FINAL43a616da7da451c7

and master_guidance78e668c9782fc528. This report audits the final object,
not its preliminary announcement or the author's confidence.

4974 lines, SHA256
`807a671b11f0c1ac1a34af9924f23b13987ad234d62654c7cc9c58684249d88e`.
