# Growing near-unit modes cannot be repaired to benchmark strength by slow modes

Status: complete UNVERIFIED candidate. No new verifier, solver, numerical
probe or round allocation. Accepted inputs:1252b8823bb5a290 (full prefix
interpolation and free completion), dfde2fa326c908bf (algebraic cycle
identity), and b98bb807d514e80d (exact benchmark recurrences). All other
steps needed here are proved in this document.

## 1. A whole joint spectral class on the actual schedule

Fix m>=4096, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), and n=2^m-1.
Keep c,a fixed within this horizon. Remove the original final d_0 step
to form the queried prefix H_m^-, whose length is q=n-1. Let S=sum H_m^-
and let P_i be its chronological polynomials, 0<=i<=q. In particular
the last two executed steps of this retained prefix are d_0,d_1, where
d_1=2-1/m. Its original terminal displacement is x_q-d_0 g_q.

Let 0<R<=1/8 be fixed. Allow ANY finite probability mixture supported on
the union of these two sets:

    Slow_m(R)={z in C: |z|<=R/S},

    Fast_m={1-u/m +/- i sqrt(u-theta)/sqrt(m):
                           5<=u<=6, 1/2<=theta<=1}.       (1)

The words slow and fast refer only to these displayed sets. No component
is presumed legal. For weights w_z>=0 summing to1, use actual real vectors

    x_i=(sqrt(w_z) Re P_i(z), sqrt(w_z) Im P_i(z))_z,
    g_i=(sqrt(w_z) Re[zP_i(z)],sqrt(w_z) Im[zP_i(z)])_z.   (2)

Include the optimum(0,0,0), and allow ANY JOINT values f_i satisfying
ALL ordered interpolation constraints for these same vectors. Let F be
ANY legal convex1-smooth interpolant, including the maximal completion.
Then uniformly for every b in[0,2],

    2F(x_q-b g_q)
       <=exp(2R)[2R/S+(5/4)m R^3/S^3].                 (3)

For R=1/8 this gives a whole-class constant gap from the exact benchmark:

    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) 2F(x_q-b g_q)
                    <=exp(-3/4)<1/2.                  (4)

The supremum over mixtures, dimension, values and b in[0,2] may be taken
inside this limsup. This includes the original final step d_0. It does
not bound W_n for arbitrary gradients or arbitrary normal spectra.

Section5 gives a definite finite mixture from this class, with POSITIVE
weights on individually illegal exponentially growing fast modes, checks
every ordered pair including the optimum, and constructs one fixed F with

    2F(x_q-b g_q)>=3/(64S) for EVERY real b.             (5)

Thus this is also an explicit fixed-prefix, all-final-step witness of
the benchmark's order, with its non-sharp constant loss quantified.

## 2. One triangle made of the last three actual queries

For a scalar parameter z=alpha+i beta set sigma=|z|^2 and
kappa=alpha-sigma. Starting locally at1, the last two steps have positions

    v_0=1, v_1=1-d_0 z, v_2=(1-d_0 z)(1-d_1 z).

The forward triangle0->1->2->0 has signed doubled area

    Im(conj(v_0)v_1+conj(v_1)v_2+conj(v_2)v_0)
                          =-d_0^2 d_1 sigma beta.      (6)

To check it, the first two path-edge areas are -d_0 beta and
-d_1 beta|1-d_0 z|^2, while the return edge contributes
beta(d_0+d_1-2d_0d_1 alpha). Their sum is(6).
The triangle's squared-chord energy is sigma E(z), where

    E(z)=d_0^2+d_1^2|1-d_0 z|^2
                       +|d_0+d_1-d_0d_1 z|^2.         (7)

Hence the REVERSE triangle, by the exact cycle identity in accepted
dfde2fa326c908bf, has weight

    K(z)=sigma[d_0^2 d_1 beta^2-(kappa/2)E(z)].        (8)

Put A(z)=P_(q-2)(z). The same three ACTUAL indices q-2,q-1,q, with
the same reverse orientation, give the JOINT necessary condition

    sum_z w_z |A(z)|^2 K(z)<=0.                        (9)

There is no optimum vertex in this cycle. Nevertheless the optimum
remains imposed elsewhere on the joint prefix. All function values
cancel in(9); no separate component values are chosen in using it.

## 3. Positive fast defects and cubic slow credit

For the parameters in(1), elementary bounds valid for m>=4096 are

    11/8<=d_0<=3/2, 31/16<=d_1<=2,
    1-6/m<=alpha<=1, |beta|<=1/20,
    sigma>=49/50, 0<kappa<=1/m, beta^2>=4/m.           (10)

For kappa, expand it exactly as theta/m-u^2/m^2. For sigma use
sigma>=alpha^2>49/50. The step bounds follow directly from a,c, and
beta^2<=(11/2)/4096<1/400.

Since d_0 alpha>1 and d_1 alpha>1, (10) gives

    |1-d_0z|<=1/2+(3/2)(1/20)<3/5,
    |1-d_1z|<=1+2/20=11/10.

Also 0<d_0+d_1-d_0d_1<=3/4 on the step rectangle in(10), and

    |d_0+d_1-d_0d_1z|
       <=3/4+3(6/m+|beta|)<1.

Consequently E(z)<9/4+4(3/5)^2+1<5. Since
d_0^2d_1>7/2, equations(8),(10) imply

    K(z)>=(49/50)(14-5/2)/m>10/m,
    |(1-d_0 z)(1-d_1 z)|^2<1/2.                       (11)

Thus each fast mode contributes at least(20/m)|P_q(z)|^2 to the
common cycle(9), regardless of its own illegal prefix.

For a slow mode, all prefix polynomials have modulus at most exp(R):
use |1-hz|<=1+h|z| and product(1+h|z|)<=exp(|z|S). In particular
|A(z)|^2<=exp(2R). Since |z|<=1/8, (7) gives

    E(z)<=9/4+4(19/16)^2+(31/8)^2<25.

The negative part of K(z) is at most

    (sigma/2) max(kappa,0) E(z)<= (25/2)|z|^3.

Here max(kappa,0)<=max(alpha,0)<=|z|; no sign assumption on a slow
mode's real part is needed. Its total possible negative contribution
to(9), after summing all slow probabilities, is therefore at most

    (25/2)exp(2R) R^3/S^3.                             (12)

Combining(9),(11),(12) yields the genuinely JOINT endpoint bound

    sum_(fast z) w_z |P_q(z)|^2
                         <=(5/8)m exp(2R)R^3/S^3.    (13)

This is the cubic credit constraint missed by an optimum cycle alone.
It allows positive weights on illegal fast components, but those weights
times their terminal amplification are uniformly controlled.

For any legal interpolant, optimum interpolation at q and final-step
smoothness give, for b in[0,2],

    2F(x_q-b g_q)
       <=sum_z w_z[2alpha-(1+b(2-b))sigma]|P_q(z)|^2.

On fast modes alpha<=1, so(13) bounds their contribution by twice its
right side. On slow modes alpha<=|z|<=R/S and |P_q|<=exp(R), so their
contribution is at most2R exp(2R)/S. This proves(3), with every queried
anchor and the actual last displacement retained.

## 4. Benchmark estimate, and why the fast modes are relevant

The exact recursions from acceptedb98bb807d514e80d have mass joins

    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,
    G(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.

Write rho=log_2 lambda and V(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. For t=exp(s),

    d^2/ds^2 log V(exp(s))
      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2>0.

Thus V(t)V(1/t)>=V(1)^2=2. Substituting t=(A/B)^(1/rho) shows
Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho. Continuity covers zeros.
Induction gives C_k=S_circle(k)+1<=(k+1)^rho. Since G(A,B)<=4A+B,
induction and superadditivity of t^rho give B_k<=1+4k^rho.

The exact retained sum is

    S=2^m-1+a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)-d_0.

Here a/(c-2)->1, c^m/lambda^m->e and (2/lambda)^m->0; hence
S/lambda^m->e. In particular m/S^2->0. Multiply(3) by B_n to obtain
limsup<=8R exp(2R-1), which gives(4) at R=1/8.
The strict inequality exp(-3/4)<1/2 follows from
exp(3/4)>1+3/4+9/32+27/384>2.

Every fast parameter in(1) has exponentially growing ACTUAL queried
polynomial for sufficiently large m, uniformly in its displayed compact
parameter set. Here is the calculation, to avoid appealing to an
unverified earlier counterexample. Put

    L_m(z)=sum_(j>=0)2^(-j-1) log|1-d_j z|,
    A_j=1+1/(a c^j),
    D_m=1+1/[a(2-c^(-1))],
    J_m=1+2/[a(2-c^(-1))]+1/[a^2(2-c^(-2))].

All A_j<=4 for sufficiently large m. For z=1+v in(1), expand
log(1+A_jv)=A_jv-A_j^2v^2/2+O(|v|^3), with remainder uniformly
bounded by2|A_jv|^3 when |A_jv|<=1/2. The weights sum to1, so

    mL_m(z)=-1-uD_m+(u-theta)J_m/2+O(m^(-1/2)).

The limits are D=1+D1, J=1+2D1+D2 with
D1=lambda^2/(2lambda-1), D2=lambda^4/(2lambda^2-1). One has
D1<8/5 and D2>31/10, by 12/5<lambda<5/2 and the increasing functions
t^2/(2t-1), t^4/(2t^2-1) on t>1 (at12/5 the latter is20736/6575>31/10).
The limiting coefficient increases with u because J/2-D=(D2-1)/2>0,
and decreases with theta. Its minimum is therefore

    -1-5D+2J=-4-D1+2D2>3/5.

Thus L_m(z)>=1/(2m) uniformly for large m. The tail j>=m omitted from
the actual finite product, multiplied by2^m, is O(m), since its terms
are bounded above by C+j log c. The removed last factor |1-d_0z| stays
bounded above and away from0. Hence uniformly on Fast_m,

    log|P_q(z)|>=2^m/(3m)

for all sufficiently large m. This is a polynomial-growth statement;
legality is supplied only for joint constructions such as the next one.

## 5. Definite positive weights, every ordered constraint, and one global F

Use R=1/8. Choose exactly two fast modes

    z_fast,1=1-5/m+2i/sqrt(m),
    z_fast,2=1-6/m+i sqrt(5)/sqrt(m).

For every j=0,...,m-1 choose two slow modes

    r_j=d_0/(8S d_j),
    z_(j,ell)=r_j[t_ell+i sqrt(1-t_ell^2)],
    t_1=3/4, t_2=1/2.                                  (14)

These are 2m+2 complex blocks, realized by(2) in R^(4m+4). All slow
moduli are at most1/(8S), with explicit logarithmic physical scales.
Select z_base=z_(0,1), whose parameters are

    r=1/(8S), alpha=3/(32S), sigma=1/(64S^2).

The actual schedule has S>=100. In the next base-mode calculation,
write p_i=P_i(z_base). Every factor contracts because h_i r<=1/8<2t_1.
Hence 7/8<=|p_i|<=1. Define

    f_0^base=3alpha/4,
    f_(i+1)^base=f_i^base
                         -h_i sigma(1-alpha h_i/2)|p_i|^2.

The decrements are nonnegative and total at most sigma S=alpha/6, so

    7alpha/12<=f_i^base<=3alpha/4.                     (15)

For EVERY finite pair i<j, let T=sum_(i<=s<j)h_s. Telescoping the
contracting products gives |p_s-p_i|<=r(s_s-s_i)|p_i|, with s_s-s_i
the intervening step sum. Therefore

    |p_j-p_i|>=rT|p_i|(1-rT/2)>=(15/16)rT|p_i|.       (16)

For the closed path i,...,j,i, its signed area is
A_ij=sigma sum_(i<=s<t<j)h_s h_t Im(conj(p_s)p_t).
Telescoping the difference between a subproduct and its conjugate,
with all remaining factors of modulus at most1, gives
|Im(conj(p_s)p_t)|<=|p_i|^2 |Im z_base| T. Consequently

    |(Im z_base)A_ij|<=sigma^2 T^3|p_i|^2/2.          (17)

The trapezoidal work identity and the scalar edge identity show that
the two ordered pair slacks equal
(kappa/2)|p_j-p_i|^2 +/- (Im z_base)A_ij. Since
kappa>=5alpha/6 and sigma T<=alpha/6, (16)-(17) bound both slacks below by

    [1125/3072-1/12]alpha sigma T^2|p_i|^2
        >(alpha sigma/4)T^2|p_i|^2.

Each h_i>1, so T>1, and |p_i|>=7/8. Thus all ordered finite residuals
are at most -delta, where delta=alpha sigma/8.

The optimum constraints have this margin too. The lower slack is at least
7alpha/12-sigma/2>alpha/2, and the upper slack is at least

    (alpha-sigma/2)(49/64)-3alpha/4
      =alpha[1/64-49/(768S)]>alpha/100>delta.           (18)

This verifies ALL base pairs including the true optimum.

Assign zero temporary values to every non-base mode; these values are
not individually legal. Every mode has |z|<=1. Put M=exp(S), K=4M^2,
epsilon=delta/[2(K+delta)], and give the base weight1-epsilon and
each of the other2m+1 modes weight epsilon/(2m+1). Use actual vectors(2)
and JOINT values f_i=(1-epsilon)f_i^base, f_star=0. All weights are
positive and sum to1, so ||x_0||=1 exactly.

For any non-base mode, |P_i(z)|<=M and |zP_i(z)|<=M, so every ordered
residual with its temporary zero values is at most4M^2=K. Orthogonality
of the blocks makes each joint residual the weighted sum of these
component residuals. Consequently

    Q_ij<=-(1-epsilon)delta+epsilon K=-delta/2<0        (19)

for EVERY distinct ordered finite or optimum pair. All cycles therefore
hold on this same joint data. In particular the positive K(z_fast,l)
triangle weights in(11), which forbid individual legality, are repaired
jointly by the slow data; no modewise legality assumption has slipped in.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 now gives a concrete everywhere finite convex
1-smooth function on R^(4m+4):

    H(v)=max_(i in{star,0,...,q})
      {<g_i,x_i>-f_i+<x_i,v-g_i>+||v-g_i||^2/2},
    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.                            (20)

It interpolates these same vectors and values, with F(0)=min F=0.
This is the full finite-dimensional realization, not a covariance kernel
or a different function selected for each constraint.

## 6. One terminal vector checks EVERY anchor for ALL final b

Let Z be the orthogonal projection of the BASE BLOCK of x_q onto the
line perpendicular to the base block of g_q, with zero coordinates in
all other blocks. Then <Z,g_q>=0 and

    ||Z||^2=(1-epsilon)|p_q|^2(1-t_1^2).

As K>=4 and delta=alpha sigma/8<1, epsilon<1/8. Hence

    ||Z||^2>=(7/8)(49/64)(7/16)=2401/8192>2/7.        (21)

Set w=alpha Z. This is one fixed vector, independent of the final b,
with <w,g_q>=0 and ||w||<=alpha. For the optimum anchor in(20),

    2<x_q,w>-||w||^2=(2alpha-alpha^2)||Z||^2>=alpha/2,

since alpha<=1/4 and(21) holds.

For each finite anchor put y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.
We have ||g_i||^2<=sigma+epsilon M^2<=sigma+delta/8<=2sigma.
Also the base block of x_q-y_i has norm at most rS+r<=101/800,
using S>=100 and contraction. Other blocks do not contribute to its
inner product with w. From(15) and1-epsilon>=7/8,

    2delta_i>=49alpha/48-2sigma.

Thus every finite anchor satisfies

    2delta_i+2<x_q-y_i,w>-||w||^2
       >=alpha[49/48-1/300-101/400-3/3200]>alpha/2.     (22)

Here 2sigma/alpha=1/(3S)<=1/300 and alpha<=3/3200 were used.
Equations(21)-(22) check every actual anchor in(20). For every real b,

    2F(x_q-b g_q)
      >=2<x_q-b g_q,w>-2H(w)
       =min_i{2delta_i+2<x_q-y_i,w>-||w||^2}
       >=alpha/2=3/(64S),

with y_star=delta_star=0. This proves(5) for the SAME fixed function
and prefix, including all nonnegative final steps. The new terminal
gradient is free and need not be a spectral polynomial.

All losses are now explicit. Balanced primitive splits give
C_(2^(m-1)-1)>=lambda^(m-1), C_k increases by the split Omega(C_(k-1),1),
and the dominant split with right length0 gives B_n>=2C_(n-1).
Thus B_n>=2lambda^(m-1), and the constructed family satisfies

    liminf_m B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)>=3/(32lambda e)>0        (23)

uniformly for ALL real b. The whole spectral class nevertheless obeys
the upper bound(4) at every b in[0,2]. The construction has the benchmark
order with the loss(23), not the sharp benchmark constant.

## 7. Exact scope left open

The donor condition |z|<=R/S is essential to the cubic credit estimate.
Mixtures containing intermediate frequencies, including illegal modes
at many larger physical scales, are outside this theorem. Such modes
may supply more triangle credit and must be checked against other common
cycles; a union of individual-mode exclusions would not settle them.
Arbitrary normal mixtures, nonnormal cross-mode models, and the original
all-horizon/all-schedule exact target remain unresolved.

Matlas returned unrelated momentum and Ritz-value statements; none is
used. The exact cycle and all construction estimates are proved above.

## DOC-003116 — project/research/shared_evidence/91446efc5659cb28.md

# Longer seeds: exact ray bounds and a two-cut obstruction

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max2 round35,
the first of the TWO analytical rounds allocated after completed34 by
TASK.md and master_guidance604b16e76c7981e3. The completed ceiling is36,
and the attempted ceiling is37 including the historical interruption.
No numerical evaluation, solver, machine algebra, replay, formal-verifier
call, installation, consult, or subordinate agent was used.

Accepted inputs: fact04219554e41d9750 Sections2,7,10--13 supplies the
benchmark recurrences, balanced primitive recurrence, ray limits and
the FULL original primitive certificate identity; fact1252b8823bb5a290
supplies the smooth interpolation orientation. Everything else below
is proved jointly. No unverified finding is an accepted premise.

The frozen lengths and rays in plan79e9174f0d7d9383 are r=5,b=1 and
r=6,b=1. The original W=2R normalization and all-function target remain
unchanged. This document supplies NO qualifying primitive seed and NO
original-target upper separation. It excludes two concrete nonbasic
candidate families, including open word neighborhoods, by actual fixed
smooth convex functions. Their capacities clear rigorously evaluated
thresholds on precisely the chosen rays.

All new local letters below have the implicit suffix _ls35. The accepted
symbols h,s,A,Q_ij,V_h,Lambda,L_flow,T,M_prim,C_n,B_n,Omega,G,lambda,Z_b
retain their accepted meanings. In particular A=1+sum h throughout.
The scalar primitive prefix matrix T appears only in Sections1--2;
the different scalar Tbar in Sections5--8 is A-R and is never that matrix.

## 1. Original certificate and explicit projection functions

For a nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), put s=sum h_i and A=1+s.
The original formal gradients are g_0,...,g_n, with

 x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)
       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_n||^2.

An original full certificate has ALL n(n+1) off-diagonal multipliers
Lambda_ij>=0, zero diagonal, e=(1,...,1),

 T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
 L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
 L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
 D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)),
 M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0 on R^(n+1).

Accepted04219554e41d9750 Section10 gives the exact identity

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.       (1)

Thus a single actual smooth convex trajectory with V_h<0 excludes every
such array. No necessity or dual-attainment theorem for primitive energy
is needed for this implication. No support or gradient coordinate is
removed, including at a zero step.

We use the following elementary construction and prove its properties.
For a compact convex set K containing0 in a finite-dimensional space, set

 F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                         (2)

Completing the square shows that its unique maximizing g is P_K(x),
the Euclidean projection. Its normal inequality is

 <x-P_K(x),v-P_K(x)><=0 for every v in K.

Adding the normal inequalities at x and y gives
||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>, hence P_K is1-Lipschitz.
The two maximizing inequalities give, for an increment t,

 0<=F_K(x+t)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),t>
       <=<P_K(x+t)-P_K(x),t><=||t||^2.

Therefore F_K is convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with gradient P_K.
It has F_K(0)=0=min F_K. If0 is in the interior of K, any x!=0 admits
a sufficiently small positive multiple epsilon*x in K; substituting it
in (2) proves F_K(x)>0, so0 is the unique minimizer.

At a projection transcript put y_i=x_i-g_i. Its values are exactly
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, and direct substitution gives

 Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                                        (3)

Notice the normal is indexed by j, not i. Every ordered row, including
the optimum sample x_*=g_*=f_*=0, follows from the displayed normal
inequality. In particular Q_(i,*)=0.

For ANY trajectory of (2), the distance telescope gives

 2sum h_i f_i=||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2
                       +sum h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2,
 V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+s g_n||^2.                             (4)

The identity uses actual values and gradients. It holds even when the
last point is outside K or a step is zero.

## 2. Two distinct tight global cuts prohibit a full certificate

For this section assume every physical step is strictly positive. Define
S_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, P_j=1+S_j and D_j=1+s-S_(j+1). For h_j>1 the interval
K=[-eta,1], eta=(h_j-1)/D_j, and initial point x_0=P_j give the following
exact projection trajectory: gradients1 through query j, followed by
gradients -eta through query n. Query j is1 and query n is -eta.
Indeed the bridge lands at -(h_j-1)=-D_j eta, and every subsequent
positive step moves toward -eta while staying on the negative plateau.
Equation(4) gives

 V_h=P_j^2-A^2(h_j-1)^2/D_j^2.

Consequently every full certificate satisfies

 A(h_j-1)_+ <= P_j D_j.                                    (5)

Call j a tight global cut if h_j>1 and equality holds in (5).
The following assertion concerns full certificates, not an unproved
equivalence between primitive functions and dual certificates:

 A strictly positive word with an original full certificate has
 AT MOST ONE tight global cut.                              (6)

Here is a full-coordinate proof. A projection interval containing a
positive plateau, with initial point1+s, produces all gradients equal
to1 and V_h=Q_ij=0. Equation(1) and PSD imply M_prim e=0.
Expanding its i-th row for i<n gives

 sum_l Lambda_(i,l)(S_l-S_i)=2h_i(1+S_i).                   (7)

For completeness, T e has entries1+S_i. At a nonterminal index i,
(T^T L_flow^T e)_i=-2h_i+2h_i S_(i+1). Combining half this quantity
with D_prim,ii gives h_i(1+S_i), which proves (7).

At a tight cut j the interval trajectory above has V_h=0. Its gradient
column and e span the two block indicators, so the indicator p of
{0,...,j} is in ker M_prim. Complementarity follows directly from(1):
the left side is a sum of nonpositive Lambda_ij Q_ij and the right side
is nonnegative, so each strict negative Q forces its multiplier to0.
Let the two query blocks be I={0,...,j}, J={j+1,...,n}. Formula(3) gives

 Q_(i,l)=-(1+eta)eta sum_(t=l)^(n-1)h_t, i in I,l in J,
 Q_(l,i)=-(1+eta)sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t, l in J,i in I.

Strict positivity of the steps therefore allows cross edges only
I->n and J->j. All other cross multipliers are0 as a consequence,
not an imposed sparsity assumption.

At the terminal row of M_prim p=0, direct use of its full matrix gives

 (h_j-1)Lambda_(n,j)=sum_(i<=j)Lambda_(i,n).                 (8)

In fact (Tp)_n=S_(j+1), (Tp)_j=1+S_j, all other possible row-n targets
within J have the same Tp value, and (T^T L_flow^T p)_n is minus the
sum on the right. Its D_prim term is0.

If Lambda_(n,j)=0, (8) and nonnegativity remove EVERY edge from I to J.
The row i=j of(7) would then have a nonpositive left side, because all
remaining targets have S_l<=S_j, and the strictly positive right side
2h_j(1+S_j). This is impossible. Thus Lambda_(n,j)>0.

If k>j were another tight cut, its own exact interval transcript has

 Q_(n,j)=-(1+eta_k)sum_(t=j)^(k-1)h_t<0.

Complementarity at that second cut forces Lambda_(n,j)=0, a contradiction.
This proves(6) without deleting a formal coordinate or fixing a remainder.
No version of(6) for zero-step words is used below; the explicit negative
functions in Sections5--8 also handle the zero-step endpoints we state.

## 3. Exact bounds for the frozen rays

We now bound the actual ratios Z_6/Z_1 and Z_7/Z_1. These are not the
four-step193/25 threshold and do not optimize a continuous phase.

We first prove the sharper uniform scalar bound

 B_n<=lambda C_n, hence Z_b<=lambda C_(b-1) for integer b>=1. (9)

For a,b>=0 let w=Omega(a,b). If w=0 the claim below is immediate. For
w>0 write t=a/w in[0,1]; its defining polynomial implies
b/w=(1-t)/(1+t). The polynomial whose larger root is G(a,lambda b) is

 P(v)=v^2-(4a+lambda b)v+4a^2.

Using lambda^2=2lambda+1 gives the exact identity

 P(lambda w)=4a w (t-1/sqrt(2))^2/(1+t)>=0.

Also lambda w>=lambda(a+b)>=2a+lambda b/2, the polynomial's vertex.
Therefore G(a,lambda b)<=lambda Omega(a,b). The a=0 boundary is included.
Induction in the ORIGINAL maximizing B recurrence now proves B_n<=lambda C_n.
Accepted04219554e41d9750 Section2 gives
C_(b*2^j-1)=lambda^j C_(b-1); pass to the accepted ray limit to prove(9).

We need a rigorous lower bound on Z_1. Define d_0=1 and

 d_(j+1)=G(1,d_j)/lambda.

These are normalized masses of a particular admissible balanced tree,
so B_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j d_j. The map is increasing, d_1>d_0, and the
scalar inequality just proved gives d_j<=lambda. Thus d_j increases to
a limit d_infty>=1 satisfying lambda d_infty=G(1,d_infty).
Its squared equation is

 (lambda+1)d_infty^2-4lambda d_infty+4=0.

This upward quadratic has one root below1 and one above1, since its
value at1 is5-3lambda<0. The limit is the latter root. At58/25 its
value is(5864-2436lambda)/625<0; lambda>241/100 proves the strict sign.
Consequently d_infty>58/25.

At horizon2^j-1 use in the original B recurrence a primitive left child
with3*2^(j-2) leaves and the displayed dominant right child with2^(j-2)
leaves. Homogeneity and then a limit give

 Z_1 >= G(C_2,d_infty)/lambda^2
      > G(C_2,58/25)/lambda^2.                             (10)

The following bounds require only fixed rational comparisons:

 803/200<C_2<502/125,       lambda^2<583/100.

To check them, C_2 is the positive root of
X^2-(1+lambda)X-lambda=0, and
239/99<lambda<169/70 follows from140/99<sqrt(2)<99/70.
At the lower proposed endpoint the needed comparison is
239*200600-99*484209=6709>0.
At the upper endpoint it is70*189254-169*78375=2405>0.
Finally lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2)<204/35<583/100.

Direct radical evaluation, followed by one rational square comparison,
gives

 G(803/200,58/25)=919/100+sqrt(49938)/50 >1707/125,
 100*49938-2233^2=7511>0,
 1707/125-(1171/500)(583/100)=107/50000>0.

Equations(10) and these inequalities prove

 Z_1>1171/500>7/3.                                        (11)

Balanced primitive capacities give C_5=lambda C_2 and
C_6=Omega(C_2,lambda^2). Thus(9)--(11) imply

 Z_6/Z_1 < (583/100)(502/125)/(1171/500)
          =292666/29275<10.                               (12)

For the second ray, C_6<12: substituting12 in the defining polynomial
of Omega(502/125,583/100) gives30434/12500>0, and12 exceeds the
polynomial's vertex. Therefore

 Z_7<lambda C_6<30,    Z_7/Z_1<90/7<13.                   (13)

The strict relative margins supplied by these enclosures are

 (Z_6/Z_1)/10 <1-42/146375,
 (Z_7/Z_1)/13 <1-1/91.                                   (14)

They refer to b=1 exactly. Under the accepted physical substitution a
qualifying length5 seed would have actual horizon6(2^j-1), and a
qualifying length6 seed would have actual horizon7(2^j-1). Section13
of the accepted fact gives the limit, with the untouched dominant leaf
paid there. No such qualifying seed is asserted here, and the excluded
words below cannot be used in that implication.

## 4. Explicit nonbasic candidate words

The attempted five-step word is

 h5=(3/2,5/2,1,5/2,3/2),             A5=10.                (15)

The attempted six-step family is

 h6(c)=(3/2,167/62,c,112/31-c,167/62,3/2),
 0<=c<=112/31,                       A6=13.               (16)

The specific interior representative is c=1, so its fourth step is81/31.
Every member of(16) uses its actual six-step horizon, including c=0
and c=112/31. None is relabeled as a shorter word.

The first long step and its matching late long step have exactly tight
global cuts: indices1,3 in(15) and indices1,4 in(16). The endpoint
cut inequalities are strict. In(15) the unit step has zero left side
in(5), so every scalar cut passes. In the c=1 member of(16), the unit
step again gives zero; at its remaining step81/31 the right minus left
side of(5) is

 (192/31)(161/31)-13(50/31)=10762/961>0.

The endpoint slack is5 in this six-step word. In both selected words
the unit-curvature terminal product product_i(1-h_i) is0 because a
physical unit step is present. Thus neither word is discarded by the
complete elementary scalar-cut list or the full-curvature product test.
No assertion is made about every other scalar-function test.

Their capacities are above the correct thresholds by(12)--(14), and
above the basic primitive maxima C_5<10 and C_6<12<13. They therefore
are not evidence of a capacity gain from ordinary primitive composition.
Section2 already excludes all strictly positive two-cut members from
the full certificate cone. The next sections strengthen that exclusion
to explicit negative actual functions and open word neighborhoods.

## 5. One triangle construction for both physical lengths

Use the following two exact parameter sets:

 A   a    R     P       Tbar    b        H       gamma   mu      nu
 10  3/2  5/2   4       15/2    5/2      1       1/5     3/8     2/3
 13  3/2  5/2   130/31  21/2    167/62   112/31  5/31    21/52   16/21

Here R=1+a, P=a+b, Tbar=A-R, H is the total of the middle steps, and

 d=b-1=P-R,
 P=2AR/(A+R),
 gamma=d/Tbar=P/(2A)=R/(A+R),
 mu=d/P=Tbar/(2A),
 nu=(Tbar-R)/Tbar.

For A=10 the middle block consists of the single step1. For A=13 it
consists of the two steps c,H-c. In both cases the physical word is
(a,b,middle,b,a). The symbol d here is a bridge excess, not a horizon.

Choose any0<epsilon<=1/20 and define planar vectors

 u=(1,0), v=(-epsilon,1),
 w=(-gamma+nu*epsilon,
       -mu-gamma*epsilon+(nu-mu)*epsilon^2),
 p=w+u+v, q=w+v, K=conv{p,q,w}.                            (17)

In particular

 <w,u>=-gamma+nu*epsilon,
 <w,v>=-mu(1+epsilon^2),
 <u,v>=-epsilon, ||u||^2=1, ||v||^2=1+epsilon^2.            (18)

The single function is F=F_K from(2), with initial point

 X_0=A w+P u+Tbar v.                                     (19)

It depends on A and epsilon. For A=13 it is independent of c.

To verify the minimum, put
beta=mu+gamma*epsilon-(nu-mu)*epsilon^2 and
alpha=gamma-(nu+beta)*epsilon. Then w=-alpha u-beta v.
Both parameter sets obey

 5/31<=gamma<=1/5,
 3/8<=mu<=21/52,
 2/3<=nu<=16/21,
 0<nu-mu<1.

For epsilon<=1/20 these imply

 mu<=beta<=21/52+1/100<1/2,
 alpha>=5/31-53/840=2557/26040>0,
 alpha<=1/5<3/8<=beta.

Thus0=alpha*p+(beta-alpha)*q+(1-beta)*w is a strictly positive
convex combination. Since u,v are independent, K is a full triangle
and0 is in its interior. Section1 proves F has unique minimizer0.
Also X_0 has positive second coordinate Tbar-A beta>A/2-R>0, so the
initial distance is genuinely nonzero.

## 6. Every physical query and every directed interpolation row

The prescribed gradient pattern is

 h5:    p,p,q,q,w,w;
 h6(c): p,p,q,q,q,w,w.                                   (20)

Starting at(19), use these vectors in the original updates. Their
weighted sum is P p+(H+b)q+a w. Since A=P+(H+b)+R,
Tbar=P+H+b, the terminal point is exactly w.

Let k be the second long-step index: k=3 or4. The normals X_i-G_i
at the two p queries are

 Y_1=Tbar*w+d*u+(Tbar-R)*v,
 Y_0=Y_1+a*p.

At the final q query and all earlier q queries they are

 Y_k=P*w+d*v,
 Y_i=Y_k+tau_i*q, where0<=tau_i<=H.

Here tau_i is the sum of middle steps remaining before query k. For
six steps it is H,H-c,0 at indices2,3,4. For five steps it is1,0.
The two w-query normals are a*w and0.

We now check the entire normal cone, not just adjacent interpolation.
For the p cone it suffices that a normal have nonnegative inner
products with u and u+v. For the q cone the conditions are inner
product with u<=0 and with v>=0. For the w cone the conditions are
inner product with v<=0 and with u+v<=0.

Using(18),

 <Y_1,u>=0,
 <Y_1,v>=Kcoef*(1+epsilon^2)-d*epsilon>0,

where Kcoef=35/16 for A=10 and391/104 for A=13. Both Kcoef>2,
while d<2 and epsilon<=1/20. Also

 <p,u>=1-gamma-(1-nu)epsilon>0,
 <p,v>=(1-mu)(1+epsilon^2)-epsilon>0.

Therefore Y_0 and Y_1 are both in the full normal cone at p.
For the last q query,

 <Y_k,v>=0,
 <Y_k,u>=-P*gamma+R*(Tbar-P)*epsilon/Tbar<0.

Indeed P>=4, gamma>=5/31, 0<Tbar-P<Tbar, R=5/2 and epsilon<=1/20
bound the last expression by -20/31+1/8<0. Furthermore

 <q,u>=-gamma-(1-nu)epsilon<0,
 <q,v>=(1-mu)(1+epsilon^2)>0.

Thus Y_k+tau_i q is in the full q cone for EVERY allowed tau_i,
including zero middle steps. Finally both <w,u> and <w,v> are negative,
so a*w is in the full w cone, and the zero normal is in it as well.

These checks prove G_i=P_K(X_i) at EVERY original query. They give a
single actual static function with the exact GD updates in(20).
Formula(3) now proves Q_ij<=0 for ALL ordered indices i,j in
{*,0,...,n}, not only adjacent or chosen support pairs. The exact values
are F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2; no independent repricing is involved.

## 7. Strict negative primitive energy

The terminal point and gradient both equal w. Put z=<u,v>=-epsilon.
Expanding(19) and using(18) in(4) yields

 V_h=||A w+P u+Tbar v||^2-A^2||w||^2
    =(P^2-2AP gamma)||u||^2
       +(Tbar^2-2A Tbar mu)||v||^2
       +2P(Tbar-A nu)z
    =2P R^2 z/Tbar.

Here P=2A gamma, Tbar=2A mu, and Tbar-A nu=R^2/Tbar.
Consequently the exact energies are

 V_h5=-(20/3)epsilon,
 V_h6(c)=-(3250/651)epsilon for EVERY0<=c<=112/31.          (21)

Thus neither family admits an original full certificate: substituting
these actual gradients into(1) would make its left side strictly negative
and its right side nonnegative. This excludes ALL30 multipliers and the
full R^6 matrix for h5, and ALL42 multipliers and the full R^7 matrix
for h6(c). The zero-step endpoints in the latter assertion impose no
equality between formal gradients; one actual negative transcript is
enough to contradict any proposed PSD matrix on the full formal space.

With epsilon=1/100 the two exact negative values are

 -1/15 and -65/1302.                                     (22)

The true minimum and every original query remain as checked above.
This is a primitive-energy obstruction. It is not an objective error
upper bound and does not decide the unrestricted minimax profile.

## 8. Fixed functions exclude open boxes above the same thresholds

Fix epsilon=1/100. For each of the two base words h5 and h6(1), keep
the SAME triangle function F and SAME initial point X_0 from(17)--(19).
Set eta_box=1/100000000. For any physical word h' of the SAME length
satisfying max_i |h'_i-h_i|<=eta_box, we claim

 V_(h')(F,X_0)<-1/30.                                    (23)

Every such step remains positive. The coordinate bounds in Section5
give ||w||<1 and ||p||,||q||<2; hence ||grad F(x)||<2 for every x.
The gradient is also1-Lipschitz. Every base step is<3. If e_i denotes
the distance between the two actual iterates, their updates therefore
give e_0=0 and

 e_(i+1)<=4e_i+2eta_box,
 e_i<=2eta_box(4^i-1)/3<3000eta_box for i<=6.

Write s and s' for the two step sums, and let
z=X_n+s G_n=A w and z'=X'_n+s' G'_n. Then

 ||z'-z||<=(1+s)e_n+2|s'-s|
          <40000eta_box=1/2500,
 ||z||<13.

Equation(4) holds for the perturbed trajectory as well, even though its
terminal point need not equal its gradient. Consequently

 |V_(h')-V_h|<27/2500.

The exact inequalities
1/15-27/2500>1/30 and65/1302-27/2500>1/30 prove(23).
For the second one,65/1302-1/30=18/1085 and
18*2500>27*1085. Thus EACH complete box is excluded by ONE common
static smooth convex function, not merely by word-dependent witnesses.

All capacities in these two boxes still lie strictly above their
correct fixed-ray thresholds:

 10-5eta_box >292666/29275>Z_6/Z_1,
 13-6eta_box >90/7>Z_7/Z_1.

The first comparison follows from10-292666/29275=84/29275;
the second from13-90/7=1/7. Thus the failure is not an artifact of
being below the requisite asymptotic capacity threshold.

## 9. What changed, what is closed, and the next interface

The old full-primitive reports650089feeda25479 and504a69a1e000d6aa
described bounded numerical failures, including one length5 search.
They did not supply the explicit families or exact exclusions above.
Earlier saturated-cut drafts, including e59fe188876aa98d and
f7cee3034df8cb7f, already derived a much fuller single-cut multiplier
extraction. Accordingly Section2 is identified as a short consequence
of that mechanism, re-proved here rather than advertised as a new
single-cut theorem or used as an unverified premise.

The new work is the joint package: fixed length5/6 and b=1 thresholds;
nonbasic candidate words above those thresholds; a concrete negative
tilt of their two-cut triangle; every original query, actual minimum,
all ordered rows and exact negative energies; an entire split-middle
six-step family; and quantitative full-dimensional neighborhoods
excluded by one fixed function per box. It is not another numerical
non-discovery, a basic composition, padding or reversal argument.

The attempted mechanism tried to allocate excess mass to two separated
long steps while keeping both global cuts tight and killing the
unit-curvature terminal product with a unit step. This is now CLOSED
for the specified families and boxes. Two scalar equality witnesses
cannot be assumed compatible with one full certificate: the terminal
multiplier demanded by the earlier cut is forbidden by the later one,
and the tilted triangle makes the resulting energy deficit explicit.

What remains open is an actual full certificate for a different
length5/6 word above the corresponding threshold, in particular a
family with strict cut slack outside these excluded neighborhoods.
The present box size is deliberately conservative and is not a global
capacity upper bound. There is no proof that every nonbasic seed has
two tight cuts, is symmetric, or belongs to a triangle ansatz.

The next allocated round must not retune the now-excluded two-tight-cut
family, repeat a finite scan, transfer the four-step193/25 threshold, or
reprove the general conditional lift as its deliverable. A different
full-flow family with at most one tight global cut, or a precise new
strict-slack certificate mechanism, remains a legitimate next search.

Primary-source retrieval: Matlas was queried for primitive interpolation
certificates and returned unrelated statements, not matched support.
The local primary text of Zhang--Jiang arXiv:2410.12395v2 was read at
Definition2.7, Proposition2.8 and the proof of Theorem3.1 to check the
energy convention and the distinction from ordinary composition.
The proof above imports no additional theorem from that paper.
The balanced scalar result is used through accepted04219554e41d9750,
in its exact stated scope. No further external black box is used.

This is an UNVERIFIED research handoff after the FIRST assigned round.
It is not a formal submission, publication, target closure, loop-exit
claim, resource renewal, or use of the second round in advance.

# Exact sublevel state for joins that raise the minimum

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof and counterexample, worker max, 2026-09-26.
No verification call is allocated. This does not assert the unrestricted
Zhang--Jiang comparison. It extends the zero-preserving state test rather
than contradicting its explicitly restricted sufficiency statement.

## 1. What the zero-level profile loses

For a finite convex polyhedral function J on a Euclidean space, write
env J(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2]. All functions below have an attained
minimum. The unique minimizing z is the proximal point. If x-z is the
gradient of an affine plane active in J at z, the subgradient inequality
and the strict quadratic term show that z is the unique proximal point.
Moreover env J is convex, differentiable, and has 1-Lipschitz gradient
x-z. One direct proof of the Lipschitz assertion adds the two proximal
subgradient inequalities, obtaining
||g(x)-g(x')||^2 <= <g(x)-g(x'),x-x'>.
The minimizing inequalities give differentiability with this gradient.
Finally min env J=min J and their minimizer sets coincide: equality in
J(z)+||x-z||^2/2 >= min J requires x=z and J(z)=min J.

Consider the two functions on coordinates (t,y)

    J_A(t,y)=max(0,1-t,-y),
    J_B(t,y)=max(0,1-t,-y,3(1-t-y)/4).

Both have precisely the zero set {t>=1,y>=0}. Start at x_init=(-1,-7)
and take the one-step prefix h=(7). Both functions have the same legal
proximal samples:

    query x_0=(-1,-7), proximal z_0=(-1,-6), height V_0=6,
    gradient g_0=(0,-1);
    query x_1=(-1,0), proximal p=(0,0), height V_1=1,
    gradient g_1=(-1,0).

At z_0 the values of (0,1-t,-y,3(1-t-y)/4) are (0,2,6,6).
At p they are (0,1,0,3/4). Thus the selected gradients really are
active-plane subgradients for both functions. The GD equality
x_0-7g_0=x_1 holds. Set u=(1,0), so the same boundary gradient is
-m_boundary u with m_boundary=1.

The entire proposed state, including the added gradient magnitude, agrees:

    Phi(t)=max(0,1-t),
    Psi(s)=min(1,6+s),
    Dist(t)=(t+1)^2+49 for t>=1, and +infinity otherwise.       (1)

Here Phi(t)=J(p+tu), Psi(s)=min_i[V_i-s<u,z_i-p>], and Dist is
the squared distance from the same x_init to the zero slice at t.
In fact all prefix queries, gradients, heights, initial points, boundary
points, and zero sets agree, a stronger equality than the state requires.

Append the identical fresh-coordinate cell in a new coordinate w. Choose
bridge b=3/2, scale d=1/2, cap c=1, endpoint epsilon=1/2, local initial
query (epsilon,-1), and no ordinary steps. Its center, common plane
height, and two planes are

    p_new=(1/4,0,1/2),   F_cell=13/16,
    C(t,y,w)=11/16+t/2,
    E(t,y,w)=1+t/4-w/2.

These are exactly F_cell+d<t-axis,z-p_new> and
F_cell+d<(epsilon,0,-1),z-p_new>. For T=A,B define

    Jbar_T=max(J_T,C,E).

The old query x_1 is sent by the prescribed bridge to x_2=(1/2,0,0).
At p_new the two new planes both equal 13/16, while both old functions
equal 3/4. Therefore g_2=(1/4,0,-1/2) is an active normal and
x_2=p_new+g_2. The new planes at the two old proximal points have values

    at z_0=(-1,-6,0): C=3/16, E=3/4, both <=6;
    at p=(0,0,0):     C=11/16, E=1, both <=1.

This checks both directions of every cross-block comparison. All gradients
and all GD iterates are actual gradients and iterates of env Jbar_T.

Nevertheless neither Jbar_T has a zero. Indeed its minimum is

    mu=19/24,

because its planes 1-t and 11/16+t/2 have minimum possible maximum
19/24, attained only when t=5/24. At that t the endpoint constraint is
w>=25/48. The remaining minimum-set constraints are exactly

    argmin Jbar_A={t=5/24, y>=-19/24, w>=25/48},
    argmin Jbar_B={t=5/24, y>=-19/72, w>=25/48}.              (2)

For B, the extra inequality is
(3/4)(19/24-y)<=19/24, equivalent to y>=-19/72; it dominates
y>=-19/24. Every displayed point satisfies all the other planes, proving
both attainment and the complete descriptions in (2).

The actual closest minimizers to (-1,-7,0) are thus the respective corners,
and their squared distances are

    R_A^2=(29/24)^2+(149/24)^2+(25/48)^2=30931/768,
    R_B^2=(29/24)^2+(485/72)^2+(25/48)^2=976801/20736.       (3)

Both have F_cell-mu=1/48>0 and ||g_2||^2=5/16. Their normalized last
errors for the identical schedule (7,3/2) are therefore different:

    2(env Jbar_A(x_2)-mu)/R_A^2 =272/30931,
    2(env Jbar_B(x_2)-mu)/R_B^2 =7344/976801.                (4)

Subtracting the same mu from the WHOLE respective functions normalizes
their minima to zero and changes none of their GD iterates. Every retained
past function value is reduced by that same constant. This is not an
independent reset of a checkpoint value.

Conclusion: Phi,Psi,Dist and m_boundary cannot determine the true distance
normalization of a minimum-raising join for general allowed polyhedral
histories. The previous zero-preserving rule correctly rejects this join;
its restricted sufficiency is unaffected. The stronger rule that accepts
all legal joins needs more information than the zero slice.

## 2. The missing information can be written explicitly

Define the two-parameter sublevel distance profile

    Xi(t,r)=min{||x_init-z||^2:J(z)<=r, <u,z-p>=t},          (5)

with value +infinity when the set is empty. Extend it by +infinity for
r<0 when min J=0. The minimum exists whenever feasible: the feasible
set is closed and the squared norm is coercive.

For the preceding two histories, for r>=0 and t>=1-r the exact profiles
are

    Xi_A(t,r)=(t+1)^2+max(0,7-r)^2,
    Xi_B(t,r)=(t+1)^2+max(0,7-r,8-t-4r/3)^2.              (6)

They are +infinity outside that domain. These formulas follow by projecting
the initial y-coordinate -7 onto y>=-r for A and onto
y>=max(-r,1-t-4r/3) for B. At r=0 both reduce to (1), while at the
actual raised minimum r=19/24 and t=5/24 they give the two different
old-coordinate costs in (3).

## 3. Exact finite update with global recentering allowed

Take any old J_old=max(0,L_1,...,L_M) with min J_old=0. Let its entire
old ambient space contain x_init, p, u, every old normal and every old
proximal point z_i. The retained samples have heights V_i=J_old(z_i),
and the boundary query is p-m_boundary u, m_boundary>0. Retain Phi,
Psi as in (1) and Xi as in (5). No assumption about a small state size
is made.

Specify finite new-cell normals (a,-q), a>0, 0<=q<=1, including a single
q=0 cap (c,0) and endpoint (epsilon,-1). The local queries (u_j,-y_j)
and selected normals (a_j,-q_j) obey their prescribed GD differences and
all local support inequalities, with terminal query and selected normal
(epsilon,-1). Write

    U_j=u_j-a_j,
    h_j=< (a_j,-q_j),(U_j,-(y_j-q_j)) > >=0,
    r_0=(R_x,-R_y),   lambda=sqrt(1+epsilon^2).

A supplied bridge b>1 and scale d>0 determine

    theta=(b-1)m_boundary-d R_x.

As in the current joining geometry require theta>=0. Choose F_cell>0,
and a new unit coordinate e orthogonal to the entire old ambient. Put

    p_new=p+theta u+d R_y e,
    L_(a,q)=F_cell+d<a u-qe,z-p_new>,
    Jbar=max(J_old,all L_(a,q)),
    u_new=(-epsilon u+e)/lambda,
    m_new=d lambda.

The new query/proximal data are

    x_j=p_new+d(u_j u-y_j e),
    g_j=d(a_j u-q_j e),
    z_j=p_new+d(U_j u-(y_j-q_j)e),
    V_j=F_cell+d^2 h_j.

The complete two-direction interpolation test is exactly

    max_j[Phi(theta+d U_j)-d^2 h_j] <= F_cell
       <= min_(a,q)[Psi(d a)+d a theta-d^2 R_y q].         (7)

An old plane at a new z_j depends only on its old projection
p+(theta+d U_j)u, giving the left side. A new plane at an old z_i is
F_cell+d a(<u,z_i-p>-theta)+d^2 R_y q, giving the right side.
Together with the supplied local inequalities this checks every pair.
It gives the proximal data for the same single Jbar, without requiring
that Jbar still have a zero.

The unrecentered outgoing and acceptance profiles are

    Phibar(tau)=max{Phi(theta-epsilon tau/lambda),
                     F_cell+max_(a,q)[-d(epsilon a+q)tau/lambda]},

    Psibar(s)=min{Psi(-epsilon s/lambda)
                            +s(d R_y-epsilon theta)/lambda,
                 min_j[F_cell+d^2h_j
                            +s d(epsilon U_j+y_j-q_j)/lambda]}. (8)

For each real r set

    Z_r(t)=max_(a,q),q>0[d R_y+(F_cell-r)/(d q)
                                      +(a/q)(t-theta)],
    w(t,tau)=lambda tau+d R_y+epsilon(t-theta).

The exact sublevel-distance update is

    Xibar(tau,r)=inf_t {Xi(t,r)+w(t,tau)^2:
        t<=theta+(r-F_cell)/(d c), w(t,tau)>=Z_r(t)}.       (9)

This follows by writing an enlarged point as z=z_old+w e. Its old
coordinate t=<u,z_old-p> must obey J_old(z_old)<=r. The cap gives the
upper bound on t, each positive-q plane gives one lower bound on w,
and fixing the new outgoing slice determines w=w(t,tau). Orthogonality
to x_init as well as the entire old ambient gives the squared-distance
sum. Conversely an attaining old point for Xi(t,r), with that appended
w, realizes exactly the displayed cost. Thus (9) is an equality, including
empty cases; finite minima on fixed new slices are attained by closedness
and coercivity.

Since Jbar is the maximum of finitely many affine functions, includes zero,
and is finite, its minimum mu>=0 is attained. One elementary way to see
attainment is to project its nonempty epigraph polyhedron onto the value
coordinate; Fourier--Motzkin elimination gives a polyhedron in R, and
the projection is closed and bounded below. Equivalently, using (9),

    mu=min{r>=0: Xibar(tau,r)<infinity for some tau}.       (10)

Now recenter the WHOLE function by J_new=Jbar-mu. Its exact new state is

    Phi_new=Phibar-mu,  Psi_new=Psibar-mu,
    Xi_new(tau,r)=Xibar(tau,r+mu),  r>=0.                 (11)

All old and new proximal heights are reduced by the same mu; gradients
and iterates do not change. As Jbar>=mu everywhere, inserting the zero
plane into the maximum of all shifted planes leaves J_new unchanged.
The boundary gradient magnitude remains m_new. In particular the exact
normalized endpoint error is

    [2(F_cell-mu)+d^2(1+epsilon^2)] / min_tau Xibar(tau,mu). (12)

The denominator is the distance to an actual closest minimizer of the
same function whose gradients realize the entire supplied history.

This is a finite rule. Xi is a convex extended-real piecewise quadratic
function of (t,r): fix any active face of the linearly parameterized
polyhedron defining (5); its equality-constrained Euclidean projection
is affine in (t,r), and its squared distance is quadratic. Primal and
normal-cone conditions select polyhedral parameter regions. There are
finitely many active subsets; if a subset is dependent, retain a basis
for the affine hull and describe its normal cone with the full active
set. This does not require nondegeneracy. On every piece, (9) minimizes
a quadratic in one scalar t over linear inequalities in (t,tau,r).
The positive coefficient from the fixed t-slice squared distance permits
the unconstrained affine vertex to be clipped to the feasible interval.
Checking the finitely many pieces gives another finite piecewise quadratic
profile. No solver or numerical sweep is part of the construction.

## 4. In the arc-chain regime the amount of recentering is scalar

There is a useful exact consequence when the old zero-slice domain is
[F_old/m_boundary,infinity), where F_old=J_old(p)>0. Assume also that
-m_boundary u is an active old boundary normal. For every
0<=r<=F_old,

    {t:Xi(t,r)<infinity}=[(F_old-r)/m_boundary,infinity).   (13)

The supporting plane gives the lower bound. If r<F_old and t is at
least that bound, take a zero point with outgoing coordinate
t_zero=t F_old/(F_old-r), and form the convex combination with p whose
weight on that zero point is (F_old-r)/F_old. Its coordinate is t and
its value is at most r. If r=F_old, use p when t=0, and for t>0 mix p
with any zero point at a coordinate at least max(t,F_old/m_boundary).
This proves every claimed feasible slice without using a distance bound.

Assume the cross-tests (7) hold. Let R_cap=d c. Then the actual minimum
in (10) is the explicit value

    mu=max(0,[R_cap F_old+m_boundary F_cell
                  -m_boundary R_cap theta]/[m_boundary+R_cap]). (14)

To prove it, for any r<=min(F_old,F_cell) the old sublevel is feasible
at exactly the t in (13). The new cap requires
t<=theta-(F_cell-r)/R_cap. Every positive-q plane can be satisfied
by making w sufficiently large. Thus feasibility is equivalent to

    (F_old-r)/m_boundary+(F_cell-r)/R_cap <= theta.

The displayed root is no greater than F_old because the new cap at p
is at most F_old. It is no greater than F_cell because the old boundary
plane at p_new is at most F_cell. These are included in (7), so the
calculation applies at the claimed nonnegative minimum itself. This
proves both the lower bound and attainment in (14).

For a concave-arc cell satisfying

    a-epsilon q>=0,
    (a-epsilon q)/c+q>=1,

the endpoint lower bound on w dominates every other positive-q bound
on the cap-feasible range whenever r<=F_cell. Subtract the endpoint
bound from a general bound and use
t-theta<=-(F_cell-r)/(d c); the result is at most
(F_cell-r)/(d q)[1-q-(a-epsilon q)/c]<=0.
Consequently the new zero-slice domain after (11) is exactly
[(F_cell-mu)/(d lambda),infinity). The domain hypothesis used in
(13) is therefore preserved by these joins, even when mu>0.

In particular a positive minimum does not make the geometric join
illegal. It consumes function-value height, with the exact identity

    (F_old-mu)/m_boundary+(F_cell-mu)/R_cap=theta          (15)

whenever the positive part in (14) is active. The earlier zero-preserving
budget was the condition mu=0. Allowing mu>0 requires the sublevel
distance state, as the counterexample proves; it does not erase the
loss or determine its normalization from Dist alone.

## 5. Remaining quantitative question

This package gives an actual larger class of legal iterative joins and
an exact, growing finite state. Cross-history legality (7), the loss (14),
and the nearest-minimizer cost (9) all remain in the same construction.
A policy selecting cells, scales and levels for every prescribed schedule
with normalized endpoint error at least (1-o(1))U_n^ZJ is still absent.
None of the displayed example ratios is claimed to optimize W_n.
All new assertions here are research findings awaiting verification;
they are not accepted graph facts or premises for other submissions.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003119 -->
# Nonlinear failure of the two-copy ruler despite the quadratic tests

Status: source-based candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No new solver or
fact_submit. The checkpoint application and projection conversion below
are derived afresh; earlier unverified C27 and C29 are not premises.
The external checkpoint construction is the one explicitly permitted in
PROBLEM.md, Section 7, with its source and applicability checked below.

## Statement

Put c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, H_0=(),
H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), and

    s_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2),
    G_m=(H_m,H_m), L_m=2(2^m-1), S_m=2s_m.

There is one fixed integer r such that, for every m>=r+4, an actual
finite-dimensional convex differentiable 1-smooth function f with
f(0)=min f=0, started at a unit vector, satisfies on the whole G_m run

    2f(x_(L_m))
      >= (98/81)^(2^(m-r-2)) / [8(1+s_r)].           (1)

In particular the error grows without bound. For the intrinsic energy

    V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)
        +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
        -S(S+1)||g_n||^2,

the same instance has

    V_(G_m)
      <=1-[s_m/(4(1+s_r))](98/81)^(2^(m-r-2))<0.   (2)

Moreover, a negative-energy instance for this identical ordered schedule
can be realized as F_C(x)=(||x||^2-dist(x,C)^2)/2 on a simplex C with
L_m+2 vertices in dimension L_m+1. The sampled gradients are distinct
vertices, and every normal inequality against a different retained vertex,
including zero, is strict. This last conversion retains the sign of V;
it makes NO assertion preserving (1) after normalizing the new initial
distance.

Together with the independently proved, still unverified quadratic/cut
calculations in C29, this supplies an ACTUAL nonlinear rejection of that
test-safe family. The proof of (1)--(2) does not rely on C29. Neither this
example nor C29 decides primitive capacity for arbitrary schedules or
the original unrestricted Zhang--Jiang exact-profile question.

## 1. External construction, constants, and hypotheses

Source: Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for
Gradient Descent, arXiv:2609.09152v2, Section 3.1, Lemmas 3.1--3.2 and
the pre-weakening final-value calculation proving Lemma 2.2; proofs in
Appendix A.1--A.3. URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09152v2 . The PDF
and extracted text are in downloads/ye-liu-2609.09152.{pdf,txt}. The
arc construction, projection proof, displacement bound, zero regions,
assembly, and terminal calculation were reread for this application.
A fresh Matlas checkpoint-transfer query returned unrelated fixed-step
results, none used. The direct source's identity and version were checked.

The exact input, also given in PROBLEM.md Section 7, is this. Fix positive
epsilon and gamma, and chi=sqrt(1+2gamma+epsilon^2). There are finite
constants A_YL and B_YL, depending only on these fixed parameters, such
that any nonnegative finite schedule with selected checkpoint sizes
z_i>=B_YL admits a finite-dimensional legal function, minimizer zero,
and initial point e_1, with

    2f(x_n)>=1/[2(1+T)]
               product_i [z_i/(chi(A_YL+u_i)+epsilon z_i)]^2. (3)

Here u_i is the sum after the preceding selected checkpoint and before
checkpoint i, and T is the sum after the last selected checkpoint.
The first u_i includes the whole initial ordinary block.

For completeness, the source's local functions are translated/scaled
parameter-one Moreau envelopes of finite support functions. Their
gradients are projections onto finite convex hulls. The backward arc
construction yields the prescribed trajectory for arbitrary intervening
nonnegative steps and a bounded second-coordinate displacement. For a
local checkpoint b>=1+epsilon^(-2), its first coordinate stops at U>=0,
and the function vanishes both when x<=0,y>=0 and when x=U,y>=Y.
Those regions keep every future and past component inactive during the
appropriate part of the concatenated trajectory. The global function is
half the sum of adjacent-coordinate local functions and a terminal
one-sided Huber function. Each coordinate occurs in at most two terms,
so the global smoothness bound is one. Its local steps are h/2, accounting
for B_YL=2(1+epsilon^(-2)). The initial point is e_1 and all activation
offsets are nonnegative, so zero is a common minimizer. There are one
more coordinates than selected checkpoints. If the final transferred
distance is D, the terminal Huber value is D^2/[4(1+T)], yielding (3)
after multiplication by two. No inference from the paper's notation
for its performance measure is used.

Choose epsilon=1/100 and gamma=2099/20000, so chi=11/10 and
B_YL=20002. Choose any permitted fixed finite A_YL. Then choose a
fixed integer r>=18 sufficiently large that

    b_r>=20002,  chi A_YL/b_r<=1/100.

In particular s_r>1. Indeed s_r/b_r>=13/8 for r>=17:
(10/13)^17<1/40 and (c-2)^(-1)=5/3 give this estimate.
All constants are fixed independently of m.

## 2. The one changed ordinary block at the copy boundary

Choose every step of level at least r in EACH copy of H_m. Put M=m-r.
Within each copy, every ordinary block between selected checkpoints is
exactly H_r. The initial and terminal ordinary blocks are also H_r.
At the junction of the two copies, the one intervening ordinary block
is (H_r,H_r), of sum 2s_r. There is exactly one such exceptional block.
The selected level-ell step b_r c^ell occurs 2^(M-ell-1) times in each
copy, for 0<=ell<M. The terminal ordinary sum of the full word is s_r.

For a usual ordinary block,

    chi(A_YL+s_r)/b_r
       <=1/100+(11/10)(5/3)=553/300<37/20.

Hence each transfer factor at level ell is at least

    q_ell=c^ell/[37/20+c^ell/100].

This increases with ell, and direct rational comparisons give

    q_0>=1/2, q_1>=4/3, q_2>=7/2, q_3>=8;
    q_ell>=1 for ell>=4.

When M>=4 the transfer product over one copy, using ordinary block sum
s_r throughout, is therefore at least

    [(1/2)^8(4/3)^4(7/2)^2 8]^(2^(M-4))
       =(98/81)^(2^(M-4)).                         (4)

For the exceptional block, at its actual following checkpoint z, the
ratio of the actual factor to the factor using ordinary sum s_r is

    [chi(A_YL+s_r)+epsilon z]
      /[chi(A_YL+2s_r)+epsilon z]>=1/2.              (5)

All summands are nonnegative, so the denominator is at most twice the
numerator. The full transfer product is thus at least

    (1/2)(98/81)^(2^(M-3)).

Substituting its square and T=s_r in (3) proves (1). All selected
checkpoints exceed B_YL. The special ordinary block is accepted by the
local construction with its actual doubled chronological step list;
no replacement of that list by a single artificial step is made.

## 3. From the objective blowup to a negative primitive energy

For any normalized legal run with minimizer zero and f(0)=0, smooth
convex interpolation (accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290) gives

    I_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=0.

Expanding ||x_(i+1)||^2=||x_i-h_i g_i||^2 and summing gives the exact
identity

    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2-2sum_(i<n)h_i I_i
                      -2S f_n-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.

For nonnegative steps and S this is at most ||x_0||^2-2S f_n. Using
||x_0||=1, S=2s_m, and (1) proves the first inequality in (2).
Since s_m>=2^M s_r, M>=4, and s_r>1, its subtracted term is greater
than 4s_r/(1+s_r)>2. Thus (2) is strictly negative at every stated
horizon, not just eventually.

## 4. A finite strict simplex witness, with normalization loss visible

Here is the conversion in full, without invoking an unverified reduction.
Write n=L_m; every step of G_m is positive. Let the energy of the actual
nonlinear run above be -v<0. For ell=0,...,n choose
theta_ell=(ell+1)/(2(n+2)), let A_aux=diag(theta_ell), and run
psi(z)=<z,A_aux z>/2 from z_0=(1,...,1) under the same schedule.
The gradients w_i have components theta_ell P_i(theta_ell), where
P_i(t)=product_(j<i)(1-h_j t). Distinct degrees 0,...,n and n+1
distinct theta_ell imply that the w_i are linearly independent. They
also imply that the z_i are pairwise distinct. Since 0<A_aux<I,

    Q_ij^aux=-(1/2)<z_i-z_j,A_aux(I-A_aux)(z_i-z_j)><0, i!=j,

in the convention Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Let E_aux be the finite auxiliary energy and put
delta=v/[2(v+|E_aux|)]. The actual product function
f_mix(x,z)=f(x)+delta psi(z/sqrt(delta)), initialized at
(x_0,sqrt(delta)z_0), has jets
(x_i,sqrt(delta)z_i), (g_i,sqrt(delta)w_i), f_i+delta psi_i.
It remains 1-smooth, its gradient vectors G_i are independent, all its
finite Q_ij are strict, and its energy is at most -v/2. Denote its
points and values by Z_i,F_i for the remainder of this section.

Choose f_* = min_i(F_i-||G_i||^2/2)-1. Independence makes the following
linear equations soluble for z_* in the span of the G_i:

    <G_i,z_*>=f_*-F_i+<G_i,Z_i>-||G_i||^2/2.

Set X_i=Z_i-z_*, V_i=F_i-f_*. Then
V_i=<G_i,X_i>-||G_i||^2/2 and

    <X_i-G_i,G_j-G_i>=Q_ji<0,  i!=j,
    <X_i-G_i,-G_i>=-V_i+||G_i||^2/2<=-1.

Consequently G_i is the projection of X_i onto
C=conv{0,G_0,...,G_n}: the displayed inequalities are the normal
condition against EVERY vertex, hence by convex combinations against
every point of C. Orthogonally project the X_i to the gradient span;
the GD equations, inner products, and all normal inequalities are
unchanged. That span has dimension n+1 and the listed n+2 vertices
are affinely independent.

The function F_C(x)=max_(u in C)(<x,u>-||u||^2/2) is differentiable,
convex, and 1-smooth with gradient P_C: the projection variational
inequalities imply firm nonexpansiveness, and comparison of the two
maxima gives its derivative. It has minimum zero at zero and takes
values V_i at X_i. Translation and common value shifts leave the
intrinsic energy unchanged, so the new energy is at most -v/2.

Finally F_C(X_i)=<G_i,X_i>-||G_i||^2/2 makes every I_i zero, and
expansion of the preceding telescope gives

    V_h=||X_0||^2-||X_n+S G_n||^2<0.

The perturbation and z_* shift need not preserve ||x_0||. Thus the
large normalized objective in (1) belongs to the original source
instance; only the negative sign is asserted for the simplex instance.

## DOC-003120 — project/research/shared_evidence/95083b67b0f24633.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\projects\gd-exact-profile-20260926\research\shared_evidence\95083b67b0f24633.md

原始字节数：3030；原始 SHA-256：745f22c9603bbb0804bb7eb24e475c1b78f86ef1ee922fa16072c6c4c2df0caa

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003120 -->
UNVERIFIED joint-draft counterexamples; no formal gate. Define for normalized convex differentiable1-smooth f with minimizer/value0, P_t(x)=||x||^2+2t f(x), K_t^eta=P_t+eta t^2||grad f(x)||^2, and B=P_a(x_p)-K_(a+beta)^eta(x_p-beta g_p). All coordinates are actual scalar GD samples of one function, with Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2.
PRIMARY eta=1,a=3/2: actual word (3/2,beta,1). For 0<=beta<9/4 take H(x)=x^2/2 on |x|<=1 and |x|-1/2 outside, start x0=beta+7/2. Its derivative clip(x,[-1,1]) is nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz; its minimum is0 uniquely at0. Actual x=(beta+7/2,beta+2,2,1), g=(1,1,1,1), f_i=x_i-1/2. Every finite Q and every Q_(i,star) is0; Q_(star,i)=1-x_i<=0. Thus all20 directed signs hold, including beta0 without quotienting duplicate formal gradients. The full Gram of (x0,g0,g1,g2,g3) is its scalar column outer product. Direct evaluation gives B=beta-9/4<0. For beta>=9/4 take the unit quadratic from x0=2. Actual x=(2,-1,beta-1,0), g_i=x_i, f_i=x_i^2/2. ALL20 Q are0, and the full Gram is the outer product of (2,2,-1,beta-1,0). With c=beta+3/2, B=5/2-(1+c+c^2)(beta-1)^2<=-4985/256<0.
SOLE REPAIR eta=1/2,a=12: actual word ((3/2)^8,beta,1), length10, sum13+beta. For 0<=beta<=2 take the same Huber with x0=beta+14. Actual x_i=beta+14-3i/2 for0<=i<=8, x9=2,x10=1, g_i=1,f_i=x_i-1/2. Again every finite/optimum-outgoing Q is0 and Q_(star,i)=1-x_i<=0, specifying all132 ordered signs. B=beta^2/2+13beta-72<=-44. For beta>2 take the unit quadratic from ORIGINAL x0=256: x_i=256(-1/2)^i for0<=i<=8, x8=1,x9=1-beta,x10=0, g_i=x_i,f_i=x_i^2/2. All132 Q are0 and B=13-[1+12+beta+(12+beta)^2/2](1-beta)^2<-59. Both full original Gram matrices are outer products; no starting state is reset at the bridge.

All other directed multipliers are0. For step1, K_t^eta(start)-P_(t+1)(end)+2Q_(0,star)+2(t+1)Q_(1,0)=(eta t^2+t+1)||g0||^2+(t+1)||g1||^2. Thus the children satisfy their named contracts with full PSD remainders for all t>=0. Sum the step3/2 identity at t_i=3i/2 for the length8 child: full coefficients lambda_(i,star)=3,lambda_(i+1,i)=3+3i for0<=i<8, all others0; its full remainder is the sum of those displayed squares. For arbitrary incoming t use t_i=t+3i/2.

This is a symbolic hypothesis test within the same C89 round, not a numerical search or an improving seed proposal. Take F(x,y)=x^2/2+y^2/4, with Hessian A=diag(1,1/2), unique minimizer0, and X_0=(1,1), so the TRUE initial-distance square is R^2=2. Use the three physical GD steps h=(10,20/19,1/2). Exact substitution gives g_0=(1,1/2), X_1=(-9,-4), g_1=(-9,-2), X_2=(9/19,-36/19), g_2=(9/19,-18/19), and X_3=(9/38,-27/19). The gradients at queried samples0 and2 are orthogonal: <g_0,g_2>=9/19-9/19=0. However <g_2,g_1>=-45/19<0. The actual values satisfy 2F(X_2)/R^2=729/722 and 2F(X_3)/R^2=1539/2888, both greater than1/4. Accepted0e7e78056dc6258c defines c_0=0,c_1=4,beta_1=4/(1+sqrt(1+2c_1))^2=1/4. Thus discarding the nonnegative-history hypothesis from the C89 selected-query bound is false; even its terminal consequence fails despite the last step1/2<2. Every original ordered interpolation pair is legal because direct expansion for this quadratic gives Q_ij=-<X_i-X_j,A(I-A)(X_i-X_j)>/2<=0, including the optimum and terminal. The function is convex and1-smooth directly from its diagonal Hessian. This does not refute C89's stated law, whose queried correlations are all nonnegative, and it does not supply an arbitrary-h profile construction. target_claim: extend the selected-query bound without nonnegative earlier correlations; status: refuted at draft level, not verifier accepted; impact: retain the chronological sign hypothesis and leave genuinely signed long-range histories open.
