# Paid remote leaf consistency: two explicit laws and their full-space failures

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report, worker max81. Original target OPEN.
Governing assignment: TASK.md and guidance60cc1ca7a8ad2d07. One invocation,
at most two mechanisms, no automatic renewal. All calculations below form
one unverified proof attempt. No intermediate draft is used as accepted truth.
No mathematical program, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replay,
optimizer, solver, formal call, fact_submit, installation, subagent or consult.

Two new original-index arrays are constructed, outside the old E80 support.
Both have nonnegative coefficients and exact objective value flow. Neither
has full PSD, at the requested level or even at an arbitrary initial-norm
coefficient. We give explicit original-vector counterdirections. A larger
contact-routed fan family is excluded by the same calculation. The second
law also gives a quantitative necessary restriction on receiver distributions:
mass sent across an earlier checkpoint must decrease on the scale of the
reciprocal of that checkpoint's physical step, when the two checkpoint
degrees agree. These are scoped coefficient obstructions, not W bounds.

## 1. Scope, accepted inputs and literal hierarchy

For every n>=0, L>0 and predetermined h in [0,infinity)^n, W_n(h) is the
supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite
dimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,
every such x_star and x_0!=x_star, with all n actual updates
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original question concerns
lim_n B_n inf_h W_n(h), with every nonnegative word, including zero and
arbitrarily large steps. No result below changes those quantifiers.

The benchmark is the original all-integer-split recurrence

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290, read in full, supplies the original interpolation
orientation, full independent-vector certificate interpretation and exact
actual-terminal completion. It deletes no queried-prefix inequalities.
Accepted931b9653ce0f7201, read in full, supplies B_n/D<47/50 for the
following SAME-enclosing-m hierarchy. Its implication from W<=17/(16D)
is conditional; that hypothesis is still unproved. No other accepted
mathematical result is used here. Prior finding0073a83e08ff2e21 was read
in full as awareness; the reset construction needed below is rederived.

Fix m>=1536. Define

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,
    z=n-1, b=1+a, M=1+b, N=2^(m-1), A=D-b^2,
    P81_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, Tail81_i=S-b-P81_i (i<z),
    K81_i=A-P81_i-1=2S-b^2-P81_i (i<z), qtar=17/(16D).

All children use this same m. Index z is the last queried leaf, not the
unqueried terminal n. All these hierarchy symbols are local to this report.
Let level(i)=v2(i+1), the exponent of 2 dividing i+1. Then
h_i=1+a*c^level(i), leaves are the even indices, and checkpoints the odd
indices. In particular h_z=b. Write Leaf={0,2,...,z}, Check={1,3,...,n-2}.

Elementary bounds used throughout are

    1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<sqrt(2)<3/2,
    12/5<c<5/2, beta>7/8,
    d_1=beta, d_2=beta*c>21/10,
    S>2^m-1, D>2^m, A>0, K81_i>Tail81_i>0.

For example a>1/3 follows from beta>7/8 and c<5/2. The bound on c
follows from lambda<17/7 and epsilon<=1/6144. Also
b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]. The other comparisons follow by
substitution. K81_i-Tail81_i=S+b-b^2>0 since b<2 and S>=b. These observations
include positivity of every denominator in the arrays below.

Define a dyadic cell of height j to be

    I_(j,r)={r*2^j,...,(r+1)*2^j-2},
    1<=j<=m, 0<=r<2^(m-j).

For distinct queried indices u,v, J(u,v) is the least height of a cell
containing both. Such a cell exists and its height is unique. This defines
the exact least common dyadic ancestor in ORIGINAL indices. For i in the
left half, including the whole-word root, J(i,z)=m.

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, and retain independent original
vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n. Positions are always
x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. For distinct labels put

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Legal samples have Q_ij<=0. An original coefficient array with
out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n) defines

    M_cert(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij.

After value cancellation, it must be PSD on ALL original vectors to be an
upper certificate. A negative formal vector is not a primal instance.

## 2. Mechanism I: position-sensitive remote routing to the last leaf

First construct the entire family with independently chosen 0<=theta_i<=1
for each i<z. Set

    r81_i=2theta_i*h_i/K81_i,
    w81_i=2(1-theta_i)*h_i/Tail81_i.

The frozen LCA-sensitive law tested in Sections 6--8 is

    eta=1/(4D), theta_i=1-eta/J(i,z).                    (I.1)

Every remote pair then has positive weight. The literal nonzero array is

    lambda_(n,z)=2,
    lambda_(z,star)=2-sum_(i<z)r81_i,
    lambda_(i,star)=r81_i,
    lambda_(z,i)=r81_i+w81_i, lambda_(i,z)=w81_i, i<z.       (I.2)

All unlisted rows are zero, including all incoming terminal and optimum
rows. This array includes checkpoint-to-last-leaf rows at arbitrary
distance and leaf-to-last-leaf rows. Its support is not contained in E80.

All signs can be checked without an unknown feasibility condition. Put
Kbase=S+b-b^2>0. Since K81_i=Kbase+Tail81_i, and Tail81_i decreases by h_i as i
increases, integration of the decreasing function 1/(Kbase+t) gives

    sum_(i<z) h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i)
      <= integral_0^(S-b) dt/(Kbase+t)
      =log((2S-b^2)/(S+b-b^2)) <log 2<1.

Indeed h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i) is bounded by the integral over
[Tail81_i-h_i,Tail81_i], and these intervals partition [0,S-b]. The ratio is below
2 because b<2. Thus sum r81_i<2 for every allowed theta. Every coefficient
in (I.2) is nonnegative, with strict outgoing optimum mass at z.
At i<z the two finite directions have net inflow r81_i, exactly discharged
to star. At z the finite/optimum outgoing net is 2, supplied by (n,z).
Terminal flow is 2. This proves all value cancellations.

This is a new source-routing base, not an addition to any inherited
certificate. Its terminal distribution is concentrated at the actual last
leaf; its entire optimum distribution is given in (I.2).

### 2.1 Full polynomial and the paid contact equation

Write g=g_z, v_i=g_i-g for i<z and u=sum r81_i v_i. Put

    B(v)=sum_(i<z)[r81_i+w81_i(1-h_i)]||v_i||^2
         +sum_(i<t<z)(h_i*r81_t-w81_i*h_t)<v_i,v_t>,
    Jrem(v)=B(v)-A||u||^2/4.

The exact full identity, for EVERY real q, is

    M_I(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2
       +q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2
       -<x_0,u>+A<g,u>+B(v).                         (I.3)

Here is a direct derivation. Starting with rows 2Q_(n,z)+2Q_(z,star),
replace r81_i units of Q_(z,star) by Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star), and add w81_i
units of Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i). The two added expressions, after GD, are

    Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star)-Q_(z,star)
       =-<v_i,x_0>+(P81_i+1)<v_i,g>
                          +||v_i||^2+sum_(t<i)h_t<v_i,v_t>,
    Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i)
       =||v_i||^2-Tail81_i<v_i,g>-sum_(i<=t<z)h_t<v_i,v_t>.

The original base has terminal square ||g_n+(b-1)g||^2 and remainder
q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2+2sum h_i<v_i,g>.
The defining relation

    w81_i*Tail81_i=2h_i-K81_i*r81_i                              (I.4)

changes each last cross coefficient to A*r81_i, giving (I.3). In particular,
remote symmetric pairs were NOT treated as PSD. Their negative diagonal
and mixed displacement terms appear in B.

At q=1/A, (I.3) is equivalently

    M_I(1/A)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2
      +||x_0-A*g-(A/2)u||^2/A+Jrem(v).               (I.5)

Equation (I.4) is an intentional design restriction of this family. It is
not claimed necessary for a general certificate. No zero-contact rule is
imposed on an unrestricted optimum. The term -A||u||^2/4 is the ONE
global initial/common-gradient charge for this deliberately balanced law.

### 2.2 Full-space failure for every member of the contact family

Take p=2^(m-1)-1, H=h_p>4. Only v_p is nonzero, with v_p=1. The coefficient
of Jrem on this vector is

    E_p=r81_p-w81_p(H-1)-A*r81_p^2/4.                       (I.6)

If theta_p<=1/2, then r81_p<=H/K81_p and w81_p>=H/Tail81_p. Thus
r81_p-w81_p(H-1)<=H/K81_p-H(H-1)/Tail81_p<0 because K81_p>Tail81_p and H>2.
If theta_p>=1/2, then r81_p>=H/K81_p>H/A>4/A, and
r81_p-A*r81_p^2/4<0; subtracting w81_p(H-1)>=0 preserves negativity.
So E_p<0 for EVERY choice of the whole profile theta.

The complete scalar original-vector counterdirection is

    x_0=0,
    g_i=-r81_p/2 for every queried i!=p,
    g_p=1-r81_p/2,
    g_n=(b-1)r81_p/2.                                  (I.7)

It sets g=-r81_p/2, u=r81_p, makes the terminal square and the square in
(I.5) zero, and gives M_I(q)=E_p<0 for every real q. The change from
1/A to q multiplies ||x_0||^2=0. Hence even unlimited initial-norm slack
cannot repair this coefficient family. This is stronger than rejection
of the frozen small-eta profile, but only for family (I.2)--(I.4).

## 3. Mechanism II: dense earlier-leaf transport with paid reverse cycles

Put Tc=sum_(p in Check)h_p and t_p=h_p/Tc, so sum t_p=1. For EVERY
checkpoint p and earlier leaf ell<p define

    raw_(p,ell)=[1+(p-ell)/(p+1)]/J(p,ell),
    Z_p=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<p)raw_(p,ell),
    omega_(p,ell)=raw_(p,ell)/Z_p,
    mu_ell=sum_(p in Check,p>ell)t_p*omega_(p,ell).    (II.1)

There is at least one earlier leaf for every checkpoint, so Z_p>0.
The weights depend on the exact least common ancestor and position within
the enclosing chronological range. They are not uniform fan averages.
The formula for mu_ell is used only for ell<z. The new optimum distribution is mu_ell at
earlier leaves, zero at every checkpoint, and mass 1 at z.

For a global reverse-cycle amount kappa>=0, the array is

    lambda_(n,z)=2, lambda_(z,star)=1,
    lambda_(z,p)=t_p, p in Check,
    lambda_(p,ell)=(1+kappa)t_p*omega_(p,ell),
    lambda_(ell,p)=kappa*t_p*omega_(p,ell), ell<p,
    lambda_(ell,star)=mu_ell, ell in Leaf, ell<z.      (II.2)

Every unlisted entry is zero. The frozen law is kappa=1. All signs are
nonnegative. One unit flows z->star and one flows z->p->ell->star.
Every reverse cycle has zero net flow. Thus (II.2) has exactly terminal
flow 2, with no incoming terminal or optimum edge. In particular it
contains Q_(n-2,0) with a strictly positive coefficient.

The complete original-row expression is

    M_II(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,z)+Q_(z,star)
      +sum_p t_p[Q_(z,p)+sum_(ell<p)omega_(p,ell)
                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,star))]
      +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)t_p*omega_(p,ell)
                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)). (II.3)

There are no omitted charges in this expression. Each finite row is its
original ordered interpolation row. Equations (II.1)--(II.3) are a
coefficient law, not an instruction to solve for unknown PSD blocks.

### 3.1 Complete quadratic, including all independent prefix gradients

Set W_(p,ell)=t_p*omega_(p,ell). Eliminating only g_n gives
M_II(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2+R_II, where

    R_II=q||x_0||^2-<x_0,g_z+sum_(ell<z)mu_ell*g_ell>
       +(2-(b-1)^2)||g_z||^2+sum_p t_p||g_p||^2
       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell||g_ell||^2
       -sum_p t_p<g_z,g_p>-sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,g_ell>
       +<g_z,sum_(i<z)h_i*g_i>
       +sum_p t_p<g_z,sum_(p<=i<z)h_i*g_i>
       +sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>
       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell<g_ell,sum_(i<ell)h_i*g_i>
       +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)
          [||g_ell-g_p||^2
                  -<g_ell-g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>]. (II.4)

The formula follows by expanding the four types of prefix rows in
(II.3). For example, the two half-squared-difference contributions at p
give t_p||g_p||^2; the two at each leaf give mu_ell||g_ell||^2.
Both original displacements of each reverse cycle remain in the last line.
No common-gradient, terminal or initial-point relation is assumed.

### 3.2 A two-checkpoint zero-displacement counterdirection

For a queried checkpoint r<p put
Omega_p(r)=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<=r)omega_(p,ell). On the full original
subspace x_0=0, every leaf gradient zero and g_n=0, (II.4) becomes

    (1+kappa)[sum_p t_p||g_p||^2
       +sum_(r<p in Check)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)<g_r,g_p>]. (II.5)

This is a necessary test of the full matrix, not a claim that checkpoint
compression is lossless. All omitted formal coordinates are explicitly
set to zero only to exhibit a negative direction.

Choose r=2^(m-2)-1 and p=3*2^(m-2)-1, the roots of the two children of
the whole word. Both physical steps equal H=1+a*c^(m-2), and t_r=t_p.
There are 2^(m-3) earlier leaves at or before r and 3*2^(m-3) before p.
Every raw weight is at least 1/m and less than 2, so

    Omega_p(r)>1/(6m).

Also H>2^(m-2)/3>12m. For the latter comparison, 2^(m-2)>36m at
m=16 and doubling preserves it thereafter. Consequently H*Omega_p(r)>2.
Set the complete original vector to

    x_0=0, g_r=1, g_p=-1, all other g_i=0, including g_n. (II.6)

Its physical displacement is H-H=0. Equation (II.5) gives

    M_II(q)=(1+kappa)t_p[2-H*Omega_p(r)]<0.           (II.7)

This holds for EVERY real q and EVERY kappa>=0. Paying a larger common
reverse-cycle factor cannot repair the law.

More generally, for arbitrary positive checkpoint masses summing to 1 and receiver
probabilities with the same routing support and one common kappa, the
two-coordinate determinant in (II.5) requires

    h_r*Omega_p(r) <= 2*sqrt(t_r/t_p), r<p.          (II.8)

Indeed its diagonal entries are (1+kappa)t_r and (1+kappa)t_p, and its
off-diagonal entry is (1+kappa)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)/2. Thus comparable
checkpoint degrees need receiver leakage at most a constant divided by
the earlier physical step. The displayed raw kernel has only an inverse-m
lower bound on that leakage, which is too large. This requirement alone
does not certify any more localized law; all other directions remain.

## 4. One global initial/common charge and the exact terminal account

Both laws have no incoming terminal row and lambda_(n,z)=2. Directly,

    -2f_n+2Q_(n,z)
       =||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2-2f_z+[1-(b-1)^2]||g_z||^2. (4.1)

Thus the true terminal is free, with center -(b-1)g_z. Nothing identifies
it with the last queried gradient. The entire original prefix remains.

For either law let mu_i=lambda_(i,star), finite K_ij=lambda_ij, and h_n=0
only for notation. There are no incoming optimum rows and sum mu_i=2.
The complete polynomial is q||x_0||^2-sum mu_i<x_0,g_i>+g^T C g, with

    C_ii=mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(v>i)K_iv,
    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)K_iv
                      +h_i[mu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (4.2)

To check this formula, the incident half-squared-gradient terms contribute
mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n), by flow; each forward row out of i contributes
-h_i on that diagonal. For i<j the squared differences give the first
term, forward rows from i crossing j the second, and rows from j reaching
index <=i or star the third. This accounts for every original coordinate.

Eliminate g_n using (4.1), obtaining the explicit prefix matrix
Cpre=C_(0:z,0:z)-(b-1)^2 e_z e_z^T, where e_z selects coordinate z.
Let g_i=G+v_i on the queried prefix, with v_z=0. Define

    u=sum_(i<=z)mu_i*v_i, k=e^T Cpre v, T=v^T Cpre v,

where e is the all-ones prefix column. The coefficients Cpre,mu are
entirely specified by the two literal arrays and (4.2). Substitution of
all prefix gradients G gives e^T Cpre e=A. Therefore the full prefix form
is exactly

    q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,G>+A||G||^2-<x_0,u>+2<G,k>+T. (4.3)

At q=qtar the initial/common determinant Delta=qA-1 is positive, because
D>17b^2. Eliminating this ONE two-coordinate block charges

    Charge(u,k)=[A||u||^2/4-<u,k>+q||k||^2]/(qA-1). (4.4)

This follows by inversion of [[q,-1],[-1,A]] with cross column
(-u/2,k). No separate local charge is substituted for it. Full PSD is
equivalent to T>=Charge for every original prefix variation v_z=0.
At a singular determinant a range condition would additionally be needed;
the advertised qtar is not singular. For Law I, k=A*u/2 by (I.3), and
(4.4) is exactly A||u||^2/4. Its minimizing initial/common values are
x_0=0,G=-u/2, explaining why increasing q cannot repair (I.7).
For Law II, (4.2) and (II.4) retain k explicitly; (II.6) already gives
a negative value before any attempted elimination. No PSD claim is left
to an unspecified matrix problem: both full matrices have been refuted.

## 5. Complete reconstruction of the E80 reset transcript

For reuse as a SCREEN only, define Rreset=a*c^2/M=beta*c/M and sbit(k)
to be the number of ones in the binary expansion of integer k>=0. Put

    v_k=(-1)^k Rreset^k c^(-sbit(k))/M, 0<=k<N.

At leaf 2k prescribe x=M v_k,g=v_k,f=(b+1/2)v_k^2. At checkpoint
2k+1<n prescribe x=g=v_k,f=v_k^2/2. Binary carry gives
sbit(k+1)-sbit(k)=1-v2(k+1), hence
v_(k+1)/v_k=-a*c^(1+v2(k+1))/M. Thus the leaf step takes M v_k to v_k
and the following actual checkpoint step takes v_k to M v_(k+1).
Every physical update is satisfied, x_0=1, and the final position is

    t=x_n=v_(N-1)=-Rreset^(N-1)/(M*c^(m-1)).

Complete the actual terminal by g_n=u=2t/(b+2), f_n=t^2/(b+2), and set
the optimum jet to zero. This is a scalar Gram transcript; no function is
asserted to interpolate it. Substitution gives the full row table:

    Q_(leaf(v),star)=0, Q_(star,leaf(v))=-b*v^2;
    both optimum rows at check(w) are 0;
    Q_(check(w),check(w'))=0;
    Q_(leaf(v),check(w))=0;
    Q_(check(w),leaf(v))=b*v*(w-v);
    Q_(leaf(v),leaf(w))=b*w*(v-w);
    Q_(n,check(w))=Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=-b*u^2/4;
    Q_(n,leaf(v))=-b*(v-u/2)^2.                       (5.1)

In particular both adjacent queried directions are nonpositive: following
a leaf has w=v, while at the preceding checkpoint the next leaf parameter
is -d*w/M with d>0. Every E80 row is accounted for by this table. E80 means
both optimum orientations at every finite index, all checkpoint pairs,
all leaf-to-checkpoint rows, adjacent checkpoint-to-leaf rows, and all
outgoing actual-terminal rows. No leaf-to-leaf or remote checkpoint-to-
leaf rows belonged to E80.

The growth estimate is included, rather than imported from the old draft.
Since M=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],

    Rreset-1=epsilon[2/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda*epsilon]/M
              >epsilon/8=1/(32m).

For the strict bound, epsilon<1/8 and lambda<5/2 make the bracket exceed
32/45-5/16>5/16, while M<5/2. For m>=16, induction gives
2^(m-1)-1>=96m^2: the base is 32767>=24576 and the induction difference
96m^2-192m-95 is positive. Hence N-1>32m(2m+1).
The binomial inequality (1+1/(32m))^(32m)>=2 yields
Rreset^(N-1)>2^(2m+1). Because M*c^(m-1)<2^(2m), we have t<-2.

This transcript is explicitly not interpolable. At pbad=n-2 the checkpoint
parameter w=v_(N-2)=-M*t/beta>2M, whereas v_0=1/M. Consequently

    Q_(pbad,0)=b*v_0*(w-v_0)>0, Q_(0,pbad)=0.        (5.2)

Law I omits this row. Law II includes it with positive weight. The new
failure below is insufficient paid action even though that violated row
is now present. Neither (5.1) nor (5.2) is a lower bound on unrestricted W.

### 5.1 A symbolic tail bound; no trajectory or recurrence was executed

For k=N-1-j, binary complementation gives exactly

    |v_k/t|=Rreset^(-j)c^sbit(j), 1<=j<N.

Since an integer with r binary ones is at least 2^r-1,
c^(2sbit(j))<=(j+1)^4. Put rreset=Rreset^(-2). The previous lower bound
on Rreset gives 1-rreset>=1/(64m): with zeta=1/(32m)<1, use
1-(1+zeta)^(-2)>=zeta/2.
For every integer d>=1,
j^d<=d!*binom(j+d-1,d), and summing the geometric-series identity gives
sum_(j>=1)j^d r^j<=d!/(1-r)^(d+1), 0<r<1. This identity follows by
multiplying d+1 geometric series and counting compositions, or by d
successive differentiations of their convergent series. Therefore

    sum_(k<N-1)v_k^2/t^2 <=768(64m)^5=:Moment5,
    sum_(p=2k+1 in Check)h_p*v_k^2/t^2
                                  <=92160(64m)^7=:Moment7. (5.3)

For the second bound, v2(k+1)=v2(j), so
h_p=1+beta*c^v2(j)<=1+j^2<=2j^2. Apply the d=6 inequality to
2j^2(j+1)^4<=128j^6. For the first use (j+1)^4<=16j^4; the displayed
constant 768 is a harmless enlargement of 16*4!.

All coarse exponential comparisons used below follow from

    2^(m-1)>2^24(64m)^7, m>=1536.                    (5.4)

At m=1536, m<2^11 makes the right side less than 2^143, whereas the
left side is 2^1535. The ratio of the left side to m^7 increases at each
integer increment, since (1+1/m)^7<sum_(j>=0)(7/m)^j<2 for m>=1536.
This proves (5.4) for all relevant m by induction. This is symbolic
reasoning, not a generated arithmetic table or executed recurrence.

## 6. Complete reset screen for both frozen laws

On (5.1), Law I has exact value

    M_I(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2
      +b*sum_(earlier leaves ell=2k)
           [r81_ell*v_k*(t-v_k)-w81_ell*(t-v_k)^2]
      +b*sum_(checkpoints p=2k+1)w81_p*t*(v_k-t).      (6.1)

Every selected original row appears here: outgoing optimum rows vanish,
last-leaf-to-checkpoint rows vanish, leaf pairs and both other orientations
are evaluated by (5.1). For the frozen eta=1/(4D), w81_p<=2eta. Use
v(t-v)<=t^2/4 and t(v-t)<=v^2/4, and sum r81_ell<2. Formula (5.3) and
(5.4) give

    last sum in (6.1) <=(b*eta/2)Moment5*t^2 <t^2/8.

Indeed its coefficient is at most 96b(64m)^5/D, which is less than 1/8
by b<3/2, D>2^m and (5.4). The leaf relay contribution is at most
b*t^2/2 and its symmetric-pair part is nonpositive. Finally

    2/(b+2)+2b*(b+1)^2/(b+2)^2-b/2
       >4/7+7b/18 >121/126.

Here (b+1)/(b+2)>2/3 and b>1. Thus

    M_I(qtar)<qtar-(3/4)t^2<0.                       (6.2)

The inequality 121/126-1/8>3/4 and t^2>4 justify the displayed margin.
This reset conclusion is for the frozen eta law. The broader arbitrary-
theta contact family is excluded instead by (I.7), without a claim that
every such theta has the same sign on the reset transcript.

For Law II with kappa=1, the EXACT corresponding expression is

    M_II(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2
       +2b*sum_(p=2k+1)t_p*sum_(ell=2s<p)
                         omega_(p,ell)*v_s*(v_k-v_s). (6.3)

The reverse leaf-to-checkpoint rows are zero, as are the source-to-
checkpoint and outgoing optimum rows. The displayed 2 is the actual
1+kappa forward weight. Since v_s(v_k-v_s)<=v_k^2/4, the last sum is
at most (b/2)sum_p t_p*v_k^2. The total checkpoint mass satisfies
Tc>=h_(2^(m-1)-1)>2^(m-1)/3. Equations (5.3)--(5.4) prove

    sum_p t_p*v_k^2/t^2 <=Moment7/Tc<1/4.

Therefore

    M_II(qtar)<qtar-[2/(b+2)-b/8]*t^2
                       <qtar-t^2/4<0.              (6.4)

We used 2/(b+2)-b/8>4/7-3/16>1/4 and qtar<1. The actual negative
terminal-row cost was retained in (6.3) and only dropped for this upper
bound. The included remote rows are too weak under this global degree
normalization. Their mere presence does not pay the required reset cost.

## 7. The older two-gradient deep waves

For 2<=j<=m-3 set p=2^j-1, H=h_p, x_0=0, g_0=-H/b, g_p=1, and every
other original gradient, including g_n, zero. The physical displacement
is exactly zero. This is a formal full-space screen only.

For the frozen Law I, direct substitution in (I.3) gives

    M_I=(r81_0+w81_0)H^2/b^2-(H-1)(r81_p+w81_p).           (7.1)

Both J(0,z) and J(p,z) equal m. Define, only for this calculation,

    F(P)=(2-2eta/m)/(2S-b^2-P)+(2eta/m)/(S-b-P).

Then r81_i+w81_i=h_i F(P81_i) for i=0,p, and F is strictly increasing.
For j>=3, H>4 and b/(b-1)<4; using F(P81_p)>F(0) in (7.1) gives

    M_I<F(0)H[H/b-(H-1)]<0.                        (7.2)

At j=2, H=1+beta*c and P81_p=2b+1+beta<6. Now
b-(b-1)H=1-beta^2>epsilon. Also

    [F(P81_p)-F(0)]/F(0) <= P81_p/(S-b-P81_p).

It follows from (7.1) that M_I>0 if
15/(S-8)<1/(6m), which follows from S>90m+8. This last inequality
holds since S>2^m-1 and m>=1536 (the ratio 2^m/m increases for m>1,
and the asserted comparison already holds at m=16).
Thus Law I passes exactly this j=2 screen and fails all j>=3 in its range.

Law II with kappa=1 passes every one of these old deep waves. Write

    L_le=sum_(r in Check,r<=p)t_r*omega_(r,0),
    L_gt=sum_(r in Check,r>p)t_r*omega_(r,0).

Its exact value is

    M_II=[(2-b)L_le+2L_gt]*H^2/b^2
                 +2t_p-t_p*omega_(p,0)*(2b-3)*H/b>0. (7.3)

To derive it, the routed base contributes
(L_le+L_gt)H^2/b^2+t_p-t_p*omega_(p,0)(b-1)H/b.
For a reverse cycle ending before p the interval displacement is b*g_0;
for one ending after p it is zero. The cycle at p contributes the
remaining mixed term with coefficient b-2 and its checkpoint diagonal.
Adding these gives (7.3). Its signs follow from b<3/2 and t_p>0.
The new equal-scale wave (II.6) still refutes its full matrix.

## 8. The older H4 positive/negative block direction

Set x_0=0, g_i=1 at i=0,1,2,3, g_i=-1 at i=8,9,10,11, and all other
original gradients zero, including g_n. The two active four-query blocks
have identical physical mass, so displacement is zero. Put

    H1=1+beta, H2=1+beta*c,
    P2=b+H1, B4=2b+H1+H2, C4=b+H2.

Here P2<7/2, B4<9, C4>4. All active original prefix masses P81_i are
below 100, their total active step mass is 2B4<18 (in fact <17), and
all J(i,z)=m.

For Law I put k0=(2-2eta/m)/(2S-b^2) and l0=(2eta/m)/(S-b).
Freeze r81_i=k0 h_i,w81_i=l0 h_i only within this calculation. Zero physical
displacement makes the exact frozen polynomial

    (k0+l0)/2 * sum_active h_i(2-h_i).

The sum is twice
2b(2-b)+(1+beta)(1-beta)+(1+beta*c)(1-beta*c), which is less than -1:
the first term is <=2, the second <1/4, and the third <1-(21/10)^2.
Thus the full sum is <-2 and the frozen value is <-(k0+l0).

The actual coefficients differ by at most
|r81_i-k0 h_i|<=200 k0 h_i/S and
|w81_i-l0 h_i|<=200 l0 h_i/S for each active i, since P81_i<100 and S>400.
In the polynomial (I.3) with g_z=x_0=0, the factor multiplying each
r81_i or w81_i has absolute value at most 18: |g_i|<=1 and the total
active step mass is <17. Thus the total perturbation is at most
61200(k0+l0)/S<(k0+l0)/2, since S>2^18. Consequently M_I<0.

For Law II use its exact routed expression (II.3), setting all values
f_i=0 after their cancellation. Checkpoints p<=13 contribute at most
4000/Tc in absolute value. To verify this bound, there are seven such
checkpoints, h_p<8, every formal position has absolute value <9,
each finite row has absolute quadratic value <=20, and each optimum
row <=19/2. The bracket
Q_(z,p)+sum omega[2Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)+Q_(ell,star)]
has absolute value <=70. Summing t_p<=56/Tc gives less than 4000/Tc.

For p>=15, g_p=0 and its ancestor height J(p,ell) is the SAME for all
four active leaves ell=0,2,8,10. Every such p is outside the cell
{0,...,14} containing them. The full bracket equals

    2*sum_active_leaves omega_(p,ell)*L_ell,
    (L_0,L_2,L_8,L_10)=(1,1+P2,1-B4,1-B4+P2).

Since raw_(p,ell)=(2p+1-ell)/[(p+1)J], its numerator before the positive
denominator (p+1)J Z_p is

    N_p=8p-16+6P2+(16-4p)C4 <69-8p <=-3(p+1), p>=15.

This uses C4>4 and P2<7/2. Hence every such checkpoint contributes
negatively. At the whole root p0=2^(m-1)-1, J=m and Z_p0<=p0+1,
so its contribution is at most

    -6h_p0/[Tc*m*(p0+1)].

This one term already dominates 4000/Tc. Indeed
h_p0/[m(p0+1)]>(7/16)(6/5)^(m-2)/m>4000.
At m=1536 use (6/5)^4>2 and 383 blocks of four powers, which give a
lower bound exceeding 2^383 before division by m and multiplication by
7/16. Thereafter (6/5)^(m-2)/m increases since m>5. Therefore M_II<0.
This is an all-depth analytical sign proof for the actual first-block
embedding, not a finite numeric H4 experiment or a statement about all
four-block embeddings.

## 9. What is excluded, omitted rows, and the remaining interface

Law I fails for every independent theta_i in [0,1] satisfying its
definition, even though the exact optimum distribution and all remote
fan weights are allowed to vary. The obstruction uses its deliberately
balanced contact equation (I.4). It does not exclude a different contact
relation, a different terminal distribution, or added independent rows.
One omitted potentially relevant row is Q_(n-2,0), explicitly positive
on the reset transcript. Adding it would define a different law, not
repair (I.2) within its parameters.

Law II fails for the literal LCA/position kernel (II.1), every common
kappa>=0, and degrees t_p proportional to h_p. The necessary condition
(II.8) applies to the specified routed support with arbitrary degrees
and receiver probabilities, but is only one necessary two-coordinate
test. It is not an enlarged-support impossibility theorem. For example,
the omitted direct checkpoint pair Q_(p,r), with r,p from (II.6), and
its reverse are not in (II.2). Either could alter that two-coordinate
matrix; neither is presumed to be a free PSD repair. Non-last leaf-to-
leaf rows and nonuniform reverse-cycle amounts are also outside this law.

The concrete failure mechanisms are different. In Law I, exact contact
balance combines a large optimum charge at a long-step coordinate with
negative forward-pair diagonal costs. In Law II, a low-decay ancestor
tail creates a mixed term of size h_r Omega_p(r) between equally weighted
checkpoint coordinates. Increasing a common reverse-cycle coefficient
scales the offending matrix. Its normalization by the whole checkpoint
mass also underpays the reset transcript despite including its known
violated row. Passing the older two-gradient waves does not address this.

An unresolved full-function construction must pay the reset while
controlling all such ancestor-crossing mixed terms, and must also prove
the complete remainder after the single charge (4.4) and actual terminal
square (4.1). Merely requiring unknown blocks to be PSD restates the
missing theorem. We give no third coefficient law or automatic next round.

No positive primal candidate is asserted. Consequently no static function,
minimum set, closest radius or attained W lower bound is attributed to any
of the formal negative directions or to the incompatible reset transcript.
The original unrestricted exact-profile minimax question remains OPEN.

# Actual H2 primitive reserve and the retained U remainder

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation79, actual ended baseline78.
Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Guidance e7cc96e528402c8e.
One allocated invocation, completed ceiling79/attempted80, controller cap1.
No formal calls or fact_submit are authorized. Every new assertion below
belongs to this ONE joint candidate proof, not to the accepted fact graph.

## Verdict and scope

The literal same-m H2=(A,B,A) has an explicit candidate full original-space
primitive certificate: eight nonnegative ordered interpolation multipliers
and three positive adjacent-gradient squares. It proves, within this joint
draft, V_H2 >= (epsilon^2/2) sum_(i=0)^2 ||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.
No gradient is identified or deleted. This closes the previously unproved
low-d H2 stress test analytically, subject to the unavailable verifier gate.

For every compact convex body D containing0, every start, and every d>=1,
the same candidate gives U_d(T_H2 x)<=U_(d+2S2)(x), with the explicit
reserve above divided by d+2S2, plus further nonnegative terms. The
primitive certificate also gives the full smooth-convex three-step result
W_3(H2)=1/(1+2S2). This is a finite-block assertion, not an asymptotic result.

The full all-depth U law remains OPEN. A general bridge identity displays
the precise signed defect, which turns negative beginning at the H3 join.
Its magnitude is bounded by 27 epsilon C_j^2 times an actual gradient-norm
drop. The initial-maximization remainder is retained exactly. It pays the
defect on a complete, fixed-interval cap/cap family with U ratio below1/4,
even though both child primitive reserves vanish. No uniform payment for
all bodies and starts is proved, and no uniform 1+C/m loss is proved.

One substantive mechanism is used: primitive energy with its actual
projection-normal and maximization reserves. The optional second H2
mechanism is not opened because the first H2 certificate succeeds.
The original unrestricted minimax target is OPEN. No new fact_id exists.

## 1. Parameters, accepted inputs, and original rows

Fix ONE integer m>=1536 and set

 epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
 c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, s=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), S_j=sum H_j.

The symbol s in this report is the constant sqrt(2)-1, not a word mass.
For the physical H2 use A=1+a, B=1+beta, S2=2A+B.
For a general child use C_j=1+S_j and k_j=a*c^j.
All children inherit the SAME enclosing m. No function or radius resets.

For a convex differentiable 1-smooth function f, actual GD points satisfy
x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i). Retain the actual
terminal gradient as a mathematical sample, even though no extra step is
taken there. Use the accepted residual orientation

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.       (1.1)

This orientation and its necessity are in accepted1252b8823bb5a290,
proof paragraph1, which was read. For a word h with mass S define

 V_h=2 sum_(i<N) h_i(f_i-f_N)
      +sum_(i<N) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-S(S+1)||g_N||^2. (1.2)

Accepted04219554e41d9750, Sections10-11, supplies this definition, the
full original-space certificate criterion, the exact-root primitive join,
and the dominant identity J_(h,(h,S+1))=V_h. Those applicable sections,
including the coefficient audits and original-coordinate qualifications,
were read completely. Their exact-root join is NOT treated as a theorem
about shortening physical steps. The missing variant is derived below.

Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 has only the previously assigned scalar comparison
and conditional negative-reduction scope. No rate is inferred from the
affine-height representation d8b59c7d92425121. This report needs neither
as a new mathematical premise. The original normalization remains W=2R.

## 2. Exact elementary row identities

For one physical step x_v=x_u-h g_u, direct substitution in (1.1) gives

 V_(h)=-2Q_uv-2(1+h)Q_vu+(2-h^2)||g_u-g_v||^2.       (2.1)

To check every coefficient, the two rows are

 Q_uv=f_u-f_v+(1/2-h)||g_u||^2
             -<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2,
 Q_vu=f_v-f_u+||g_u||^2/2
             +(h-1)<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2.

Adding2Q_uv+2(1+h)Q_vu to V_(h) cancels both function values and
leaves diagonal coefficients2-h^2 and mixed coefficient-2(2-h^2).
Consequently the leaf is primitive for0<=h<=sqrt(2), without a product
equality assumption.

For h>0, adding the same two interpolation rows gives
Q_uv+Q_vu=||g_u-g_v||^2-h<g_u,g_u-g_v>. Therefore

 ||g_u||^2-||g_v||^2
   =(2/h-1)||g_u-g_v||^2-(2/h)(Q_uv+Q_vu).           (2.2)

This norm drop is nonnegative for0<h<=2, with its complete original-row
certificate retained. Its sign is NOT asserted for an arbitrary long step.

## 3. General off-root primitive join: the missing term

Let l,r be any nonnegative words, with capacities
P=1+sum l and C=1+sum r. They need not already be primitive for the
identity in this section. Insert the actual bridge1+t, where0<=t<C.
Write p for the bridge query index, z for the terminal index, and j for
the nonterminal right-child indices, with physical steps r_j.
Thus x_z=x_p-(1+t)g_p-sum_j r_j g_j.
Put

 T=P+C+t, K=(2P+C+t)/(C-t),
 Delta=PC-t(P+C+t), R=(P+t)Delta/(C-t),
 E=sum_j r_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+t Q_(p,z)
                                      +sum_j r_j Q_(j,z).       (3.1)

Then the exact identity, on the actual original formal space, is

 V_(l,1+t,r)=V_l+K V_r-(K-1)E
                              +R(||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2).         (3.2)

No changed trajectory at an exact-root bridge appears here. All child
energies use their actual positions in the shortened or lengthened word.

Here is the complete coefficient audit. Writing G=sum_j r_j g_j, expansion
of E cancels all mixed gradient terms. Its value part is

 2sum_j r_j f_j+(t-C)f_p+(2-C-t)f_z,

and its gradient part is

 sum_j r_j(1-r_j)||g_j||^2
       +[(C-t)/2-t^2]||g_p||^2+(C+t)||g_z||^2/2.    (3.3)

For example, in sum r_j Q_(j,p) the position and squared-difference
terms combine to t<G,g_p>+||G||^2/2
+sum r_j(1-r_j)||g_j||^2/2+(C-1)||g_p||^2/2.
The analogous sum r_j Q_(j,z) contributes -||G||^2/2
+sum r_j(1-r_j)||g_j||^2/2-<G,g_z>+(C-1)||g_z||^2/2.
Q_(z,p) contributes t<g_z,g_p>+<G,g_z>
+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2, and t Q_(p,z) contributes
-t(t+1/2)||g_p||^2-t<g_p,G>+t||g_z||^2/2-t<g_p,g_z>.
These four displayed expressions sum to (3.3).

The choice (K-1)(C-t)=2(P+t) matches every value coefficient in (3.2).
After that cancellation the two remaining gradient coefficients in
V_parent-V_l-K V_r+(K-1)E are

 P^2-Kt^2=R,
 KC^2-T^2=-R.                                      (3.4)

The first equality follows by multiplying by C-t and factoring
(P+t)[PC-t(P+C+t)]; the second follows since the two expressions sum to0.
This proves (3.2) with every value, gradient, and mass term accounted for.

At Delta=0, one recovers the accepted exact-root identity, including
K=(T/C)^2. Off that root, discarding R is unjustified. If Delta>=0
and the actual bridge-plus-right-child history has a certified gradient
norm drop, (3.2) does give a valid shortened-bridge primitive certificate.
The following application proves that drop from two actual short steps.

## 4. The literal H2: signs and complete multiplier array

Set C=1+A=2+a in this section, t=beta, and define

 K=(3C+beta)/(C-beta),
 Delta=C^2-beta(2C+beta), R=(C+beta)Delta/(C-beta),
 delta_A=2-A^2.                                    (4.1)

All these parameters refer to the ACTUAL H2=(A,B,A).
One has1<A<sqrt(2), 1<B<2, C>beta, K>1, delta_A>0.
The sign of Delta needs the actual bridge comparison, not just B<2.
Using a=s(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and2s+s^2=1 gives

 s C-beta=epsilon(1+epsilon-2s^2)/(1+epsilon)>0,
 Delta=(s C-beta)(lambda C+beta)>0.                 (4.2)

This proves beta<s C without changing either leaf step.

For original indices0,1,2,3, equation (3.2) becomes

 V_H2=V_(A;0,1)+K V_(A;2,3)
       -(K-1)(A Q_21+Q_31+beta Q_13+A Q_23)
       +R(||g_1||^2-||g_3||^2).                    (4.3)

Apply (2.2) to the actual bridge B between1,2 and to the actual last
leaf A between2,3, then use (2.1) on the two leaves. The result is

 V_H2=-sum_(i!=j) Lambda_ij Q_ij
        +d0||g_0-g_1||^2+d1||g_1-g_2||^2
                              +d2||g_2-g_3||^2,    (4.4)

where

 d0=delta_A,
 d1=R(2/B-1)=R epsilon/B,
 d2=K delta_A+R(2/A-1),                             (4.5)

and the complete nonzero multiplier list is

 Lambda_01=2,
 Lambda_10=2(1+A),
 Lambda_12=2R/B,
 Lambda_21=(K-1)A+2R/B,
 Lambda_13=(K-1)beta,
 Lambda_31=K-1,
 Lambda_23=2K+(K-1)A+2R/A,
 Lambda_32=2K(1+A)+2R/A.                            (4.6)

All other entries, including every diagonal entry, are zero. Every entry
in (4.6) and every coefficient in (4.5) is positive. The full remainder
is the four-gradient path quadratic, with matrix

 [ d0,    -d0,       0,     0;
  -d0, d0+d1,     -d1,     0;
    0,    -d1,  d1+d2,   -d2;
    0,      0,    -d2,    d2 ].                     (4.7)

It is PSD on the entire original four-gradient space because its
quadratic form is exactly the three squared differences in (4.4).
Its kernel is the constant-gradient subspace; no equality among those
formal gradients has been imposed. The original initial-position row is
zero, consistently with the primitive criterion in the accepted fact.

As an independent flow audit, the function coefficients in
-sum Lambda_ij Q_ij are (2A,2B,2A,-2S2). For the bridge coefficient,
-2A+(K-1)(C-beta)=-2A+2(C+beta)=2B. The last nonterminal
coefficient is -2(K-1)A+2KA=2A. Thus no function-value term is hidden.

There is a simple uniform reserve. Since epsilon<1, s>1/4, and
A=sqrt(2)-2s epsilon/(1+epsilon),

 delta_A=[2s epsilon/(1+epsilon)](sqrt(2)+A)>epsilon/2.

Since s<1/2, (4.2) gives sC-beta>epsilon/4 and lambda C+beta>4.
Hence Delta>epsilon and R>epsilon. With B<2, this proves

 d0>=epsilon^2/2, d1>=epsilon^2/2, d2>=epsilon^2/2,
 V_H2 >= (epsilon^2/2)sum_(i=0)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2. (4.8)

The bounds are deliberately conservative; they are symbolic inequalities,
not arithmetic or numerical evaluations.

## 5. Projection bodies, optimum normalization, and V exactly

Let D be any nonempty compact convex subset of a finite-dimensional
Euclidean space containing0. Define

 P_D(x)=argmin_(g in D)||x-g||^2,
 F_D(x)=max_(g in D)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
 U_d(x)=max_(g in D)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2), d>0,
 T_h(x)=x-h P_D(x).                                (5.1)

Squared-distance minimization along every segment in D gives
<x-P_D(x),g-P_D(x)><=0 for every g in D; expansion proves sufficiency.
Adding this condition for two projections gives
||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2<=<x-y,P_D(x)-P_D(y)>.
Thus P_D is1-Lipschitz. Completing the square gives the unique maximizer
P_D(x) in F_D. The maximizing inequalities bound the first-order remainder
by ||t||||P_D(x+t)-P_D(x)||<=||t||^2, proving grad F_D=P_D.
Therefore F_D is finite, convex, differentiable, and1-smooth.

The minimum is0 and the complete minimizer set is

 K_D={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in D}.                  (5.2)

If z is in K_D, all candidates for F_D(z) are nonpositive and g=0 attains0.
If <z,g>>0 for some g, sufficiently small positive multiples of g belong
to D and give positive objective. This proves both directions.
For every z_star in K_D and every M>0, completion of the square gives

 U_M(x)<=||x-z_star||^2/M.                           (5.3)

Indeed2<x,g>-M||g||^2<=2<x-z_star,g>-M||g||^2
<=||x-z_star||^2/M for each g in D. This bound uses the true minimizer
distance, including when0 is not a closest minimizer.

For an actual word trajectory, write x=x_0, y=x_N, g_i=P_D(x_i).
Here f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, so telescoping the actual squared
distances in (1.2) gives the EXACT identity

 V_h=||x||^2-||y+S P_D(y)||^2.                      (5.4)

In detail, ||x||^2-||y||^2=2sum h_i<x_i,g_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2.
Substitution in V_h leaves this expression minus2S f_N
minus S(S+1)||g_N||^2, which is (5.4). No s-composability definition
or equality between mass and product is invoked.

Also every original finite row becomes
Q_ij=<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><=0. All rows in (4.6) are therefore actual
same-body projection normals, including the two nonlocal terminal rows.

## 6. Exact U gap with the actual maximizer remainder

For any complete word of mass S and any d>=1, put

 M=d+2S, q=P_D(y/d), g=P_D(y), p0=P_D(x/M),
 zbar=[x-y+(d+S)q]/M,
 Rmax=M||p0-zbar||^2+2<x-Mp0,p0-zbar>.               (6.1)

The point zbar is in D: x-y=sum h_i g_i, and the nonnegative weights
h_i and d+S sum to M. All these points use the same fixed body.
Projection optimality at p0 gives Rmax>=0, term by term.

There is the exact identity requested in the assignment:

 U_M(x)-U_d(y)
   =[||x||^2-||y+S q||^2]/M+Rmax.                  (6.2)

To verify it, the difference between the initial maximum at p0 and its
value at zbar is exactly Rmax. Moreover x-M zbar=y-(d+S)q. Expanding
the value at zbar and subtracting2<y,q>-d||q||^2 gives the numerator
||x||^2-||y||^2-2S<y,q>-S^2||q||^2, divided by M.

For1<=d1<d2, let q_i=P_D(y/d_i). Comparing each maximizer with the
other feasible point gives

 d1(||q_1||^2-||q_2||^2)
 <=2<y,q_1-q_2><=d2(||q_1||^2-||q_2||^2).

The squared-norm difference must be nonnegative, since otherwise the
displayed lower bound would exceed its upper bound. The inner-product
difference is then nonnegative too. Consequently

 Lrad=2S<y,g-q>+S^2(||g||^2-||q||^2)>=0,
 U_M(x)-U_d(y)=(V_h+Lrad)/M+Rmax.                   (6.3)

Combining the explicit original-row identity (4.4) with (6.3) gives
the complete candidate H2 equality and lower bound

 U_(d+2S2)(x)-U_d(T_H2 x)
   =[-sum Lambda_ij Q_ij+sum_(i=0)^2 d_i||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2
                                                    +Lrad]/(d+2S2)+Rmax
   >= [epsilon^2/(2(d+2S2))]sum_(i=0)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.       (6.4)

This holds at arbitrary starts, arbitrary finite dimension, and EVERY
d>=1, including the low-d regime left unpaid previously. It also retains
the actual maximization and radial reserves rather than silently spending
them twice. At d=1, U_1=2F_D, so (5.3) gives

 2F_D(T_H2 x)<=dist(x,K_D)^2/(1+2S2).                (6.5)

Scaling f=L F_D and using steps h/L preserves the trajectory and the W=2R
normalization. The projection-class implication is correctly normalized.

## 7. Finite H2 full-function consequence, without a class restriction

The certificate (4.4) is valid for all convex1-smooth f, not only F_D.
Translate a selected minimizer to0 and subtract its value. Let
u=(h_0,...,h_(N-1),S+1), whose sum is Dmass=1+2S.
The accepted identity from04219554e41d9750 is

 V_h=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2Dmass f_N
                                      -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.       (7.1)

Its direct audit is also short: the last vector is y-(S+1)g_N;
expanding the terminal optimum row cancels its mixed term, and the
remaining expression is (1.2) after telescoping squared distances.
The weights and -Q_(i,star) are nonnegative. Thus V_H2>=0 proves
W_3(H2)<=1/(1+2S2), uniformly over the full original function class.

The matching lower example is the scalar Huber function with threshold
tau=1/(1+2S2): f(t)=t^2/2 for |t|<=tau and
f(t)=tau|t|-tau^2/2 outside. It is convex,1-smooth, and uniquely minimized
at0. Start at1. Every query is in the positive linear region since every
prefix mass is at most S2 and1-S2*tau=(1+S2)/(1+2S2)>=tau.
Thus x_3=1-S2*tau and2f(x_3)=2tau-(2S2+1)tau^2=tau.
This proves the finite equality claimed in the verdict, within this draft.
It supplies no all-depth schedule bound.

## 8. Higher joins: explicit signed debt and its scale

Keep the SAME enclosing m. For the join forming H_(j+1), put
C=C_j=1+S_j, k=k_j=a*c^j, and T=2C+k. Its mass is T-1.
The general identity (3.2) has

 K_j=(3C+k)/(C-k),
 Delta_j=C^2-k(2C+k), R_j=(C+k)Delta_j/(C-k),
 V_parent=V_left+K_j V_right-(K_j-1)E_j
                             +R_j(||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2).       (8.1)

Here E_j is precisely (3.1), on the actual two child histories and bridge.
All its weights are nonnegative. No radius or child parameters reset.

The needed scalar mass facts can be derived symbolically. From the word
definition C_(j+1)=2C_j+a*c^j and C_0=1. With v=a/(c-2),

 C_j=(1-v)2^j+v c^j, 0<v<1,
 k_j/C_j=a/[v+(1-v)(2/c)^j] <c-2.                  (8.2)

Substitution proves the displayed closed form. Since c>lambda>2 and
c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta, one has0<a<c-2 and hence0<v<1.
The ratio in (8.2) increases with j. Also lambda<49/20 and
epsilon<1/100 give c<5/2, so k_j/C_j<1/2 and K_j<7.

At j=1 the H2 comparison is Delta_1>0 from (4.2). At j=2,
C_2=5-epsilon+2s(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and k_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),
so, using lambda=5s+2s^2,

 k_2-s C_2=epsilon[s+4s^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda epsilon]>0.       (8.3)

Indeed epsilon<1/8, s>2/5, and lambda<5/2 imply
lambda epsilon<5/16<2/5<s. Monotonicity in (8.2) then gives
Delta_j<0 for EVERY j>=2. Thus the primitive sign genuinely changes
at the H3 join, not at H2.

The magnitude is uniformly controlled. Write r=k_j/C_j; then
s<r<s+lambda epsilon, and

 0<-Delta_j/C_j^2=2r+r^2-1
   <2sqrt(2)lambda epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2<9epsilon.

The last estimate uses sqrt(2)<3/2, lambda<5/2, epsilon<1/8.
Since (C+k)/(C-k)<3, it follows that

 0<-R_j<27epsilon C_j^2.                            (8.4)

For actual gradients the negative contribution in (8.1) is therefore
bounded below by

 -27epsilon C_j^2 (||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2)_+,

where t_+=max(t,0). This estimate quantifies the debt; it does not pay it.
The factor C_j^2 and the uncontrolled actual endpoint norm drop cannot be
suppressed inside an unspecified o(1) or a claimed uniform1+C/m loss.

## 9. An actual whole-parent payment by the retained remainder

This section rederives the previously found cap/cap diagnostic, then
computes its actual remainder explicitly. The family itself is NOT claimed
as a new counterexample. Its precise payment in (6.2) is the new use here.

Fix2<=j<m, write C=C_j, k=k_j, r=k/C, T=2C+k, P=T-1, and use ONE
static interval D=[-r,1], with initial point x=C. Its F_D is a legal
convex1-smooth function uniquely minimized at0 because0 is interior to D.

Throughout the complete left H_j, an actual prefix of mass v<=C-1
has position C-v>=1 and projection1. Its endpoint is1. The bridge1+k
sends it to-k. Throughout the complete right H_j, a prefix of mass
v<=C-1 has position-k+vr<=-r and projection-r. The final point is-r.
All queries, both children, and the last physical leaf are present.
Every prefix assertion follows from nonnegative physical steps; no
trajectory or recurrence was executed on a machine.

Both child primitive residues are zero by (5.4): their shifted endpoints
equal their respective inputs. Every row of E_j is zero: the bridge
normal at1 is zero, the terminal normal at-r is zero, and every right
gradient equals-r. Nevertheless the parent has

 V_parent=C^2-T^2r^2=R_j(1-r^2)<0.                 (9.1)

For the second equality, multiply the definition of R_j by1-k^2/C^2
and use C^2+kT=(C+k)^2. This exhibits actual failure of primitive gluing
from child signs or their adjacent-gradient reserves alone.

Now retain the actual U points, for ANY d>=1. Set M=d+2P. Since C/M<1,

 p0=C/M, q=-r/d, zbar=(C-P r/d)/M,
 x-Mp0=0,
 Rmax=P^2 r^2/(d^2 M).                             (9.2)

The final scaled projection is inside the same interval, including its
boundary at d=1. These formulas are actual maximizers, not chosen reserves.
Substitution into (6.2) yields

 [C^2-r^2(1+P/d)^2]/M+P^2r^2/(d^2M)
       =C^2/M-r^2/d
       =U_M(C)-U_d(-r).                            (9.3)

Thus the auxiliary square pays the real negative primitive defect at d=1;
for larger d the exact radial adjustment in (6.3) is also present.

This payment has a uniform margin on this entire endpoint-cap family.
Since a>1/3 and beta>9/10, C_2=4+2a+beta>5, so C_j>5 for j>=2.
Here a>1/3 follows from beta>9/10 and c<5/2. With r<1/2 and
P=2C+k-1<(5/2)C-1,

 U_d(-r)/U_M(C)=r^2 M/(d C^2)
   <(5C-1)/(4C^2)<1/4.                            (9.4)

The last inequality follows from C^2-5C+1>0 for C>5.
This proves an actual whole-parent gap greater than3/4 of its initial U
on the stated family. It does NOT prove such a margin on arbitrary starts
or bodies. In particular it cannot be used as a blanket lower bound on
Rmax in an unrelated trajectory.

## 10. Remaining interface and tested failures

First direct attempt: extend primitive positivity from H2 by child
nonnegativity and their square reserves. Equations (8.1) and (9.1)
decisively refute that general-body strengthening. Even two perfectly
legal children with every adjacent-gradient reserve equal to0 can have
negative parent V. This is consistent with the earlier H3 counterexample.

Second direct attempt: retain the exact initial-maximization remainder
and quantify the parent payment. Equations (6.2), (8.4), and (9.2)-(9.4)
give respectively the exact interface, the uniform size of its signed
primitive term, and a complete payment on a legal diagnostic family.
They do not give a lower bound for Rmax or the radial term in every
same-body trajectory. No counterexample to the desired U law was found.

For the first unclosed parent H3, both actual H2 children now have the
explicit original-row reserves (4.4). The remaining arbitrary-start
inequality is a comparison of those reserves and all the retained
nonlocal normals, radial correction, and initial-maximization remainder
against (-R_2)(||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2). It is an unproved inequality, not a
result obtained merely by displaying the exact identity. At higher
depths the child V themselves may be negative and also need a genuinely
quantified U invariant. An unknown SDP or an exponential cell list has
not been substituted for that missing estimate.

The new H2 certificate is stronger than a projection-only calibration,
but its finite three-step full-class consequence is not a full-class
all-depth rate. Nor can any all-body height-zero projection result be
silently extended through the accepted affine-height representation.
Every eventual full-function estimate still needs its uniform body,
height, dimension, start, and enclosing-m quantifiers.

## 11. Retrieval, honesty, and allocation closeout

Skills applied: query-memory, direct-proving, search-math-results, and
construct-counterexamples. The verify-proof instructions were read to
check the gate, but no submission is permitted in this allocation.
All new steps have therefore been kept inside this joint unverified draft.

Search context: mature_subproblem; active program actual primitive reserve
and U gluing; missing mechanism shortening with an exact reserve, followed
by uniform actual-remainder payment; modes repair and analogy.
Matlas returned a self-contracted-sequence statement requiring steps<=1/L
or backtracking, a continuous constrained shadow-flow theorem, and a

Web background retrieval located Anytime Acceleration of Gradient Descent,
arXiv:2411.17668. The source concerns exact concatenation; it is not used
to infer shortening. No external mathematical theorem is imported.

The attempted HTML URL https://arxiv.org/html/2411.17668v3 returned
`curl: (22) The requested URL returned error: 404`. A public PDF was then
downloaded successfully from
https://yuxinchen2020.github.io/publications/Anytime_AGD.pdf
to downloads/Anytime_AGD_round79.pdf. Extraction encountered
`pdftotext: command not found`; pypdf is also not installed. No installation
was attempted. The downloaded PDF is not claimed to have been extracted
or read. The needed exact proof was already fully available in accepted

no conclusion relies on the unextracted paper or a search-result snippet.

An initial combined recall exceeded the output budget. Relevant TASK,
latest guidance, accepted definitions and proof sections, and previous

The assigned one invocation79 is now documented. Zero mathematical

# Quantitative queried cuts of full objective certificates

Status: complete self-contained candidate calculations, NOT verifier-accepted.
Current TASK/master_guidance closes routine verification and numerical search;
neither a solver nor fact_submit is used. Every new calculation below belongs
to this single proof package, not to a chain of unverified premises. The only
accepted mathematical dependency is fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, for the exact
normalized problem, interpolation necessity, and attained full certificates.

## 1. The unchanged target and the full certificate hypothesis

For a finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), W_n(h) is the
supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite
dimension, convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer
x_star, and x_0 != x_star, using x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i).
The exact benchmark is U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, with the original
phi/psi recursions in accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d`. The affirmative
target remains: for every epsilon>0, all sufficiently large n and EVERY
nonnegative length-n schedule have W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n. A negative
answer requires a fixed positive relative improvement along unbounded
horizons by actual full-objective upper bounds. Neither outcome is claimed.

Work at smoothness one and let x_0,g_0,...,g_n be independent formal vectors.
Set x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All finite f_i
are independent formal scalars. Put I={star,0,...,n}, lambda_ii=0, and

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

A supplied full certificate consists of a finite real q_cert and ACTUAL
lambda_ij>=0 for which

 M=q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij                 (1)

has every value coefficient zero and is PSD on the full original formal
space. In particular the finite-node flows obey

 out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n).                                         (2)

No optimum-source edge is forbidden. No support, symmetrizability,
positive-definiteness, h_i<2, or Huber equality assumption is made.
Zero-step gradients remain independent.

Necessity of Q_ij<=0 on legal instances (accepted1252b8823bb5a290) gives
W_n(h)<=q_cert for any such certificate. Conversely, that SAME accepted
fact supplies a full certificate at q_cert=W_n(h) for every nonnegative
h, including empty/all-zero schedules, by positive-step attainment and
explicit zero-step lifting. Thus any universal necessary inequality proved
below can be applied to at least one attained certificate at the actual W.
An inequality involving the multipliers of a particular supplied certificate
is not by itself a multiplier-free lower bound on W.

Every feasible q_cert is positive. Setting all gradients to zero first
gives q_cert>=0. Summing (2) yields
sum_i lambda_(i,star)=2+sum_i lambda_(star,i)>=2. If q_cert=0, set all
finite gradients to the same scalar1. The coefficient of scalar x_0 in
M is then -sum_i lambda_(i,star)<0, so M is unbounded below. This excludes0.

## 2. A nontrivial queried-cut capacity inequality

Fix a queried index j<n, write b_cut=h_j, and let T_cut={j+1,...,n}.
Its complement in I includes the optimum and indices0,...,j. Define

 s_tail=sum_(j<t<n)h_t,
 ell_tail(k)=sum_(j<t<k)h_t for k in T_cut,
 C_in=sum_(i outside T_cut,k in T_cut)lambda_ik,
 C_out=sum_(i in T_cut,k outside T_cut)lambda_ik,
 F_cut=sum_(k in T_cut)lambda_(j,k),
 B_cut=sum_(k in T_cut)lambda_(k,j),
 I_cut=sum_(i!=j)lambda_(i,j),
 J_forward=sum_(k in T_cut)ell_tail(k) lambda_(j,k),
 J_in=sum_(i outside T_cut,k in T_cut)ell_tail(k) lambda_ik,
 mu_query=lambda_(j,star),
 mu_tail=sum_(i in T_cut)lambda_(i,star).

These are actual array sums, including every remote edge and optimum edge.
Summing (2) over T_cut gives C_out-C_in=2. Set

 A_cut=I_cut-b_cut F_cut,
 D_cut=1+2s_tail+C_in+J_in,
 K_cut=b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward.                       (3)

The following matrix is necessarily PSD:

              [ q_cert       -mu_query/2       -mu_tail/2 ]
 T_query   =  [ -mu_query/2    A_cut             K_cut/2   ].    (4)
              [ -mu_tail/2    K_cut/2            D_cut     ]

Proof. Evaluate (1) on the formal subspace

 x_0=z,  g_i=0 (i<j),  g_j=u,  g_i=v (i>j).

All value coefficients have already canceled, so they may be set to0.
Only finite-source edges to star contribute z; their total is
-<z,mu_query*u+mu_tail*v>. The coefficient of ||u||^2 in the norm
terms is (out_j+in_j)/2=I_cut, since j<n. The only additional diagonal
terms are -h_j lambda_(j,k)||u||^2 for k>j. This gives A_cut.

For the ||v||^2 coefficient, set u=z=0. A tail point k then has
x_k=-ell_tail(k)v, and a non-tail finite point has position0. Within-tail
position terms sum to

 sum_(k in T_cut)ell_tail(k)(out_k^internal-in_k^internal).

Use (2) to replace the internal divergence by
2*1_(k=n)-out_k^cross+in_k^cross. The outgoing cross position terms
add back sum_k ell_tail(k)out_k^cross. This leaves
2s_tail+J_in. The cross norm terms are
(C_out+C_in)/2=1+C_in; internal norm terms are zero. Hence D_cut.

For the coefficient of <u,v>, norm terms give -(F_cut+B_cut).
An edge j->k with k in T_cut contributes -ell_tail(k)<u,v>, totaling
-J_forward. An edge sourced in T_cut and targeting its complement
has the position shift +b_cut*u, totaling b_cut C_out<u,v>.
All other position terms have either zero source gradient or cancel
the common tail shift. These are exactly K_cut. Thus the restriction is

 q_cert||z||^2-<z,mu_query*u+mu_tail*v>
                   +A_cut||u||^2+K_cut<u,v>+D_cut||v||^2,

whose scalar coefficient matrix is (4). Since the original polynomial
is PSD on every formal vector, its restriction is PSD. This restriction
is a necessary test, not an assertion that its formal vectors are legal
trajectories, and it deletes no edge from the original certificate.

Complete the independent z square, using q_cert>0. Put

 a_cut=A_cut-mu_query^2/(4q_cert),
 d_cut=D_cut-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert),
 k_cut=K_cut-mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert).

Then (4) is equivalent to

 a_cut>=0, d_cut>=0, k_cut^2<=4a_cut*d_cut.                     (5)

In particular, when d_cut>0 the quantitative incidence cost is

 I_cut >= b_cut F_cut + mu_query^2/(4q_cert)
              + [b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward
                       -mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert)]^2/(4d_cut). (6)

When d_cut=0 the square numerator in (6) must be zero and the remaining
condition is I_cut>=b_cut F_cut+mu_query^2/(4q_cert). There is no division
by a vanished residual and no strict-PSD assumption. Equivalently,

 |b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward
                     -mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert)|
       <=2 sqrt((I_cut-b_cut F_cut-mu_query^2/(4q_cert))
                 *(1+2s_tail+C_in+J_in-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert))).   (7)

If every edge incident to j vanishes, I_cut=F_cut=B_cut=J_forward=
mu_query=0. Then (5) forces b_cut C_out=0. Since C_out>=2, b_cut=0.
This recovers the qualitative incidence obstruction, now as the zero-cost
case of an exact quantitative condition at every finite certificate level.
If b_cut=0 there is no contradiction; the zero-step case is included.

For an alternative exact q cost define

 Z_cut=[ A_cut K_cut/2 ; K_cut/2 D_cut ],
 m_cut=(mu_query,mu_tail)^T.

Condition (4) is equivalent to Z_cut>=0, m_cut in Range(Z_cut), and

 q_cert >= (1/4) m_cut^T Z_cut^+ m_cut.                         (8)

Here Z_cut^+ inverts its positive eigenvalues and vanishes on its kernel.
For proof, diagonalize the real symmetric Z_cut. A component of m_cut
on a zero eigenvector would give an unbounded linear polynomial in that
free direction. Once these components vanish, complete one square for
each positive eigenvalue. This proves necessity and sufficiency, including
all singular cases. If 4A_cut D_cut-K_cut^2>0, the right side is explicitly

 (D_cut*mu_query^2-K_cut*mu_query*mu_tail+A_cut*mu_tail^2)
                         /(4A_cut D_cut-K_cut^2).             (9)

## 3. Why total cut flow cannot replace its position moment

Here is ONE exact supplied certificate. Take n=2, h=(1,1), q_cert=1/5,
with the following nonzero multipliers and every other multiplier zero:

 lambda_02=1, lambda_10=1, lambda_21=2,
 lambda_(1,star)=1, lambda_(2,star)=1.                          (10)

All coefficients are nonnegative. The finite flows are respectively0,0,2.
With x_1=x_0-g_0 and x_2=x_0-g_0-g_1, direct expansion of (1) gives

 M=(1/5)||x_0-(5/2)(g_1+g_2)||^2
                                      +(3/4)||g_1-g_2||^2.   (11)

For an explicit coefficient check, the sum of the position terms in (10)
is -<x_0,g_1+g_2>-||g_0||^2+<g_0,g_1>+<g_0,g_2>
+3<g_1,g_2>. The norm terms add ||g_0||^2+2||g_1||^2+2||g_2||^2
-<g_0,g_1>-<g_0,g_2>-2<g_1,g_2>. Their sum is exactly the expansion
of (11) after adding q_cert||x_0||^2. This is full PSD with the
original independent g_0 present as a kernel direction.

For j=0, T_cut={1,2}, the exact data are

 b_cut=1, s_tail=1, C_in=1, C_out=3,
 F_cut=B_cut=I_cut=1, J_forward=J_in=1,
 mu_query=0, mu_tail=2,
 A_cut=0, D_cut=5, K_cut=0.

Thus (4) is the singular rank-one square (z-5v)^2/5, and (8) gives
the exact cost1/5. Dropping the target-position moment J_in would assert

 mu_tail^2 <= 4q_cert(1+2s_tail+C_in),                          (12)

which here reads4<=16/5, false. Equivalently, that proposed scalar cost
would falsely require q_cert>=1/4. Dropping J_forward from K_cut likewise
produces K_cut=1 while A_cut=0, falsely rejecting the same full certificate.
Therefore unweighted cross-cut flow cannot be substituted in these exact
capacity tests. The actual remote edge0->2, whose target lies one unit
of step mass into the tail, supplies the missing moment. This refutes
(12), not every conceivable scalar invariant or an existential re-selection.


## 7. The correct mass-only relaxation, with its explicit loss

One CAN remove the actual first moments at a single cut if their loss is
paid explicitly. Nonnegative h and lambda imply

 0<=J_forward<=s_tail F_cut,
 0<=J_in-J_forward<=s_tail(C_in-F_cut).

In particular the valid scalar tail bound is

 q_cert>=mu_tail^2/[4(1+2s_tail+(1+s_tail)C_in)].               (24)

Proof: in(4), the z,v principal minor gives mu_tail^2<=4q_cert D_cut,
and D_cut<=1+2s_tail+(1+s_tail)C_in. The supplied certificate(10) has
equality in(24). Thus(24), which pays the full possible elapsed mass,
survives that example whereas the unweighted replacement(12) does not.

There is also a closed elimination of the TWO moment bounds from the
coupled query test. Write a=a_cut from(5) only within this paragraph, and
define the scalar data

 R_mom=b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert),
 E_mom=1+2s_tail+C_in+s_tail(C_in-F_cut)-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert),
 U_mom=s_tail F_cut.

Every true certificate necessarily satisfies

 a>=0, E_mom+R_mom+a>=0,
 dist(R_mom+2a,[0,U_mom])^2<=4a(E_mom+R_mom+a).              (25)

Here dist denotes the ordinary distance of a real number from the stated
closed interval. For proof put t=J_forward. Then0<=t<=U_mom and
d_cut<=E_mom+t. Equation(5) gives

 (R_mom-t)^2<=4a(E_mom+t), E_mom+t>=0.

Completing the scalar square gives
(t-(R_mom+2a))^2<=4a(E_mom+R_mom+a). For a>0, this is possible
for t in the interval exactly when the last two conditions in(25) hold;
the square inequality itself forces E_mom+t>=0. For a=0 the same
conditions require t=R_mom in[0,U_mom] and E_mom+R_mom>=0, which is
the correct zero-residual rule. This proves(25) including degeneracy.

Conversely(25) is sufficient for the ABSTRACT moment relaxation of(5):
choose such a t and set J_forward=t, J_in=t+s_tail(C_in-F_cut).
These values satisfy the two displayed interval bounds and yield(5).
No claim is made that those abstract moments arise from actual target
indices or a common array across several cuts. It is precisely that
cross-cut realizability/correlation information that(25) relaxes.
The scalar relaxation is therefore a genuine necessary inequality,
not a constant-preserving F/G separator or an exact iterative closure.
