# One paid first inner reversal and an actual-energy child state

**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED REPORT.** Project gd-exact-profile-20260926,
worker max3, invocation83 after ended82; completed ceiling83 / attempted84,
controller cap1. Operative TASK.md and master_guidance cadd37d70aded16f;
plan eab346254c0666ea.

No fact_submit, formal verification, mathematical program, numerical or
arithmetic replay, executed trajectory/recurrence, solver, optimizer,
benchmark DP, installation, consult, or subagent was used. This report is
one joint proof draft. Every ingredient used below is proved within it.
No earlier unverified finding is a correctness premise.

**Outcome.** For every positive first inner reversing level J, there is a
complete full-word objective bound strictly below 4/(5D) for every start
whose FIRST complete H_J ends in the closed core. All starts of radius
at most v Q automatically meet that condition. The first reversing
bridge is allowed to leave the core; its entire following H_J is retained.
After that complete child, an actual radius/gradient/energy state proves
that every remaining physical step stays in the core. The original
crossing price is paid once, and the complete quadratic suffix is kept.

This is a restricted trajectory theorem, NOT an all-start odd-family
bound. Starts for which the first H_J ends outside the core remain an
unresolved interface. The whole odd-family rate and the original
all-function minimax problem remain OPEN. The second, capacity-amplitude
mechanism is not activated in this allocation.

## 1. Fixed data and the precise scoped result

Fix ONE integer m>=1536. Every child uses these SAME parameters:

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, h_j^lev=1+d_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,h_j^lev,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,
    D=1+2S_m, n=2^m-1,
    ell=1+a, theta=a*beta.

Here h_i denotes a chronological physical entry of H_m when no level
superscript is present. d_m is auxiliary, not an extra physical step.

Fix 0<kappa<=1 and finite B>=kappa, and keep the ONE function

    v=1-kappa, u=v+B,
    G(r)=min(B,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,
    g(x)=sign(x)*G(|x|), g(0)=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, F(r)=f(r), r>=0.

Let Psi_j(s) be the actual output of the complete word H_j on this
fixed function from s. Its exact interval radius is

    M_j(R)=max_(|s|<=R)|Psi_j(s)|, R>=0.

For the theorem below assume that

    J=min{j in {0,...,m-1}: kappa*(1+d_j)>1}
    exists and 1<=J<=m-1.                              (1.1)

Thus every physical core step in H_J is nonreversing. Define

    Q=(c-1)/(c-2)=1+1/(c-2),
    t=kappa*d_J, q=kappa*(1+d_J)-1=t-v,
    L_J=S_J/d_J, N=m-J-1,
    K_fr(z)=z for 0<=z<=1, K_fr(z)=exp(z-1) for z>=1,
    Z_fr=K_fr(q)*exp(-L_J*t).                                (1.2)

K_fr is only a scalar comparison function, not a new gradient law.

For a nonzero actual initial point s put R=|s| and

    y=|Psi_J(s)|.

**Scoped full-word theorem (joint unverified).** If y<=1, then the actual
trajectory under the complete H_m obeys

    2f(Psi_m(s))/R^2 < 4/(5D).                          (1.3)

If the initial H_J contains no strict sign crossing, the stronger bound
1/(2D) holds. A zero hit gives zero payoff.

All 0<R<=v Q satisfy y<=1, hence satisfy (1.3), uniformly in B, kappa,
m and the positive level J. The initial H_J may have any number of
exterior crossings before it enters the core. It may enter the core
on its actual last leaf, including by a same-side middle-to-core step.

For the complete first block H_(J+1), let r_(J+1) be its terminal radius.
The proof supplies the actual-energy state

    r_(J+1)<=y Z_fr,    Z_fr<1/4,
    kappa*d_(J+1)*r_(J+1)<25/32,
    G(r_(J+1))=kappa*r_(J+1),
    2F(r_(J+1))=kappa*r_(J+1)^2.                       (1.4)

The first reversing bridge may have landed outside radius1. That
excursion is proved to return during its following COMPLETE H_J.
It is not treated as a quadratic trajectory.

For each later complete initial block H_l, J+1<=l<=m, its actual
terminal radius r_l remains in the core and satisfies

    kappa*d_l*r_l
      <(25/32)*beta^(2(l-J-1)),
    r_l<=y Z_fr*theta^(l-J-1),
    G(r_l)=kappa*r_l, 2F(r_l)=kappa*r_l^2.            (1.5)

Every intervening physical prefix also remains in the core after
completion of that first H_(J+1). The exact final suffix formula is

    |Psi_m(s)|
      =r_(J+1)*product_(l=J+1)^(m-1)(q_l*P_l),
    q_l=kappa*(1+d_l)-1,
    P_l=product_(h in H_l)|1-kappa*h|.                 (1.6)

An empty product is1. All factors and every final physical leaf are
present. These are actual objective and gradient values, not merely
an absolute gradient cap.

The two excluded cases J=0 and no physical inner reversal are not
claimed as new domains here. Prior drafts cover specified portions of
them; those prior statements are not needed below.

## 2. Legality, the true energy, and ordered interpolation rows

Direct integration gives

    2F(r)=kappa*r^2,                          0<=r<=1,
    2F(r)=(r-v)^2+kappa*v,                    1<=r<=u,
    2F(r)=2B*r-[B^2+v*(2B-kappa)],            r>=u.    (2.1)

At r=1 the first two values equal kappa. At r=u the latter two
values equal B^2+kappa*v. Their derivatives also agree at the knots.
B=kappa collapses the middle interval; kappa=1 makes v=0.

The derivative g is continuous, nondecreasing, and has slopes kappa,
1 and0 on its pieces. Partitioning an arbitrary interval at its knots
proves |g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. Thus f is finite, convex, differentiable,
and globally1-smooth. Since G(r)>0 for r>0, its unique minimizer is0,
and the true initial distance is R. The objective normalization is W=2R
in the project's notation: our payoff is 2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2.

For x<y let Delta=g(y)-g(x). Then 0<=Delta<=y-x and

    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)),  x<=t<=y.

Integration proves both directed smooth-convex remainders are at least
Delta^2/2. Hence for EVERY pair of actual physical, terminal, or optimum
samples (x_i,g_i,f_i),

    f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.          (2.2)

Repeated positions have identical values and gradients. The full scalar
Gram matrix is positive semidefinite because each of its quadratic
forms is a square of a real linear combination of the actual vectors.
No formal terminal gradient or interpolation pair is removed.

For advertised smoothness L_adv>0, the objective L_adv*f with steps
h_i/L_adv has the same trajectory and normalized payoff.

We use the elementary lower bound

    G(r)>=kappa*min(r,1), r>=0.                       (2.3)

Inside the core it is equality. Outside the core, both B and
max(kappa*r,r-v) are at least kappa. This lower bound does not replace
the actual law, its cap, or its kink.

## 3. Exact interval map and branch ties

For a physical h>=1 put d=h-1 and q_h=|1-h*kappa|. The exact interval
image radius is

    E_h(R)=max{
      q_h*min(R,1),
      [d*min(R,u)-h*v]_+,
      [R-h*B]_+
    },                                               (3.1)

where [z]_+=max(z,0).

For a positive input radius r the signed update is

    (1-h*kappa)*r,                 0<=r<=1,
    h*v-d*r,                      1<=r<=u,
    r-h*B,                        r>=u.

At r=1 the two expressions agree because h*v-d=1-h*kappa.
At r=u they agree because h*v-d*u=v-d*B=u-h*B.
Thus the knot ties, and the collapsed middle interval at B=kappa,
are retained.

On each available affine piece the maximum absolute value occurs at an
endpoint. The core contributes the first term. On an available middle
piece the positive endpoint is bounded by the core endpoint, and the
negative endpoint contributes the second term. On an available tail
piece, its negative endpoint is the middle endpoint, and its positive
endpoint contributes the third term.

The formal middle term does not create a spurious extremum when R<1:
d*R-h*v <= (h*kappa-1)*R, since their difference is h*v*(R-1)<=0.
Its positive part is therefore bounded by the actual core term.
If R<u, a positive formal tail value satisfies
R-h*B<=R-h*G(R), the actual positive endpoint value, which is already
covered by the core or middle endpoint. This also handles R=0.

The signed update is continuous, odd, and fixes0. Its image of [-R,R]
is the entire symmetric interval with radius (3.1). Thus exact
chronological composition yields

    M_(j+1)=M_j composed with E_(h_j^lev) composed with M_j. (3.2)

No independently chosen incompatible child start is used. The quantitative
result below does not rely on a numerical replay of this identity.

## 4. Same-m masses and the core prefix lemma

For all m in the assignment,

    0<epsilon<1/1000, beta>99/100,
    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,
    2/5<a<1/2, 2/5<theta<1/2,
    c*theta=beta^2<1.                                 (4.1)

Use 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 for the c bounds. The lower a bound follows
from a>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5. For theta use
theta=beta^2/c>(99/100)^2*(7/17)>2/5.
The other inequalities follow directly.

Put eta=a/(c-2). Since lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda,

    0<eta<1,
    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j.                           (4.2)

The formula is verified by substitution and A_0=1. In particular,

    A_j>d_j/(c-2),    A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    D=2A_m-1<2c^m=2d_m/a.                            (4.3)

Also for every j>=1,

    S_j/d_j>7/5.                                      (4.4)

At j=1 the ratio is ell/beta>7/5. If L=S_j/d_j>7/5,
the next ratio is (2L+1+1/d_j)/c>(19/5)/(5/2)>7/5.
This is a symbolic induction, not an executed recurrence.

Define P_0=1 and
P_j=product_(h in H_j)|1-kappa*h| for j>=1.
For every 0<kappa<=1, jointly,

    every physical prefix product of H_j is at most1,
    P_j<=1,
    kappa*d_j*P_j<=theta for j>=1.                    (4.5)

For the leaf, |1-ell*kappa|<=1. Moreover
kappa*|1-ell*kappa|<=a:
on kappa<=1/ell its maximum is 1/(4ell)<a;
on kappa>=1/ell, kappa*(ell*kappa-1) increases up to a at1.
Multiplication by d_1=beta gives the last bound.

Suppose the claims are known for H_j, j>=1. If the level-j core
bridge reverses, its magnitude is
|1-kappa*(1+d_j)|<=kappa*d_j. Therefore

    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)
      <=c*(kappa*d_j*P_j)^2
      <=c*theta^2=theta*beta^2<theta.

The prefix ending at the bridge has multiplier at most
kappa*d_j*P_j<=theta<1. Multiplying it by any prefix product of
the right H_j preserves the prefix bound.

If that bridge is nonreversing, every effective core step in H_(j+1)
belongs to [0,1]. Its prefix products are at most1, and

    P_(j+1)<=exp(-kappa*S_(j+1)),
    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)
      <=1/[exp(1)*(S_(j+1)/d_(j+1))]
      <5/14<theta.

Here z*exp(-Lz)<=1/[exp(1)*L] follows by differentiation.
These arguments include zero factors and equality at a reversal
threshold, and prove (4.5).

Consequently a complete H_j started anywhere inside the closed core
remains there at EVERY physical prefix. Its actual output radius is
P_j times its input radius. This statement concerns a complete child,
not an arbitrary suffix begun at an arbitrary physical index.

## 5. The actual first-crossing price in a nonreversing child

Consider any complete H_b with kappa*(1+d_(b-1))<=1; for b=0 there
are no steps. Core entry is absorbing in H_b.

Suppose an actual trajectory in H_b has a first strict sign crossing,
at a level-k physical step h=1+d_k. Let r be its source radius,
p=G(r), and P its actual original prefix mass inside H_b.
Core absorption forces r>1. For every exterior source,

    r>=p+v.

Before the first crossing all radii decrease, and every queried gradient
is at least p. The complete left H_k immediately preceding this occurrence
gives P>=S_k. Therefore, for the original radius R_b of this child,

    R_b>=r+P*p>=v+(1+P)*p.

Strict crossing means (1+d_k)*p>r>=p+v, so p>v/d_k. It follows that

    R_b>v*(1+(1+P)/d_k)
        >=v*(1+A_k/d_k)>v Q.                         (5.1)

This is a price measured from the actual child's original start.
When H_b is the initial H_J of H_m, it is measured from the ORIGINAL
full-word start R. It does not reset that denominator.

The same proof also gives a local no-crossing criterion used below:
a start with radius at most v Q cannot have a strict crossing in H_J.
A zero hit is absorbing.

## 6. The first reversing bridge and its complete right child

From the definition of positive J,

    kappa*(1+d_(J-1))<=1,
    kappa*(1+d_J)>1,
    v>=a/(1+a)>2/7,
    v<t<=c*v,
    0<q=t-v<3v/2,
    q<(3/5)*t,
    Q>3,  L_J>7/5.                                   (6.1)

The bound on v uses J>=1 and hence kappa*ell<=1.
The t upper bound follows by multiplying
kappa*d_(J-1)<=v by c. The q/t bound follows from q<3v/2.

Assume the initial H_J has actual terminal radius y<=1.
Its level-J bridge has actual landing radius z=q*y<=q, and the
sign reverses unless y=0. No core approximation is made if z>1.

The following complete H_J, begun at z, has NO strict crossing:
its own core is absorbing, while an exterior first crossing would
require z>v Q by (5.1), contrary to z<=q<3v/2<v Q.
It must enter the core by its actual terminal. Otherwise all queried
and terminal radii exceed1 and every gradient is at least kappa,
so its final radius would be at most

    z-kappa*S_J<=q-L_J*t<0,

a contradiction. A same-side terminal core entry is included here.

To bound the returned radius, use the scalar comparison update

    C_h(r)=r-h*kappa*min(r,1).

This comparison map is nonnegative and nondecreasing on [0,infinity)
because every effective step of H_J has h*kappa<=1. Along the ACTUAL
noncrossing trajectory, (2.3) gives

    r_next=r-h*G(r)<=C_h(r).

Induction through the physical steps therefore bounds its final radius
by the composition of these comparison maps from z. This is an inequality
for the original fixed function; B has not been replaced or rescaled.

For r>0 define b(r)=log(r) if r<=1 and b(r)=r-1 if r>=1.
For an effective step 0<=alpha<=1,

    b(r-alpha*min(r,1))<=b(r)-alpha                 (6.2)

whenever the output is positive; a zero output stays zero.
Inside the core this follows from log(1-alpha)<=-alpha.
For a tail stay it is equality. For a tail-to-core transition it
follows from log(w)<=w-1 at w=r-alpha.

The final comparison radius lies below1, because
b(z)<=max(0,z-1)<L_J*t for z>0. Summing (6.2) and exponentiating
therefore bounds the actual returned radius by K_fr(z)*exp(-L_J*t).

For 0<=y<=1,

    K_fr(q*y)<=y*K_fr(q).                                   (6.3)

For q<=1 it is equality. If q>1 and q*y<=1, it follows from
q<=exp(q-1). If q*y>=1, the derivative of exp(q*y-1)/y has the
sign of q*y-1, so its maximum over that interval is at y=1.

Consequently, with r_(J+1) the output radius of the complete initial
H_(J+1),

    r_(J+1)<=y*Z_fr.                                    (6.4)

Both core and middle/tail parts of the possible excursion have been
retained in this estimate.

Two absolute bounds will pay for this one transition. Write e=exp(1).
The elementary series gives e>8/3>2.

If q<=1, then L_J*t>7q/3, so

    Z_fr=q*exp(-L_J*t)
       <=sup_(z>=0) z*exp(-7z/3)
       =3/(7e)<1/4.

Also q<(3/5)t and differentiation of t^2*exp(-L_J*t) give

    c*t*Z_fr
      <(3c/5)*4/(e^2*L_J^2)
      <1350/3136<1/2.                               (6.5)

If q>1, then q<(3/5)t gives

    Z_fr=exp(q-1-L_J*t)<exp(-1-(4/5)*t).

Equivalently using t>5q/3 gives
Z_fr<exp(-1-4q/3)<exp(-7/3)<1/4. Moreover

    c*t*Z_fr
      <c*sup_(z>=0) z*exp(-1-(4/5)*z)
      =5c/(4e^2)<25/32.                             (6.6)

Thus Z_fr<1/4 and c*t*Z_fr<25/32 for the full parameter range.
These constants are absolute. This proves the first child state (1.4).

If the excursion entered the core at a physical index tau inside the
right H_J, its exact remaining radius is its radius at tau times the
product of ALL remaining nonnegative factors 1-kappa*h. Equations
(6.2)-(6.4) bound that entire actual expression. They do not start
the quadratic product at the reversing bridge before core return.

## 7. All remaining ancestors and their actual terminal energy

After the complete INITIAL H_(J+1), the remaining literal word is

    h_(J+1)^lev,H_(J+1), h_(J+2)^lev,H_(J+2), ...,
    h_(m-1)^lev,H_(m-1).                             (7.1)

There are no skipped levels here: these are consecutive ancestors of
the leftmost H_(J+1). This is why the precise location in the statement
matters.

Suppose a radius r_l is the output of the complete initial H_l,
for l>=J+1, with r_l<=1 and kappa*d_l*r_l<1.
Every such level-l bridge reverses in the core, and its landing is

    q_l*r_l<=kappa*d_l*r_l<1.

The following complete H_l stays inside the core by (4.5). Hence

    r_(l+1)=q_l*P_l*r_l,
    r_(l+1)<=theta*r_l,
    kappa*d_(l+1)*r_(l+1)
      <=c*theta*(kappa*d_l*r_l)
      =beta^2*(kappa*d_l*r_l).                        (7.2)

Starting with (1.4), induction gives (1.5), every intervening prefix
is core, and (1.6) follows by multiplication.

In particular the true final objective is exactly

    2f(Psi_m(s))=kappa*r_m^2.                         (7.3)

The actual final gradient has magnitude kappa*r_m.
No middle-area constant is omitted: middle energy is used only before
the proved core return, while (7.3) applies after that return.
There cannot be a later core exit in this scoped theorem, by the
quantitative landing bound in (7.2).

Using (4.3), (6.4) and (7.2),

    D*kappa*r_m^2/R^2
      <(2c*t/a)*Z_fr^2*(y/R)^2*(c*theta^2)^N.           (7.4)

Since c*theta^2=theta*beta^2<1, ancestor depth creates no additional
loss. The first transition is paid once.

## 8. Objective closure using the original prefix price

If y=0, the result is zero. Assume y>0.

First suppose the original first H_J has no strict crossing.
Its radii decrease, and every queried gradient is at least kappa*y.
Thus its original start satisfies

    R>=y+S_J*kappa*y=(1+L_J*t)*y.                    (8.1)

The factor in (7.4), without its final factor at most1, is at most

    (2c/a)*t*Z_fr^2/(1+L_J*t)^2.                        (8.2)

For q<=1, use 2c/a<25/2, q<(3/5)t, and
(1+L_J*t)^2>=L_J^2*t^2. Expression (8.2) is less than

    (9/(2*L_J^2))*t*exp(-2L_J*t)
       <=9/(4e*L_J^3)
       <1125/2744<1/2.                              (8.3)

For q>1, use t>5/3 and
Z_fr^2<exp(-2-(8/5)t)<1/16. Then (8.2) is less than

    (25/2)*(25/49)*(3/5)*(1/16)
       =375/1568<1/2.                               (8.4)

For the exponential bound, e^2>4 and (8/5)t>8/3>2,
so each of the two exponential factors is less than1/4.
This proves the stronger 1/(2D) bound on the no-crossing-prefix branch.
This branch may still have the first inner reversal and its temporary
exterior excursion.

If the initial H_J has a strict crossing, its original start pays
R>v Q by (5.1). It ends at y<=1 by the theorem's explicit hypothesis.
Use t<=c*v and Z_fr<1/4 in (7.4). The coefficient is less than

    c^2/(8a*v*Q^2)
       <(25/4)/[8*(2/5)*(2/7)*9]
       =875/1152<4/5.                               (8.5)

This branch allows any earlier exterior crossings and any core-entry
time in the initial H_J. That core is absorbing up to the first
level-J bridge, so the theorem does not drop an earlier exit.
The first ORIGINAL crossing is priced only once.

Together (8.3)-(8.5) prove the complete full-word scoped claim (1.3).

## 9. An automatic input domain, and what remains outside it

Assume 0<R<=v Q. By (5.1) the initial H_J has no strict crossing.
If its terminal radius y were greater than1, every earlier queried
radius would also exceed1, and every queried gradient would be at
least kappa. Consequently,

    R>=y+kappa*S_J>1+kappa*S_J
       =v+kappa*A_J
       >v+(v/d_J)*A_J
       >v Q,

using t=kappa*d_J>v and (4.3). This contradiction proves y<=1.
In fact this automatic domain uses the stronger no-crossing-prefix
bound 1/(2D).

The actual gate is more general than this sufficient radius condition:
large starts can also enter the core in the first H_J and are covered
by (1.3). Membership in the gate is a condition on the ACTUAL fixed-law
trajectory, not an interval radius substituted into the payoff.

The unresolved complement is now explicit:

    positive J as in (1.1), R>v Q,
    |Psi_J(s)|>1.                                    (9.1)

For such a trajectory the first level-J bridge can have an exterior
source. Its later first core entry may lie inside a different child.
Its remaining ancestor list can skip levels, and the state
kappa*d_l*r_l<1 proved at the first leftmost block is not available.
Repeated later exits/returns and a final same-side middle-to-core entry
are not covered merely by (1.3). We do not label those histories closed.

This is the exact obstruction to declaring an all-start result from the
current proof. The whole odd-family sufficient bound

    sup_(s!=0) 2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <=(1+C/m)/D

for an absolute C remains unproved. No full-word objective counterexample
to that statement is claimed here.

## 10. Direct-proof screen and falsification limits

Two direct extensions were screened within the FIRST mechanism.

First, one can try to treat the landing q*y of the first reversing
bridge as already quadratic. This is unjustified when q*y>1, which
is permitted by (6.1). The proof above repairs that step with the
complete noncrossing right H_J, the comparison potential b, and the
actual return before starting the remaining quadratic suffix.
No universal product P_J may be attached to that bridge landing
without this return argument.

Second, one can try to reuse (7.2) after an arbitrary later core entry.
That induction requires kappa*d_l*r_l<1 at the NEXT actual bridge.
A mere radius bound r_l<=1 does not provide it at a large bridge.
If levels are skipped, d_l is larger by c to the number of skipped
levels while the corresponding complete-child factors were not run
after the entry. Multiplying in those absent factors would be a
different physical word. The original prefix mass may be large enough
to pay this jump, but that estimate has not been proved.

Counterexample screening keeps these two statements distinct:
the local assertion "every bridge preserves the core" fails whenever
kappa*(1+d_l)>2 and its input is1. This is a local test only, NOT an
improving complete-word objective witness and NOT a refutation of the
state (1.4)-(1.5). The latter has the stronger paid threshold and was
checked symbolically at all branch ties, zero factors, J=1 and J=m-1.

The earlier universal-amplitude counterexample in d34bc8275bb55379,
with kappa=B=1 and an O(1/m) necessary loss, remains awareness only.
Nothing in this proof assumes that failed assertion, spends its loss
at each level, or changes B to an amplitude w.

The A_(j-1) amplitude alternative is not asserted, refuted, or used.
The present state closes a concrete first-entry gate but does not
decide the exterior-source state (9.1). No separate second mechanism
or different function family is introduced to hide that gap.

## 11. Verification status, retrieval, and bounded handoff

All new mathematics is JOINT UNVERIFIED. The allocation explicitly
forbids fact_submit and all formal calls, so no new fact_id exists.
The global-memory record is a proof_attempt, not a fact.

Recall used TASK.md, current master_guidance cadd37d70aded16f,

Fact searches found no accepted first-reversal kink theorem. The

The search-math-results skill was used in mature_subproblem/repair mode.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003133 -->
# C35. Every ordered fresh-child tree relay is too weak on the near-critical ruler

Status: UNVERIFIED joint candidate, no verifier or solver. All ingredients used
below are proved internally. The result covers the exact incidence-flow class
defined here, not arbitrary tree-indexed vectors or arbitrary simplex walks.

## 1. A genuine real tree family with all normal inequalities

Partition a schedule into N ordinary blocks of masses S_i and checkpoints
b_i>1 after each block except the last. Set M_i=S_i+1, L_i=b_i-1 and
T_i=M_i+L_i for i<N-1. Choose T_(N-1)>=M_(N-1).

Choose ANY rooted tree on vertices 0,...,N-1 with root0 and with each parent
strictly earlier than its children. Write Ch_i for the children of i, and
W_i={j>i:parent(j)<i} for the waiting vertices at phase i. Define positive
energies E_i and actual vectors in the standard basis of R^N:

    alpha_i=sqrt(E_i)/T_i,
    beta_ij=sqrt(E_j)/T_i  (j in Ch_i),
    p_i=alpha_i e_i-sum_(j in Ch_i) beta_ij e_j.                (1)

The vector matrix is triangular with positive diagonal, hence these are
linearly independent vectors with an explicit positive definite real Gram
matrix. Distinct normals have negative inner product exactly along a tree
edge, and zero inner product otherwise. Let C=conv(0,p_i) and

    F(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).

Its maximizer is the Euclidean projection P_C(x). The projection variational
inequalities give firm nonexpansiveness, hence Lipschitz continuity, of P_C.
Bounding maximization differences with the two maximizing linear forms gives
grad F=P_C. Thus F is one fixed everywhere-defined convex 1-smooth function.

Start at x_init=sqrt(E_0)e_0. At the beginning of phase i the nonzero
coordinates are sqrt(E_j) for the current vertex i and for j in W_i.
During ordinary partial mass u the current coordinate is
(T_i-u)alpha_i, each waiting coordinate stays sqrt(E_j), and each child
coordinate is u beta_ij. After the checkpoint, the current coordinate
vanishes and every child coordinate becomes sqrt(E_j). This is the
prescribed GD recursion whenever its projection normals hold.

For y=x-p_i, all its coordinates are nonnegative. Its own normal value is

    delta_i(u)=(T_i-u-1)alpha_i^2
                         -(u+1)sum_(j in Ch_i)beta_ij^2.      (2)

A past vertex has nonpositive dot product with y: its positive coordinate
has been exhausted, and all its remaining coordinates are nonpositive.
A future vertex j has no mass yet in its children, since the tree has a
unique parent. Thus its dot product with y is exactly

    E_j/T_j                      if j in W_i,
    (u+1)sqrt(E_j)alpha_j/T_i     if j in Ch_i,
    0                            otherwise.

The comparison with zero is also required. The own value (2) decreases in u;
the child comparisons increase; the waiting comparisons are constant.
Therefore ALL comparisons against ALL past/future vertices and zero are
equivalent to the following endpoint conditions for i<N-1:

    L_i E_i-M_i sum_(j in Ch_i)E_j >=
       T_i^2 max(0,
           max_(j in W_i) E_j/T_j,
           (M_i/T_i) max_(j in Ch_i) E_j/T_j).                 (3)

Empty maxima are zero. The terminal phase has no children or waiting
vertices, and its condition is just T_(N-1)>=M_(N-1).
These checks include every actual ordinary query and checkpoint query.
They construct a fixed legal smooth-convex function, without oracle resets.

The minimizer set is C^polar: a violated dot-product inequality yields a
positive maximand at a small multiple of the corresponding vertex.
Its inequalities force every coordinate of a minimizer nonpositive, by
backward induction through the tree. Thus 0 is a closest minimizer to
sqrt(E_0)e_0, with true squared initial distance E_0.

Writing T=T_(N-1) and D=2S_(N-1)+1, the exact normalized objective is

    score=(2T-D) E_(N-1)/(T^2 E_0).                            (4)

For fixed tree, phase masses, T and terminal energy E_(N-1)=1, setting (3)
to equality in decreasing order of i constructs the componentwise least
positive feasible E_i. Indeed every right hand side is a nondecreasing
function of already-set future energies. This proves, without numerical
optimization, that the recursion attains the best score in this exact class.
There is always a positive future waiting or child energy until the last
phase, so the constructed E_i are positive.

The primitive defect is also exact on the SAME normalized trajectory.
At each projection query F_t=<x_t,g_t>-||g_t||^2/2. Consequently

    2h_t F_t+h_t(1-h_t)||g_t||^2
                         =||x_t||^2-||x_(t+1)||^2.

If s is the entire schedule mass and
V=2sum_t h_t(F_t-F_end)+sum_t h_t(1-h_t)||g_t||^2
  -s(s+1)||g_end||^2, then

    V/E_0=1-[(T-S_(N-1)+s)/T]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.                 (5)

No raw negative sign is substituted for objective or distance control.

## 2. A chronology bound valid for EVERY tree shape

Put H_i=E_i/T_i. The next chronological vertex i+1 is either a child of i
or already waiting. If it is waiting, (3), even after dropping all child
costs, implies

    H_(i+1)/H_i <= L_i/T_i.

If it is a child, retaining its own term in the child sum and its own
normal comparison gives

    H_(i+1)/H_i <= L_i T_i/[M_i(T_i+T_(i+1))].

Hence every legal tree relay satisfies

    H_(i+1)/H_i <=
        max(L_i/T_i, L_i T_i/[M_i(T_i+T_(i+1))]).             (6)

This is only a necessary bound; the constructive recursion (3) still keeps
every other comparison. The upper bound (6) is used only to exclude payoff.

## 3. Uniform quantitative exclusion on H_m

Fix m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m),
a=(1-1/m)/c, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j).
Let S_j be the mass of H_j and M_j=1+S_j. Geometric summation gives

    M_j=A c^j+(1-A)2^j,  A=a/(c-2) in (0,1).

Choose ANY dyadic blocking depth r<m. Put k=m-r, N=2^k, M=M_r and
q=a c^r/M. The N ordinary blocks are all copies of H_r and

    L_i=M q c^(nu_2(i+1)),
    T_i=M(1+q c^(nu_2(i+1)))  (i<N-1).                       (7)

The following simple uniform bounds will suffice:

    12/5<c<39/16,      2/5<q<=c-2<7/16.                    (8)

For the upper bound, sqrt(2)<99/70 and
(169/70)(129/128)<39/16. Also a<c-2 because ac<1<c(c-2).
This gives 0<A<1 and q<=c-2. Since M_r<=c^r, q>=a.
Finally a=(m-1)/[lambda(m+1)] >=127/(129lambda)
>2540/6321>2/5, using lambda<49/20. This proves (8).

Every even index i<N-1 has the low checkpoint q_i=q. In (6), its
waiting option is at least its child option. For a nonterminal next phase
this is q+q^2<=1+q_(i+1), which follows from q<1 and q_(i+1)>=q.
For the terminal next phase it follows from q+q^2<1<=T/M.
Thus every such low transition obeys

    H_(i+1)/H_i <= q/(1+q).                                  (9)

Every odd index has q_i=q c^ell for some ell>=1, and is followed by
a low checkpoint. Its child option dominates the waiting option:
q_i^2+q_i>1+q, because q_i>24/25 while q<7/16.
Therefore

    H_(i+1)/H_i <= q c^ell / D_ell,
    D_ell=(2+q+q c^ell)/(1+q c^ell)>1.                       (10)

There are N/2 low transitions and 2^(k-ell-1) transitions of level ell>=1.
Counting binary valuations gives

    product_(i<N-1)q c^(nu_2(i+1))=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k).

Since (2T-D)/T<2, (4), (9), and (10) yield

    score < [2/(M(1+q)c^k)]
             (qc)^(N-1) /
             [(1+q)^(N/2) product_(ell=1)^(k-1)
                           D_ell^(2^(k-ell-1))].             (11)

Let K=1/(2M_m-1), the global Huber score. The exact mass relation is

    M_m=M[(1-q/(c-2))2^k+(q/(c-2))c^k] <=M c^k.

It follows that score/K<B_k, where

    B_k=4(qc)^(2^k-1) /
       [(1+q)^(2^(k-1)+1)
          product_(ell=1)^(k-1)D_ell^(2^(k-ell-1))].          (12)

For k=2,

    B_2=4(qc)^3(1+qc)/[(1+q)^3(2+q+qc)].

This increases in both q and c. Its logarithmic q derivative is
3/[q(1+q)]+(c-1)/[(1+cq)(2+(1+c)q)]>0. Its logarithmic c
derivative is 3/c+q/(1+cq)-q/(2+(1+c)q)>0. Consequently (8) gives

    B_2 <
      273^3*529/[1024*23^3*897]
      =10763254593/11175730176 =: eta <1.                    (13)

The recurrence is exact:

    B_(k+1)/B_k^2 = (qc)(1+q)/(4D_k)
                  <6279/16384 =: zeta <1.                  (14)

Thus, for every k>=2,

    score/K < B_k
             <=zeta^(2^(k-2)-1) eta^(2^(k-2)) <1.            (15)

This excludes EVERY ordered rooted tree and EVERY legal positive energy
choice in the specified family. If k grows, the ratio tends to zero
exponentially in 2^k. In particular no choice of tree shape avoids the loss.

When k=1 the sole tree is the two-vertex path. The exact endpoint condition
gives, with T_0=M(1+q) and D=2M-1,

    score=q(2T-D)/[T(T+T_0)].

The maximum is attained at T=(D+sqrt(D^2+2D T_0))/2. Writing epsilon=1/M
its ratio to K is

    2q[2(2+q)-epsilon] /
       [3+q-epsilon+sqrt((2-epsilon)(4+2q-epsilon))]
      <=4q(2+q)/[2+q+sqrt(3+2q)].

The last expression increases in q>=0 because
sqrt(3+2q)/(2+q) decreases. As q<9/20 it is less than
441/[245+10sqrt(390)]<1; the latter inequality is 196^2<39000.

Allowing r=m leaves just one vertex and one ordinary block. The choice
T=2S_m+1 attains K. Therefore the union over ALL blocking depths, ALL
ordered rooted trees and ALL legal parameters has exact best score K.

## 4. The obstruction and the next genuinely different mechanism

Tree branching reduces the number of edges from root to terminal, but it
creates waiting coordinate masses. Their supporting planes must be below
the active plane at every intervening query. Inequality (9) is the
quantitative price at each low checkpoint, independent of the chosen tree.

The unique-parent condition is load-bearing. With two parents contributing
to one child coordinate, two earlier gradient vectors can have positive
correlation. A future plane can then have a negative term from a child
already preloaded by another parent. The waiting comparison E_j/T_j used
above is no longer correct, so (6) and (15) do not cover directed acyclic
split-and-merge constructions. Nor do they cover signed coordinate mass,
revisiting exhausted coordinates, other simplex geometries, or general
smooth-convex functions. The original all-schedule minimax target is open.

# Max38: sharp noncore predecessors of the retained triangle

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No accepted fact is
asserted. This is analytical round one of the newly authorized two-round
wave: completed baseline37, intended completion38, completed ceiling39,
attempted ceiling40 including the historical interruption. The actual
outer loop records completion only after this session returns. There is
no numerical, solver, machine-algebra, replay, formal-verifier, installation,
consult or subagent use. Every calculation below is textual.

The original target remains the all-word, all-sufficiently-large-horizon
comparison W_n(h)>=(1-o(1))/B_n, with W=2R. The result below does not solve it.

Accepted inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 (normalization and interpolation),
b98bb807d514e80d (K_n and the original benchmark), and1fca3e5dd555cb4a
(the complete alternating-ray reference F_ray_n). All geometric ingredients,
including the old concrete triangle, are proved here from their definitions.
Neither the old draft ec1fba9a30a3fa47 nor its independent research audit
is assumed correct. No unverified intermediate finding is a proof premise.

Symbols p,q,e,n are local abbreviations for the namespaced p38,q38,e38,n38
defined below. Likewise c,z,X,y,C0 abbreviate the local family symbols
c38,z38,X38,y_new38,Ctri38; they do not redefine the project-wide Huber
parameters or the mass recurrence C_n. Inner products and
norms are Euclidean; a vector written with two coordinates lies in a fixed
plane E38. Extra coordinates, when used, are orthogonal to that plane.

## 1. Exact family, data and complete result

Put s=sqrt(3) and fix

    p=(1,2s), q=(-2,-s), e=(1,0), n=(1,-s),
    C0=conv{p,q,e}.

The complete old data, including its actual final point, are

    index   position     gradient    absolute value
    0       p            p           13/2
    1       -p           q            9/2
    2       3e           e           5/2
    3       2e           e           3/2.                       (1)

The physical old word is exactly(2,2,1). Define

    Wedge38={v:<e,v><=1, <n,v><=1}.                             (2)

Our declared enlarged family consists of every finite-dimensional compact
convex C with C0 subset C subset Wedge38, and the static function

    F_C(x)=max_(v in C){<x,v>-||v||^2/2}.                       (3)

We retain every jet in(1), every old queried supporting normal, and every
point of C0. Expansion past the unqueried p-q facet is allowed. In particular
this is not the stronger requirement F_C=F_C0 at every unsampled point.
No queried supporting face or interpolation row is removed. The section
on scope below distinguishes this enlargement from keeping C0 unchanged.

For a given actual preceding step c>=0, a new gradient z has to occur at

    X=p+c z,   y=X-z=p+(c-1)z,                                (4)

and its value is F_C(X)=<X,z>-||z||^2/2. Let D38(c) denote the smallest
possible squared TRUE initial distance among this entire family and all
ambient dimensions. The parameter domain is every c>=0. The following
table attains the exact minimum, not just an upper bound on its radius.
The resulting exact normalized terminal payoff is V38(c)=3/D38(c).

I. If 0<=c<=sqrt(2), take z=p. Then

    D38(c)=13(1+c)^2.                                        (5)

II. If sqrt(2)<=c<=12/5, set k=c-1 and

    Delta=sqrt(k^2+58k+9),
    u=(11k-9-3Delta)/(8k),
    z=(u,(u-1)/s),
    D38(c)=(1+c u)^2+[2s+c(u-1)/s]^2.                        (6)

Here k>0. The two choices at c=sqrt(2) need not have the same z, but have
the same optimal radius. No continuity of an inverse state is assumed.

III. If 12/5<=c<=6, again set k=c-1, and choose a unit w perpendicular
to E38. Define

    u=(k-7)/(2k),
    v=-(k+7)/(2s k),
    a_perp=sqrt((5-k)(5k-7)/(6k^2)),
    z=(u,v)+a_perp w,
    rho=||z||^2=(-k^2+6k+21)/(2k^2),
    D38(c)=(-k^4+2k^3+14k^2+6k+21)/(2k^2).                  (7)

The perpendicular coordinate is strictly positive for12/5<c<6. It is
essential for attaining the all-dimension optimum in this open interval.

IV. If c>=6, put k=c-1 and take

    z=-p/k,   X=z,   D38(c)=13/k^2.                          (8)

At c=12/5 both adjoining choices give z=q. At c=6 both adjoining choices
give z=-p/5. In every case one may use the explicit compact set

    C_c=conv{p,q,e,z}.                                      (9)

Thus this is a finite closed-form construction, without a feasibility
oracle, inverse continuity assumption, changed word or hidden radius reset.

Two requested decisions are particularly simple:

    c=1: D38=52, V38=3/52 < F_ray_4(1,2,2,1)=1/13;
    c=2: D38=60-9sqrt(17),
         V38=1/(20-3sqrt(17)) > F_ray_4(2,2,2,1)=1/11.        (10)

The c1 loss is a complete obstruction to beating that reference inside
the declared projection family, even with arbitrary extra dimensions.
It is not an obstruction to arbitrary smooth convex interpolants.

## 2. Static smoothness, all old data and the actual minimum

For compact convex C, completing the square in(3) shows that its unique
maximizer is P_C(x), the point of C nearest to x. The elementary normal
criterion is

    g=P_C(x) iff g in C and <x-g,v-g><=0 for every v in C.    (11)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance along the segment
g+t(v-g) at t=0. Sufficiency follows by expanding the difference between
the squared distances to v and to g. Strict convexity of squared distance
gives uniqueness. Applying(11) at two points and adding yields

    ||P_C(x)-P_C(x')||^2 <= <x-x',P_C(x)-P_C(x')>.

The two maximizing inequalities defining F_C then give

    0<=F_C(x')-F_C(x)-<P_C(x),x'-x>
      <=<P_C(x')-P_C(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

Consequently F_C is differentiable, its gradient is P_C, and that gradient
is1-Lipschitz. It is convex as a supremum of affine functions and finite
because C is compact.

The exact minimum value is0, and its entire minimizer set is

    C^polar={m:<m,v><=0 for every v in C}.                    (12)

Indeed, polar points make every maximand nonpositive, with equality at0.
For a nonpolar point m choose v in C with <m,v>>0. A sufficiently small
positive multiple t v belongs to C and gives a positive maximand. This
proves(12), including the necessity direction.

Here p+2q+3e=0, and p,e are linearly independent. Thus0 lies in the
relative interior of C0. At the old first point p the residual is0.
At -p with gradient q, the residual is n, and its normal products against
p-q,e-q,0-q are -6,0,-1. At3e and2e the residuals are2e ande, and the
normal products against p-e,q-e,0-e are respectively0,-3t,-t for
residual t e, t=2 or1. Thus C0 realizes all old projections.

Every old projection and value persists under exactly the restrictions
in(2) on added vectors: n has support value<n,q>=1 and e has support
value<e,e>=1. The actual updates are p-2p=-p, -p-2q=3e, and3e-e=2e.
The values are <x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, namely those in(1).

For any sampled jets of(3), with residual y_j=x_j-g_j, expansion in the
orientation of accepted1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                                    (13)

Equation(11) therefore verifies EVERY ordered interpolation inequality,
including both directions of new-old pairs, every old-old pair, and both
directions involving the optimum jet(0,0,0). We never delete an incoming
row at a query or at the terminal point.

Most importantly, for any family member and predecessor(4), every
m in C^polar has <p,m><=0 and <z,m><=0. Since c>=0,

    ||X-m||^2=||X||^2+||m||^2-2<X,m> >= ||X||^2.

Zero is itself a minimizer, so the TRUE distance is exactly||X||. No
uniqueness assumption or chosen-minimizer surrogate is used. The fixed
terminal value is3/2 and the actual minimum value is0, giving payoff3/||X||^2.

For our explicit C_c in its minimal ambient space, the minimum is unique0
on the planar branches. In the interior of branchIII it is the whole ray
{-t w:t>=0}: the old triangle forces a polar vector to have zero component
in E38, and the positive a_perp imposes its nonpositive w component. The
new initial w component is c*a_perp>=0, so0 is again its closest minimizer.
If extra unused ambient coordinates are included, their full orthogonal
subspace is added to the minimizer set, without changing the distance.

## 3. Necessary and sufficient predecessor conditions

For arbitrary z, the old normals require z in Wedge38. Equation(4) and
the new projection condition require precisely

    <p+(c-1)z,a-z><=0,   a=p,q,e.                            (14)

These are necessary in any larger C. Conversely, if they hold, C_c in(9)
is sufficient: at z the self-product is0 and the three old vertex tests
give every new normal inequality by convexity. Section2 then supplies the
actual static function, all its values and all ordered interpolation rows.

For k=c-1>0 define rho=||z||^2 and support slacks

    S_a=k rho+<p-k a,z>-<p,a>,  a=p,q,e.                    (15)

The conditions(14) are S_a>=0. In particular

    S_p=k rho+(1-k)<p,z>-13,
    S_q=k rho+<p-kq,z>+8.                                  (16)

For orientation only, writing z=v+a_perp w with v in E38 reduces this to
rho>=||v||^2, two linear old caps and three linear support slacks in
(v,rho). Conversely any such rho is realized by one extra perpendicular
coordinate. This explains the possibility of branchIII. We do not leave
this finite problem as an unsolved optimization: the following explicit
identities prove the table and all its optimality claims.

## 4. Optimality of the four branches

For0<c<=sqrt(2), write d=z-p. The p row, valid also when c<=1, says

    c<p,d>+(c-1)||d||^2>=0.

Since X=(1+c)p+c d,

    ||X||^2 >= 13(1+c)^2+(2-c^2)||d||^2
             >=13(1+c)^2.                                 (17)

The choice z=p attains equality. Its residual is c p, which supports C0
at p because <p,p-q>=21 and <p,p-e>=12. At c=0 the same old query p is
repeated and has the same gradient and value. This proves branchI.

For c>1 put k=c-1. The candidate in(6) satisfies<n,z>=1 and S_p=0:
on that line,

    rho=(4u^2-2u+1)/3, <p,z>=3u-2,
    3S_p=4k u^2+(9-11k)u+7k-45.                            (18)

The negative root of this quadratic is exactly(6). For
sqrt(2)-1<=k<=7/5, its value u is at most -2: the polynomial in(18),
evaluated at -2, is9(5k-7)<=0 and its leading coefficient is positive.
Thus<e,z>=u<=1.

To check the other NEW rows, put b=(k-7)/(2k). The polynomial in(18) at
b is (k-5)(5k-7)/(2k)>=0. Also b lies strictly to the left of the vertex
of that quadratic, because the difference is(-7k-19)/(8k)<0. Consequently
b<=u, including equality at k=7/5. With y=p+kz, we obtain

    <y,p-q>=3[7+k(2u-1)]>=0,
    <y,p-e>=12+2k(u-1)>=5-k>0.                              (19)

As S_p=0, these verify S_q,S_e>=0. Every condition(14) is satisfied.

For its optimality, let z0 denote this explicit candidate, and define

    E_k=k^2+2k-1,
    ell=3c E_k/Delta,
    A=(c^2-ell)/k,
    d_cap=[5c E_k+ell(5-7k)]/(4k).                          (20)

For the displayed k interval, ell>=0, A>0 and d_cap>=0. Here
Delta^2-9c^2=8k(5-k)>=0, so ell<=E_k=c^2-2<c^2. For d_cap, its bracket
after factoring c E_k/(4k) is5+3(5-7k)/Delta. If7k<=5 it is positive.
If7k>5, Delta>=3c makes it at least
5-(7k-5)/c=(10-2k)/c>0. At E_k=0, ell=d_cap=0.

Expansion, using n=(3e-p)/2 and the root in(6), gives the identity

    ||p+c z||^2-D38(c)
       = A S_p+d_cap(1-<n,z>)+ell||z-z0||^2.                (21)

For a check of every coefficient, A k+ell=c^2 and
A(1-k)p-d_cap n-2ell z0=2c p. The latter follows by decomposing
along n and its orthogonal unit vector(s,1)/2; the two components of z0
are1/2 and(4u-1)/(2s), respectively. In that orthogonal direction
[-2k(4u-1)/9-(1-k)]=Delta/3, which is exactly the formula for ell.
The n component gives the stated d_cap. At z=z0 the right side is0,
which fixes the constant term as well. Thus(21) is valid in any ambient
dimension, not merely in the plane. All its terms are nonnegative for a
feasible predecessor. This proves branchII, including the shared radius
at c=sqrt(2).

For branchIII, use the definitions(7), k in[7/5,5]. Direct substitution
gives

    <n,z>=1, <e,z>=(k-7)/(2k)<=1,
    <p,z>=-(k+21)/(2k),
    ||(u,v)||^2=(k^2-7k+49)/(3k^2),
    rho-||(u,v)||^2=(5-k)(5k-7)/(6k^2)>=0.                 (22)

The formulas give S_p=S_q=0. The planar component of y=p+kz is
(5-k)(-3,s)/6. Since<p-e,(-3,s)>=6, its remaining slack is

    S_e=<y,p-e>=5-k>=0.                                   (23)

This proves actual feasibility, including the extra coordinate. The
distance from(4) is

    13-c(k+21)/k+c^2(-k^2+6k+21)/(2k^2),

whose expansion is exactly(7).

Here the optimality identity is even simpler. Define positive coefficients

    A=c(k^2+1)/(2k^2),
    B=c(k^2+2k-1)/(2k^2),
    d_cap=kB.

For EVERY feasible z in EVERY ambient dimension,

    ||p+c z||^2-D38(c)
       = A S_p+B S_q+d_cap(1-<n,z>) >=0.                   (24)

Indeed k(A+B)=c^2; the vector coefficient is
A(1-k)p+B(p-kq)-d_cap n=2c p, using q=-(p+3e)/2 and n=(3e-p)/2.
The constant is fixed by the candidate, for which all three slacks vanish.
This proves sharpness of branchIII and supplies a checkable positive
combination of ORIGINAL constraints, rather than a solver certificate.

The strict need for an extra dimension also follows from(24). In the
open interval7/5<k<5, all three coefficients are strictly positive, so
any optimal z must have S_p=S_q=0 and<n,z>=1. Subtracting the support
rows gives <p+kz,p-q>=0. These two linear conditions force the planar
coordinates in(7); S_p=0 then forces rho in(7). Equation(22) says that
rho strictly exceeds the squared norm of those planar coordinates.
Thus no planar predecessor attains D38(c). This is a statement about
dimension in this declared family, not about all convex smooth functions.

Finally, for c>=6 put k=c-1 and write d=z+p/k. The p row says

    -c<p,d>+k||d||^2>=0.

Therefore

    ||p+c z||^2 >=13/k^2+(c^2-2)||d||^2>=13/k^2.            (25)

The equality choice z=-p/k belongs to the old triangle itself. To check
this without a picture, C0 is the intersection in E38 of
<e,z><=1, <n,z><=1 and <(-3,s),z><=3. At z=-p/k these values are
-1/k,5/k,-3/k, respectively, and k>=5. Its residual p+kz is0, so all
new support rows hold. This proves branchIV. Together(17),(21),(24),(25)
prove necessity, sufficiency and exact cost on the whole parameter domain.

## 5. Requested c1/c2 data and a dimensional stress test

At c1 the original C0 suffices: X=2p, z=p, y=p, f(X)=39/2,
and the exact squared initial distance is52. This is a positive-payoff
unit prepend, so the old all-core obstruction is not an obstruction to
all predecessors. Its normalized payoff3/52 nevertheless loses to the
same-word ray value1/13.

At c2 write r=sqrt(17). The exact new data are

    u=(1-3r)/4, z=(u,(u-1)/s),
    X=p+2z, y=p+z,
    ||z||^2=13, <p,z>=(-5-9r)/4,
    f(X)=(73-9r)/4,
    ||X||^2=60-9r.                                        (26)

The old cap<n,z>=1 is active, and<e,z>=u<1. The p support row is
equality. The other two normal products, written against the support p,
are

    <y,q-p>=(-39+9r)/2<0,
    <y,e-p>=(-21+3r)/2<0.                                 (27)

The signs follow from r<13/3 and r<7. Also u<-2, since r>3, so q
lies on the retained supporting edge between e and z; C_c=conv{p,e,z}
still contains every old gradient and all of C0. The old normal n still
supports at q, and the old e normals still support at e and p. Formula
(13) includes all these old faces and both orientations of every new row.

The c2 payoff in(10) exceeds1/11 because r>3, while its denominator is
positive because r<20/3. It is also larger than the best original-C0
predecessor at c2, which is X=3p, with squared radius117. For completeness,
the latter can be checked by the entire vertex/edge/core list. A core
predecessor would be -p, outside C0. At q the p-directed support test
requires k>=7/5, so k1 fails. At e the p-directed test fails. On the p-e
edge the vertical component of p+z is strictly positive, incompatible
with its horizontal outward normal. On the q-e edge the required multiple
of n is(k-5)/4=-1, pointing inward. On the p-q edge the parameter from q
toward p is(5k-7)/(12k)=-1/6, outside that edge. The remaining vertex p
is feasible. This separates the noncore enlargement from merely changing
the start on the globally unchanged old function.

As an independent stress test, c3 gives k2 and

    z=(-5/4,-3s/4)+sqrt(3/8)w,
    X=(-11/4,-s/4)+3sqrt(3/8)w,
    ||z||^2=29/8, ||X||^2=89/8,
    V38(3)=24/89.                                         (28)

Here(24) has A=15/8, B=21/8, d_cap=21/4. Equality fixes the first
two coordinates and norm in(28), so the positive squared height3/8
cannot be suppressed. These are exact actual jets of(3), with all old
data intact, and not an arbitrary Gram matrix awaiting realization.

## 6. Complete SAME-word accepted scalar/ray comparison

For h=(c,2,2,1), accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives

    K_4(h)=max{1/(2c+11), (c-1)_+^2/11,
               1/[7(c+1)^2], 1/[3(c+3)^2]}.              (29)

The fourth term is always below the first, since
3(c+3)^2-(2c+11)=3c^2+16c+16>0. For the FULL accepted alternating-ray
reference, the only additional candidates are the two-cut choices with
c as first checkpoint and one of the steps2 as second checkpoint.

This exhaustion follows directly from1fca3e5dd555cb4a. A selected step1
cannot be a nonterminal checkpoint with a subsequent positive slope. As
a last checkpoint after another cut it has z_term=-O<=0 and positive
old slope; it is infeasible. Alone it contributes0. The pair consisting
of the two steps2 has first slope1/(1+c), next slope the same, and final
budget(1-c)/(1+c), which is smaller than its required old slope times2.
The triple(c,2,2), when its intermediate slopes are legal, has slopes
1,k,k and terminal budget k but requires at least2k because of the final
unit step. Thus it too is infeasible. Cases c<=1 either fail positivity
or contribute only zero in the appropriate single-cut case.

For checkpoints(c,the first2), the ordinary block sums are(0,0,3).
The feasibility condition is k=c-1>=4, and its optimized value is

    R_1(k)=2k-7, 4<=k<=7;
           k^2/7, k>=7.                                  (30)

For checkpoints(c,the second2), the ordinary sums are(0,2,1).
The condition is k>=6, and its value is

    R_2(k)=2k/3-3, 6<=k<=9;
           k^2/27, k>=9.                                 (31)

All offsets and retained areas here are those of the accepted theorem;
the terminal retained area is0. For6<=k<=7, R_1-R_2=4k/3-4>0.
For k>=7, R_2<=k^2/27<k^2/7=R_1, including the clamped part of(31),
since k^2/27-(2k/3-3)=(k-9)^2/27. Thus R_2 can be deleted only after
this comparison, not by forgetting a feasible checkpoint subset.

Define exact breakpoints

    b0=2/7,
    b1=(sqrt(209)-7)/4,
    b2=12-2sqrt(11).

The complete accepted reference has the closed form

    R38(c):=F_ray_4(c,2,2,1)
      =1/[7(1+c)^2],        0<=c<=b0;
       1/(2c+11),           b0<=c<=b1;
       (c-1)^2/11,          b1<=c<=b2;
       2c-9,                b2<=c<=8;
       (c-1)^2/7,           c>=8.                          (32)

To verify the remaining comparisons, the first two terms of(29) cross
at b1 after c>=1 because their equality reduces to
c(2c^2+7c-20)=0. The third term crosses the first at b0. For0<=c<=1
the second term is0, and on[1,b1] the first remains larger. Where the
third might exceed the second it is already at most the first. On k in
[4,7], equality of R_1 with k^2/11 is k^2-22k+77=0, whose relevant root
is11-2sqrt(11). Beyond k7, k^2/7 exceeds k^2/11. These observations and
the exhausted list above prove(32) with every boundary included.

Equations(5)-(8) and(32) give the explicit exact comparison for EVERY c:

    V38(c)/F_ray_4(c,2,2,1)=3/[D38(c) R38(c)].              (33)

Thus there is no unknown feasibility or value optimization in this
comparison. It does not assert that the ratio is everywhere greater than1.
For illustration the exact comparisons are

    c0: V38=3/13 > R38=1/7;
    c1: V38=3/52 < R38=1/13;
    c2: V38=1/(20-3sqrt(17)) > R38=1/11;
    c3: V38=24/89 < R38=4/11;
    c6: V38=75/13 > R38=3.                                 (34)

For the entire initial interval0<=c<=sqrt(2), the ratio exceeds1
exactly when c<(-10+6sqrt(10))/13, with equality there. This follows
from the first two branches of(32) and the sign of13c^2+20c-20.
For the entire range c>=6 it exceeds1: on6<=c<=8 the difference
3(c-1)^2-13(2c-9)=3c^2-32c+120 has negative discriminant and positive
leading coefficient, and for c>=8 the ratio is the constant21/13.
In the intervening range(33) is the exact radical/rational comparison;
no additional root classification is needed for evaluating its sign.

All scalar convex quadratics, and their orthogonal sums, are also below
this COMPLETE ray reference. A scalar curvature t in[0,1] has payoff

    t(1-c t)^2(1-2t)^4(1-t)^2.                             (35)

The factor without(1-c t)^2 is at most1/16. For0<=t<=1/2, it is at
most t(1-t)^6, whose maximum is6^6/7^7<1/16; differentiation places the
maximum at1/7, and16*6^6=746496<823543=7^7. For1/2<=t<=1,
|1-2t|<=t, so it is at most t^5(1-t)^2<=t^2(1-t)^2<=1/16.
Since |1-c t|<=max{1,|1-c|}, (35) is at most

    max{1,(c-1)^2}/16.

If c<=2 this is1/16<1/(2c+11)<=R38(c). If c>=2 it is at most
(c-1)^2/16<(c-1)^2/11<=R38(c). Orthogonal sums average these scalar
payoffs with nonnegative squared-distance weights; zero curvature
directions belong to the minimizer set and contribute no true distance.
Hence no untested curvature sample or diagonal quadratic exception is
hidden. This comparison does not optimize all scalar nonlinear functions;
the full value-optimized scalar channel discussed elsewhere remains a
comparison gap, as does unrestricted W_4(h). No comparison with the
accepted complex-polynomial channel on this NEW four-step word is asserted.

## 7. An explicit reusable output state and its quantified limit

For every c the output is the actual static function(3) with vertices(9),
all five queried/terminal jets(1),(4), the new value<p+c z,z>-||z||^2/2,
the old normals0,n,2e,e, the new normal p+(c-1)z, and the exact polar
minimum set and true distance already proved. Subsequent enlargements
must retain ALL these normals, in particular the new one. Reusing only
the original wedge would drop an actual earlier constraint.

There is a concrete arbitrary-depth continuation for the useful c2
member, with a fully paid cost. Retain its fixed z from(26) and its
C_2=conv{p,e,z}. Both p+z and z are normal vectors at z: the first is
proved in(27), while the second follows from

    ||z||^2=13>=<z,p>, <z,e>.

Therefore for every t>=0 the residual p+(1+t)z is normal at z.
Prepend ANY finite nonnegative physical word d=(d_0,...,d_(m-1)) of
total H=sum d_j to the existing word(2,2,2,1). Define the actual new
points before reaching X in(26) by

    X_j=p+[2+sum_(i=j)^(m-1)d_i]z, 0<=j<=m.

At every new point the gradient is exactly z; its residual has the
nonnegative-normal form just proved. Every update X_j-d_j z=X_(j+1)
is the actual prescribed update on the SAME F_C2. All old jets are
unaltered, all new values are supplied by(3), and(13) verifies every
new-new and new-old directed row. The minimum stays unique0 in its plane.

Writing A0=<p,z>=(-5-9sqrt(17))/4, the exact cost and payoff are

    D_prefix(H)=13+2(H+2)A0+13(H+2)^2,
    V_prefix(H)=3/D_prefix(H).                             (36)

This invariant is genuinely executable for arbitrary depth and every
nonnegative prefix step, but its loss is quadratic in the total prefix
mass. On the SAME full word the accepted Huber reference is1/(2H+15),
and the ratio is exactly3(2H+15)/D_prefix(H), which tends to0 as H tends
to infinity. In particular, for m unit prepends the horizon is m+4;
accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives B_(m+4)<=1+4(m+4)^log_2(3).
As log_2(3)<2, even V_prefix(m)/U_(m+4)^ZJ tends to0. This is a complete
quantitative failure of THIS constant-gradient continuation to provide
the required arbitrary-word profile lower bound. It is not an upper bound
on W and not a prohibition against changing the earlier gradients.

## 8. Scope, failed extrapolations and provenance

The zero-residual restriction has been removed. Both requested preceding
steps are legal; the exact all-dimension optimal cost is solved for every
c>=0 inside the stated projection-conjugate enlargement family. A third
coordinate is necessary on its middle branch. The actual minimizer set,
including its nonunique case, is retained in the true-distance calculation.
The construction never changes the old coordinates, gradients, absolute
values, physical word or old queried supporting normals.

This is still a restricted family. It does not exhaust all static convex
1-smooth interpolants of the old jets, permit arbitrary conjugate heights,
or optimize a shifted minimum value outside(3). If one moves to those
broader families, both the terminal numerator and the true distance must
be recalculated. The reusable state is not claimed closed under another
application of the four-branch optimum table: its new support normal must
be carried. The explicitly solved constant-gradient continuation is fully
paid and asymptotically insufficient. A successful joint changing-gradient
prepend rule remains open. None of these statements finalizes the original
all-h/all-large-horizon Goal.

Retrieval was conditioned on the active noncore fixed-triangle program,
with a missing paid inverse/support interface and search mode mutation.
Matlas returned1805.08096 Property8 (a concave-subgradient representation)
and2111.07720 Lemma5.1 (a projected optimization bound); neither supplies
the missing mechanism and neither is imported. A primary-source web search
located Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, arXiv1502.05666. Its exact local text
downloads/1502.05666.txt, Section2.4, Theorem4 and proof, and Corollary1
were read. Those definitions distinguish conjugate strong convexity from
primal smoothness; their finite interpolation criterion is already covered
by accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Sections2-4 above reprove all special
construction and cost claims, with no external theorem used as a black box.

New fact IDs:none. This entire joint draft remains UNVERIFIED until a
separately authorized fact_submit gate accepts it. One analytical round
remains after this session returns; no additional resource allowance is
created by this draft.

Audit awareness: independent research finding09aad8d7776a27bc reports
that all four scoped items in the earlier final triangle draft survive its
attack. Its checked source SHA256 is
b3aeabfa253c8bce6831d18371082d8af0661973eee2e83389fccf953cc84988.
That is not verifier acceptance, and it is not an audit of this new
all-dimension table. No proof here uses it as a premise. Its additional
complex-channel comparison is for(2,2,1), so it is not transferred to
the different physical word(c,2,2,1).

# Exact Huber padding gives a core-ended realization of every primitive defect

Status: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No numerical PEP or
fact_submit call. The only fact used is 1252b8823bb5a290, specifically its
finite smooth-convex interpolation construction. This file includes every
new deduction; earlier shared findings are context, not premises.

## 1. Statement and definitions

Fix a finite nonnegative stepsize schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Put
s=sum_i h_i, T=1+s, and R_i=sum_(t=i)^(n-1) h_t for 0<=i<=n.
Thus R_n=0. Consider any actual finite convex differentiable 1-smooth
trajectory x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, with g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i).
Let

  delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
  V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-T(T-1)||g_n||^2.

Then there exists another actual trajectory for the SAME h, in at most
one additional dimension, on an everywhere finite convex differentiable
1-smooth function with minimizer0, such that

  x_n_new=g_n_new, f_n_new=||g_n_new||^2/2,
  V_h_new=V_h.

In particular, h has a negative primitive-energy witness if and only if it
has a witness whose endpoint equals its terminal gradient relative to a
minimizer. No preservation of the normalized objective error is asserted.

Here and below a core endpoint means precisely x_n=g_n with optimum0.

## 2. Choose one finite Huber companion

If R_i=0, nonnegativity of h forces every later step to be zero.
Consequently x_i=x_n, g_i=g_n, f_i=f_n and delta_i=delta_n.
Choose any c>0 such that

  c^2 >= max_(i:R_i>0) (delta_n-delta_i)_+/R_i,                 (1)

with empty maximum0 and (a)_+=max(a,0). This is a finite maximum.

Define the fixed scalar Huber function

  H_c(z)=z^2/2                         if |z|<=c,
         c|z|-c^2/2                   if |z|>=c.

Start its GD run at z_0=T c. Every step of the given schedule stays on
its positive affine ray:

  z_i=(1+R_i)c, H_c'(z_i)=c,
  H_c(z_i)=(R_i+1/2)c^2.                                    (2)

These formulas include its final point z_n=c. The derivative is
continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, so H_c is a fixed legal
convex differentiable 1-smooth function.

Run the direct sum f(x)+H_c(z). Its exact sampled data are

  X_i=(x_i,(1+R_i)c),
  G_i=(g_i,c),
  F_i=f_i+(R_i+1/2)c^2.                                    (3)

The original-index GD equations hold, including zero steps.

The companion's finite interpolation residual is zero for every ordered
pair: its two gradients are equal and its sampled values are affine.
Therefore

  Q_ij_new=Q_ij_original<=0.                                (4)

Its adjusted values obey

  Delta_i=F_i-||G_i||^2/2=delta_i+R_i c^2>=delta_n=Delta_n.    (5)

For R_i>0 this follows from(1); for R_i=0 it follows from the duplicate
sample observation above. No strictness or limiting argument is needed.

## 3. Add the new optimum, retaining every query jet

Replace the old optimum by the new jet

  X_star_new=X_n-G_n=(x_n-g_n,0),
  G_star_new=0,
  F_star_new=Delta_n=delta_n.                               (6)

The previous optimum, if one was specified, is not retained. All query
jets(3) are retained exactly.

For every query i, direct expansion gives

  Q_(i,star_new)=Q_(i,n)_new=Q_(i,n)_original<=0,
  Q_(star_new,i)=Delta_n-Delta_i<=0.                         (7)

At i=n both quantities are zero. Equations(4),(7) check every ordered
interpolation pair in the enlarged finite data set.

By the finite interpolation construction in fact1252b8823bb5a290, these
data are interpolated by one everywhere finite convex differentiable
1-smooth function with the new optimum jet. Concretely, after translating
the new optimum to0 and its value to0, form

  H(v)=max_(j in {star,0,...,n})
       {<G_j,X_j>-F_j+<X_j,v-G_j>+||v-G_j||^2/2},
  F_new(x)=sup_v {<x,v>-H(v)}.

Here X_j,F_j in the displayed formula denote the translated data.
This is the accepted fact's explicit construction, with the whole
finite set treated as interpolation input. It interpolates every
original-index query and the zero optimum. Translation preserves all
GD differences. At the last query,

  x_n_new=G_n=(g_n,c),
  f_n_new=F_n-delta_n=||G_n||^2/2.                           (8)

The initial displacement is explicitly

  x_0_new=(x_0-x_n+g_n,Tc),

so it is nonzero since c>0.

## 4. Exact preservation of primitive energy

The energy is unchanged by a common translation of positions and a
common subtraction from all function values. It is additive under the
orthogonal direct sum(3). The Huber companion has exactly zero energy:
from(2),

  V_companion/c^2
    =2 sum_i h_i R_i+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)-s(s+1)=0,

because

  2 sum_i h_i R_i=s^2+sum_i h_i^2.

Thus V_h_new=V_h, without an approximation or an unproved inequality.

This supplies a core-ended realization of every negative witness.
The converse follows because a core-ended witness is itself legal.
For n=0 or an all-zero schedule the construction still works, and V_h=0.

## 5. What happens to the retained optimum slacks

After the normalization, the incoming optimum slack is exactly

  I_i_star_new=-Q_(i,n)_original>=0.                         (9)

Thus the construction DOES NOT remove queried-to-terminal interpolation
slack. Define d=x_0-x_n and Sigma_n=-sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n).
Expansion of the definitions gives

  V_h+2 Sigma_n=||d+g_n||^2-T^2||g_n||^2.                    (10)

Equivalently, the core realization has

  ||x_0_new||^2-T^2||g_n_new||^2
     =||d+g_n||^2-T^2||g_n||^2,

independent of c. Padding removes the terminal-adjusted-value obstruction
but leaves exactly the old query-to-terminal slack ledger. This is why
the result does not, by itself, prove tail heredity at a prepended
saturated bridge.

## 6. Exact hostile check: the old direct-reset counterexample

Take f(x)=x^2/4, h=(5), x_0=1. The original data are

  (x_0,x_1)=(1,-3/2),
  (g_0,g_1)=(1/2,-3/4),
  (f_0,f_1)=(1/4,9/16),
  (delta_0,delta_1)=(1/8,9/32),
  V_h=-25.

A direct preserved-data core reset fails because delta_1>delta_0.
Choose c=1/4. Then c^2=1/16>=(delta_1-delta_0)/5=1/32.
After padding and normalizing, the data are

  x_0_new=(7/4,3/2),  g_0_new=(1/2,1/4), f_0_new=5/16,
  x_1_new=(-3/4,1/4), g_1_new=(-3/4,1/4), f_1_new=5/16.

They satisfy x_1_new=x_0_new-5g_0_new and
x_1_new=g_1_new. Their finite Q residuals are unchanged from the legal
original pair; their optimum residuals follow(7). Direct substitution
in the energy gives

  V_h_new=5(1-5)(5/16)-30(5/8)=-25.

Thus the very example that defeats an unpadded reset is repaired
exactly by one extra equality-mode coordinate.

## 7. Saturated append heredity, with the entire proof included

Let b and c_block be any nonnegative physical blocks, with capacities
D=1+sum b_i and C=1+sum(c_block)_i. Let r>0 solve

  T_join=D+C+r, r T_join=DC.                               (11)

If the joined schedule (b,1+r,c_block) is primitive, then b is primitive.
This is a necessary prefix heredity theorem at an exactly saturated
primitive bridge. No primitive assumption about c_block is needed.

Proof by contrapositive. If b is not primitive, sections1-4 turn an
actual negative witness into a legal core-ended witness for b, with
endpoint e=x_m=g_m and value||e||^2/2. Denote its energy V_b<0.

Construct a scalar base Huber run with gradient clip(z,-r/C,1), initial
point D. During b it has gradient1 and ends at1; the bridge lands at-r;
during c_block its gradient is v_base=-r/C and it ends atv_base.
All incoming optimum slacks are zero and the final point equals its
gradient. Squared-distance telescoping therefore gives

  V_base=D^2-T_join^2(r/C)^2=0                             (12)

by(11).

For an auxiliary vector array, retain ALL supplied core-ended b samples.
The new bridge lands at-r e. During c_block use constant gradient

  v_aux=-(r/C)e.

Its starting point is C v_aux, and its terminal point is v_aux.
Assign each new value <v_aux,x>-||v_aux||^2/2. This array is not presumed
legal across the join; the following audit supplies that legality after
mixing.

For epsilon>0, combine base and auxiliary points and gradients in
orthogonal coordinates with auxiliary scale sqrt(epsilon), and combine
their values with scale epsilon. All GD equations are exact.

Every active base interpolation constraint has nonpositive auxiliary
residual:

* Old-old constraints: the auxiliary data are the original legal b run.
* New-new constraints: constant auxiliary gradient makes them zero.
* New-to-old constraints: the base residual can be zero only at an old
  target whose remaining b sum is0. That target is the old core endpoint,
  including any zero-step duplicates. At this target, direct expansion
  gives Q_aux(new,m)=0.
* Old-to-new constraints: the base residual can be zero only at a new
  target whose remaining c_block sum is0. That target has x=g=v_aux,
  so Q_aux(old,new)=Q_aux(old,star)<=0.
* Incoming optimum constraints: old ones are legal; new ones are0.
  Outgoing optimum constraints: old ones are legal; new ones are
  minus the remaining c_block sum times||v_aux||^2, hence nonpositive.

For every auxiliary pair with Q_aux>0 the corresponding base residual
is therefore strictly negative. The finite choice

  epsilon=min(1, min_(Q_aux>0) [-Q_base/(2Q_aux)])

is positive, interpreting the inner minimum as1 when there are no such
pairs. The combined data obey all interpolation constraints, including
both optimum directions and every original zero-step index. The
construction in fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes one fixed legal function.

For any core-ended legal trajectory of capacityK, distance telescoping
gives

  V=||x_0||^2-K^2||g_end||^2-2sum h_i I_i_star.              (13)

The auxiliary full trajectory is core-ended, adds no new incoming
optimum slack, and its bridge query was already a core endpoint.
Equation(11) gives T_join^2||v_aux||^2=D^2||e||^2.
Using(13) for the old b run and the full auxiliary array yields

  V_aux=V_b.

Together with(12), the fully legal combined trajectory has energy
epsilon V_b<0. This proves the stated contrapositive.

## 8. Scope and the next unmatched condition

The missing existence of SOME core-ended negative witness is settled
by this candidate construction; the old preserved-data reset criterion
remains correct but was not a universal obstruction after dimensional
padding. The saturated-append converse now covers arbitrary primitive
failures in its prefix.

This does not establish a corresponding arbitrary-tail converse for a
prepended saturated bridge. Its complete value/optimum-slack penalty
remains, and(9) identifies the retained quantity exactly. Neither the
original normalized objective ratio nor the unrestricted asymptotic
Zhang-Jiang target follows from this primitive-energy reduction.

No new statement here is in the fact graph. All claims await a selected
verification allocation; they may not be cited downstream as facts.

# Damping two: a failed potential law and a feasible high-score pair relaxation

**JOINT UNVERIFIED.** Project gd-exact-profile-20260926, worker max,
bounded invocation96. Guidance 1a0f45a58c176bdb; plan 78c88a2f77bc58c4.
No verifier submission is authorized in this allocation. This entire new
argument is one joint proof attempt, not an accepted fact.

## 1. Outcome and limits

With exactly the prescribed new damping factor 1-2/N, the original
all-pair initializer constraints are derived below. Every adjacent
two-cycle is strictly positive. The two directed triangles straddling
the top dyadic boundary are also strictly positive. This is not an
exhaustive cycle test and does not establish feasibility.

The ONE explicit relative-potential law tested is the forward-tight law
p_k=sum_(j<k) C_(j,j+1), with a specified common shift paying all zero
rows. It passes all adjacent rows and every zero row, but its original
row (N/2-1,N/2+1) fails, by a strictly positive quantified amount. This
excludes that potential law and all its common shifts, not arbitrary
initializers of the changed itinerary.

For mechanism2, retain exactly the original pair

    i=N/4-1, n=N-1.

Using its FULL inherited chronological prefix constants, both ordered
pair rows, both zero rows, and its closest-polar distance, the explicit
relaxed point in Section10 has score

    Rpair > 3/(32 S_(m-2)) > 69/(64 D_m) > 17/(16 D_m),
    D_m=1+2 sum_(k<n)h_k,                             (1.1)

uniformly for every integer m>=1536. Therefore no valid upper certificate
for this pair relaxation can be below 17/(16D_m). This statement includes
all choices of its nonnegative weak-dual multipliers, not just the
specific multiplier used for damping4. An exact expression for Rpair is
given, not merely a bound for a candidate dual objective.

The point in this relaxation is NOT proved to realize the full original
itinerary. In particular (1.1) is NOT a legal full-function lower witness.
Full feasibility with unrestricted relative potentials, and a legal
above-threshold full itinerary, remain OPEN. The original all-word
minimax question also remains OPEN. No third potential law, third vertex
set, or different damping parameter is tried.

All scores use W=2R: their numerators are twice the original terminal
objective gap. The terminal half-square is retained. For any advertised
smoothness L>0, multiplication of the constructed objective by L and use
of steps h_k/L preserves these normalized scores and physical points.

## 2. Frozen geometry and the exact original kernel

Fix one integer m>=1536 for the whole word and every child, and put

    N=2^m, n=N-1, epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_l=1+a c^l, h_k=H_(nu_2(k+1)), 0<=k<n.

Here nu_2 is the exponent of two dividing its positive integer argument.
H_l in displayed formulas denotes a single level-l step, whereas the
task's H_m denotes the complete recursively concatenated word. Define

    S_j=sum_(k<2^j-1)h_k
       =a c^j/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))2^j-1.               (2.1)

At level l<j there are 2^(j-l-1) occurrences. Summing the 1 and a c^l
parts by finite geometric sums proves (2.1); no recurrence is evaluated.

Set Kmix=N-1-m and eta=1/(N^2 Kmix). Binary labels are elements of F_2^m;
their character pairing is modulo two. For every nonzero label b set
chi_b(u)=(-1)^(b dot u). For b^(j)=N-2^j, 0<=j<m, the character amplitude
satisfies omega_(b^(j))^2=2^j/(N-1). Every other nonzero character has
omega_b^2=eta. Retain all these positive amplitudes and set

    w_k=((omega_b chi_b(Gray(k)))_(b!=0),1/sqrt(3)),
    Gray(k)=k xor floor(k/2), T=4/3+1/N^2.

Xor is binary exclusive-or; pop is binary popcount. Cancellation of
successive Gray bits proves chi_(b^(j))(Gray(k))=(-1)^bit_j(k).
The sum over all binary characters at a nonzero label is zero: pair
characters differing in a coordinate where that label is one. The sum
is N at the zero label. Subtract the constant and distinguished
characters from this identity to obtain the exact kernel

    B_kl=<w_k,w_l>
       =T-2(k xor l)/(N-1)+2eta pop(k xor l)
                                   -eta N+eta N 1_(k=l).             (2.2)

Thus B_kk=T. For an actual level-l adjacent step, write

    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta(N/2-l-1),
    B_l^adj=T-2E_l.

Let rho_l be the positive root of

    rho_l^2+(H_l-2)(B_l^adj/T)rho_l+1-H_l=0.

The ONLY changed law in this round is

    x=r_0=rho_0(1-2/N), y=r_1=rho_1(1-2/N),
    r_l=1 for l>=2,
    sigma_k=x^ceil(k/2)y^floor((k+2)/4),
    v_k=sigma_k w_k, D0=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}.        (2.3)

The level-zero positive integers at most k are the odd integers, giving
ceil(k/2) occurrences. The level-one integers are 2,6,10,..., giving
floor((k+2)/4) occurrences. This proves the formula for sigma_k
and sigma_(k+1)=sigma_k r_(nu_2(k+1)). No high root is substituted into
the law. Walsh orthogonality, with its positive constant vertical row,
makes the N frame vectors a basis of R^N. Positive radial scaling
preserves independence. Let V be the matrix with columns v_k and
G=V^T V, so G is positive definite. All tiny characters are essential
to, and retained in, this full-dimensional assertion.

## 3. Full legality, actual values, and closest-minimum interface

Define the original chronological prefixes and constants

    P_k=sum_(j<k)h_j v_j, A_kl=<P_k,v_l>,
    C_kl=A_kl-A_kk+G_kl-G_kk,
    K_k=A_kk+G_kk, c_n=A_nn+G_nn/2.                 (3.1)

Let fpot_k=<z_0,v_k> denote initializer potentials, not sampled function
values. Every real potential vector has the unique realization

    z_0=V G^(-1)fpot.                               (3.2)

For Phi(z)=max_(v in D0)(<z,v>-||v||^2/2), the exact projection normal is
<z-v,w-v><=0 for all generating vertices w, including zero. This follows
by differentiating squared distance along each segment; the converse
follows by expansion of squared distance. Completing the square gives
grad Phi=P_D0. These elementary projection-function facts, including
convexity, differentiability, smoothness one, and the full minimum set,
are accepted in fact1e0bf3f41457d8e4, Part A Section2. Only that scope is
used. In particular

    argmin Phi=Kpolar={z:V^T z<=0}, min Phi=0.

At the proposed physical points z_k=z_0-P_k the normal is EXACTLY

    fpot_l-fpot_k<=C_kl for EVERY ordered original pair k,l,
    fpot_k>=K_k for EVERY original index k.          (3.3)

These conditions are necessary and sufficient for the proposed vertices
to be the actual projections, terminal included. Then the actual GD
updates use precisely h_k, and

    Phi(z_k)=fpot_k-A_kk-G_kk/2,
    Phi(z_n)=fpot_n-c_n>=G_nn/2>0.                  (3.4)

The zero normal proves the last inequality. There is no step h_n.

Adding differences around a directed cycle in (3.3) shows that its sum
of C weights must be nonnegative. This necessary condition is independent
of all potentials and common shifts. Its converse is not used to assert
feasibility here. For a prescribed relative vector p, zero rows alone
can always be paid by the explicit common shift

    gamma(p)=1+sum_(j=0)^n |K_j-p_j|.              (3.5)

Indeed p_k+gamma(p)>K_k for every k. Basis independence in (3.2) ensures
this is an actual initializer potential vector, although the pair rows
may fail. In particular zero rows are not a hidden restriction on the
relative differences when every initializer is allowed.

For any potential vector, its exact squared distance to the complete
minimum set is

    r(fpot)=min_(d>=fpot) d^T G^(-1)d.              (3.6)
